### Direct Fallout3.exe launch and first-run config

The installed 10085 app was launched with the existing console profile
`fallout-3-gog`. Its profile already targets
`C:\\Games\\Fallout3GOG\\Fallout3.exe` directly (not
`FalloutLauncher.exe`), and the runtime log confirms that `Fallout3.exe` loaded
at `00400000`. It then attempted to create `FalloutLauncher.exe`; PS4 Wine
reports `NtCreateUserProcess process creation is not supported on PS5`, then
faults and exits. This is the game reaching its launcher fallback, not our app
selecting the launcher executable.

Direct RETR of the expected config paths showed that both
`Documents/My Games/Fallout3/Fallout.ini` and `FalloutPrefs.ini` were absent in
the active `fallout-3-gog` prefix. This matches Fallout 3's first-run path of
trying the launcher to generate settings. Created the expected directory and
uploaded the checked-in configs to that prefix, setting
`bIgnoreResolutionCheck=1` in both files, `sD3DDevice="PS5"`, and 1920x1080.
Direct RETR readback matched both local files byte-for-byte. No game files or
prefix were copied, and no package rebuild is needed for this config-only
change: the installed 10085 app already launches the direct-executable profile.

### Fallout 3 new-game crash (17:25 run)

The user selected Fallout 3's new-game path and it crashed during game
startup. This was definitely the native CPU path: the runtime logged
`cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, `wow64native initialized`, and
`Fallout3.exe ... at 005E0000: native`. The 20 FPS observation was not enough
to identify the CPU mode; the runtime log is authoritative. The current
launcher also logged `gnm submit_done rc=0`, so this was not the older
submit-done/suspend failure.

At 17:28:26 Wine recorded an unhandled read access violation in thread 0x90:
PC `0x00776434`, invalid read address `0x05b25474` (`code=c0000005`). The
module load log places Fallout3.exe at `0x005e0000`; this PC corresponds to
RVA `0x1b6434` (preferred image address `0x005b6434`, a call instruction in
the executable). About half a second later the Wine exception path faulted in
`winevulkan.so+0x3aa23`, resolving in the matching local Wine build to
`convert_VkPhysicalDeviceLimits_host_to_win32` in `vulkan_thunks.c:39907`,
then reported Vulkan surface loss as the process shut down. Treat the
Fallout3.exe invalid read as the first observed fault and the Vulkan errors
as subsequent; exact causality still needs the dump/trace decoded.

This is not the same recorded signature as Fallout New Vegas: NV crashed
before its first frame in `wgl:internal_context_create`, with a SIGSEGV on an
unmapped high address. Fallout 3 ran for about 2m45s, produced the menu, then
faulted on the new-game transition. Both were native-mode failures, but the
available traces do not prove a shared cause. Memory telemetry before the
Fallout 3 fault showed about 50 MiB flexible free and zero dmem allocation
failures, so this run does not point to memory exhaustion.

GoldHEN announced and the watcher retrieved
`~/codex_scratch/fallout3-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793060907.sorbisdmp` (214,783
bytes; SHA-256 `e21862122ae89710d4280c0f13ac751e8ab7f5c81766add9411718caea86dc0d`).
It is preserved locally; the raw `.sorbisdmp` format has not yet been decoded.
The watcher has been re-armed for `GLDS10085` on the existing klog stream.

For a controlled CPU-mode comparison, uploaded
`/data/prospero-win/profiles/fallout-3-gog-translator.profile`. It keeps the
same executable, working directory, prefix, DXVK overrides, 1080p display, and
XInput mode as `fallout-3-gog`; only its id/name and `[runtime] cpu =

No package rebuild or reinstall is needed to test this profile. If the

### 2026-10-27 Fallout 3 loading crash and page-at-a-time A/B

Correction: the failed 2026-10-27 launch selected `fallout-3-gog-translator`
(`cpu=translator`), not the native profile. The app log reached Wine startup;
the PS4 kernel then reported a fatal `SIGILL` on thread `orbis` in
`libkernel.sprx`, with reason `page fault (user read data, page not present)`.
Fault address was `0x000000100010b5e8`, RIP `0x00000008000009fe`, and RSP
equaled the fault address. The kernel backtrace was unavailable. The
`PW_EXCEPTION_SETUP` and `PW_EXCEPTION_REGS` Wine logs did not fire, so this is
below that exception path.

Immediately before the fault, klog recorded `exec-range build=0974` and
successful range migrations for executable mappings, including a 2.31 MiB
span at `0x1128000`. The signature exactly matches the earlier 10076
executable-range failure; the 10078 page-at-a-time build later reached
Oblivion gameplay. This makes range replacement the leading A/B variable, not
a proven root cause.

Built a PS4-only page-at-a-time A/B from the current 10085 Fallout 3 stage.
It retains the 0977 first-exception instrumentation and the per-page
direct-memory-to-flex migration, but removes whole-range backing replacement.
Caller-backed spans use one verified host-protection retry per contiguous
mapping. Successful per-page migrations are quiet to match the known-good
10078 behavior. The runtime was linked by `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` using
`build/ps4-wine-sdk/bin/prospero-clang` and the Orbis SDK; only the two
`ntdll.prx` payload copies differ from the base stage.

- Installer: `IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`

- Size: 444,989,440 bytes; uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`;

**10086 hardware result:** the console launcher selected the translator profile
because the newly added card shifted the list. The user intended the original
Fallout 3 profile. During this unintended translator run the user saw the
loading screen transition to black; the earlier build had crashed before
reaching black. The user closed 10086 from the PS4 menu at 07:15:06; the log
shows process teardown, not a fatal signal. At that point Wine was alive with
audio advancing, but `frames_put=4`, `vk_shown=0`, and no visible frame updates.
Klog also showed repeated exact-page migration failures (`mmap` `EINVAL`) and
an excessive stream of host-protection verification records. Do not count this
as a native-mode result or repeat 10086.

The host verification added in 10086 was a mistake: Orbis reported
`sceKernelMprotect` success while `sceKernelVirtualQuery` still reported
`0x3`, and treating that report as a failed host retry both returned an error
to Wine and flooded klog. Build 10087 removes that verification and emits only
host-protection failures. It retains the page-at-a-time direct-memory path and
the exception-register diagnostics.

### Native Fallout 3 new-game transition crash (2026-10-27)

The user confirmed the sequence: launcher, loading screen, select New, loading
screen, then crash while entering the game. This run was confirmed native by
`cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, successful `wow64native initialized`, and native
loading of `Fallout3.exe`; the translator profile had been hidden to prevent
another accidental selection. This is distinct from the earlier translator
run that reached black and was closed normally.

The coredump watcher captured
`~/codex_scratch/fallout3-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793111255.sorbisdmp` (398,633
bytes; SHA-256 `4ff8ca2edd9859c729fd5599bf4df646b21e17952feb286d104c5b8a8c629eb2`).
The raw SCE dump format is not decoded locally. The Wine trace is actionable:

- First logged game-thread exception at 07:27:34.802: `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`
  (`c0000005`), read at guest RIP `0x00a7641e`, address `0x067a0ec4`, in
  thread `0x24`.
- About 0.5 seconds later Wine logged another access violation at RIP zero,
  followed by repeated faults at `ntdll.prx+0xf3f4e`, address `0xfffffb44`,
  while handling the exception.
- Native libkernel SIGSYS was passed through; then the process faulted with
  SIGILL at `ntdll.prx+0x9c8b9`. The captured bytes are `0f 0b` (`ud2`), and
  symbolizing the matching `ntdll.shared.elf` maps this to `abort_process+0x19`,
  immediately after `_exit@plt`. Thus the final SIGILL is the fatal termination
  fallback after `_exit` returned; it is downstream of the earlier game fault
  and exception-handler faults. Do not treat it as a graphics or memory fault.

The next investigation should resolve the initial guest fault and the exception
dispatch failures separately from the process-exit SIGILL. No new package has
been built for this signature yet. Keep the existing listeners and coredump
watcher running.

### Native Fallout 3 fault-page mapping build (2026-10-27)

Mapped the first faulting instruction to Fallout3.exe, not Wine or Vulkan.
The image was loaded at `0x005e0000`; the fault RIP `0x00a7641e` is RVA
`0x0049641e` (preferred VA `0x0089641e`). It executes
`mov ebp,[ebx+esi]`. Registers match the bad read exactly:
`ebx=0x067a0718`, index `eax=0x1eb` is scaled to `esi=0x7ac`, and
`ebx+esi=0x067a0ec4`, the reported fault address. This confirms Fallout's
code attempted an indexed table read; it does not yet distinguish a corrupt
table pointer from a PS4 mapping/protection error. Memory telemetry showed
211,968 KiB flexible free, 68,560 KiB direct memory, and zero allocation
failures, so memory exhaustion is not supported by this run.

Built a targeted mapping trace, with no memory behavior changes. Patch
`prospero-win/wine/patches/0999-ntdll-trace-ps4-guest-fault-mapping.patch`
logs the PS4 `sceKernelVirtualQuery` result for the fault address and table
base before and after Wine's signal hook, plus the native register context and
hook result. It only traces data faults in the observed `0x06000000`–
`0x08000000` heap window, and never reads or repairs the faulting page. The
patch applies after 0998. The matching NTDLL was rebuilt and PRX-linked; the
package was assembled from the known 10087 pagewise stage with only its two
`ntdll.prx` copies replaced.

- Package: `pkgout/fallout3-native-10085-guest-fault-map/`
- Installer: `IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
- Package SHA-256: `fe41c51d7f24d70ce187fc5b995f5853b15037dbf6347a9c186ae4fce24fb505`
- Size: 444,989,440 bytes; FTP STOR returned 226 after sending all bytes to
  `/data/pkg/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`.

A dedicated `GLDS10085` coredump watcher is active on the existing continuous
klog log, alongside the existing GTA watcher; neither klog nor app-log
listeners were restarted. Install this package and launch the native Fallout
3 card. At the same new-game transition, the `ntdll ps4-guest-fault-map` and
`ntdll ps4-guest-fault-hook` records will tell us whether the kernel page was
unmapped or protected, whether the table base was valid, and whether Wine's
fault hook changed the mapping. This is an instrumented diagnostic build,
not yet a fix for the crash.

### Native Fallout 3 resolved-page retry build (2026-10-27)

The next native New-game run crashed at the same transition. Its fault-map
trace recorded a write page fault (`err=6`, not-present + user write) at
`0x0652a184`, RIP `0x00a2635b`. The faulting page was
`0x06528000-0x0652c000`; the `segv_hook` returned zero and a subsequent
`sceKernelVirtualQuery` reported that same page as readable/writable
(`prot=3`). Disassembly identifies the guest instruction as `mov word ptr
[eax], 0`, with the effective address matching the reported fault address.
This is evidence for a transient mapping/remap race; it is not proof of its
cause.

Patch `prospero-win/wine/patches/1000-ntdll-retry-resolved-ps4-guest-fault.patch`
adds a guarded retry before Windows exception dispatch. It retries only a
user-mode, not-present fault in 32-bit guest address space when a fresh kernel
query says the entire fault address is currently mapped with the access the
instruction needs. It does not change mapping permissions or touch the page.
It logs at most 16 successful retries. NTDLL rebuilt and PRX-linked; package
assembled from the tested 10087 pagewise stage, replacing only the two NTDLL
PRX copies.

- Package directory: `pkgout/fallout3-native-10085-resolved-page-retry/`
- Installer filename: `IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
- SHA-256: `ca1a4384a5d22b5a1e94a1e905a7d09c5e5cf32d1831c38dac42a21e93d91d79`
- Size: 444,989,440 bytes. FTP upload to
  `/data/pkg/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg` completed with server
  response 226.

A new `GLDS10085` core-dump watcher is active on the existing PS4 klog stream.
For the next run, install this package and launch the native Fallout 3 profile,
then select New once. Check for `ntdll ps4-retry-resolved-fault` first: if it
appears, the retry path ran; if it crashes without that record, the dump and
fault trace should tell us whether the mapping changed or a different fault
occurred. The change is a targeted hypothesis test, not a confirmed fix yet.

### Resolved-page retry run: no-result startup stall (2026-10-27)

On the 10089 resolved-page-retry package, native Fallout3.exe loaded and reset
its D3D9 swapchain to 1920x1080. It submitted only three 320x240 startup
frames, then remained alive without further frames for over two minutes. The
heartbeat continued but CPU time increased only about 450 ms over 90 seconds;
major/minor faults and disk block I/O were flat, with roughly 205 MiB flexible
memory free and no allocation failures. No `ps4-retry-resolved-fault` record
or new guest fault occurred, so the retry code was not exercised and cannot
explain this delay. At 08:10:03 the PS4 focus returned to the shell and the app
was suspended from the PS menu; there is no new core dump. This run did not
reach or reproduce the New-game fault and is inconclusive for patch 1000.

### Native Fallout 3 New-game crash and fault-instruction trace build (2026-10-27)

The next native run reached New and crashed. The coredump watcher retrieved
`~/codex_scratch/fallout3-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793114176.sorbisdmp` (342,689
bytes; SHA-256
`33b698aace3050f30a2eb8176d934123e1c33bd8a7a13ac060a109e886ebc398`). The
first logged fault is again a `0xc0000005` in `winevulkan.so+0x3aa23`, at
`0x280f1ff51f`; the recorded registers include `RDI=0x803066438`. The
resolved-page retry did not run because this is a high host address, outside
the 32-bit guest range. Repeated `SIGILL` at `ntdll.prx+0x9ce89` follows during
exception handling and is downstream.

The available WineVulkan symbols do not currently explain this record
consistently: the instruction at the corresponding offset in the local
WineVulkan ELF appears to access `[rdi+8]`, which would be `0x803066440`, not
the recorded fault address. This could be a binary/symbol mismatch or a bad
fault-context report; do not infer a Vulkan root cause yet.

Built a diagnostic-only NTDLL change that logs x86-64 error bits, exception
parameters, and eight bytes at the faulting RIP alongside the existing
register log. It does not change exception handling or memory behavior. The

matches `0x280f1ff51f` and the kernel reports a write fault. This rules out the
previous address/register mismatch.

(`0x2d4f0` through `0x413f8`; `.text` starts at `0x41400`), but that local PRX
does not match the installed one. The exact installed PRX's corresponding
bytes are unusual as instructions, but its symbol/section map is not available
yet. Treat the unwind-data interpretation as a clue, not an exact attribution.

### Native Fallout 3 New-game crash with stack words (2026-10-27)

write address. WineVulkan's loaded text range is `0x802fa0000–0x803088000`,
so the fault RIP is inside the module as reported.

The bounded stack snapshot now works. At fault time RSP/RBP were within the
active Wine stack (`0x1003420000–0x100351fa00`). Saved words include return
addresses `0x8008ca81e` and `0x8008c8e3a`, both inside the loaded NTDLL text
range (`0x80084c000–0x800930000`). Using the matching `ntdll.shared.elf` from
the same 08:48 target PRX link (its NTDLL PRX hash matches the package),
`addr2line` maps these offsets to `read_reply_data`
(`ntdll/unix/server.c:273`) and `process_exit_wrapper`
(`ntdll/unix/server.c:2320`). The RBP chain also contains `0x80001417bc` in
`libSceLibcInternal`. This is a useful symbolic caller chain into Wine's
server-reply/process-exit path, although the frame layout is nonstandard and
does not prove which function directly called the faulting code. The
fault-time RDI points into WineVulkan's executable range rather than a normal
application heap region, consistent with a bad pointer or corrupted call
state; the stack alone does not identify where that state was introduced.

The available WineVulkan target ELF is newer than the WineVulkan PRX retained
in the tested 10087 base stage, so its `.eh_frame` layout and symbols cannot
be used to attribute `winevulkan.so+0x3aa23`. Earlier conclusions based on that
newer module's section offset remain unverified for this package.

Native profile instrumentation was enabled for this run (`pc_sampler=1`,
`profile=1`). It emitted per-thread `PW_NATIVE_PROFILE` counters but no

### Native Fallout 3 primary guest fault identified (2026-10-27)

The next crash after selecting New produced a 32-bit guest read fault before
the later WineVulkan teardown fault. Klog recorded:

```text
ntdll ps4-sig-fault-frame n=1 sig=11 code=1 addr=5c2671c rip=776452 rsp=1896f780 cs=33 ss=3b flags=10283 trap=12 err=4
```

`err=4` is a user-mode read from a not-present page. The Wine exception log
agrees: `c0000005`, read access at `0x05c2671c`, EIP `0x00776452`, and
`EDI=0x05c26718`. The exact Fallout3.exe used in the local scratch copy, GOG
installer, and console FTP copy has SHA-256
`df9f0e1ec2c5413a9cef2db2dde6d926b99707d528049af4eb15538587d55f99`.
Runtime loaded it at `0x005e0000`; EIP therefore corresponds to RVA
`0x196452` (preferred VA `0x00596452`). The matching instruction is:

```asm
00596452: d8 47 04    fadd dword ptr [edi+0x4]
```

With the logged EDI, this reads exactly `0x05c2671c`, matching the faulting
address. This establishes that the first failure is a real game-side invalid
pointer read while entering the New-game path. Wine then attempts to launch
its debugger, which fails; shutdown later produces the recurring
`winevulkan.so+0x3aa23` syscall fault and `VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR`. Those
are downstream teardown events, not the original crash trigger.

The existing 0999 guest-fault mapping diagnostic only queried addresses from
`0x06000000` upward, so it missed this pointer at `0x05c2671c`. Patch
`1003-ntdll-map-low-guest-fault.patch` expands that diagnostic to guest
addresses `0x00400000–0x08000000` and queries both the fault address and RDI
with `sceKernelVirtualQuery`. It changes logging only; it does not alter
mapping or exception handling. The rebuilt NTDLL PRX hash is
`407b953a8ab84c0c8819085c6b4bff201e23a32f3953b0b16a6b93796d349678`.

The diagnostic package is
`pkgout/fallout3-native-10085-low-guest-fault-map/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
(SHA-256 `9512c921881e49e0cbb0e16319c5ea6ec2b38dad669d1e6a4559a01be859edc1`).
It was uploaded to
`/data/pkg/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`. Install that exact
filename for the next run. Keep the native Fallout profile and existing
listeners; the armed core watcher should capture the next failure. The key
next datum is whether the object pointer at `0x05c26718` is mapped, and with
what protection, immediately before/at the game fault.

The first launch of this diagnostic build stayed on a black screen and was

### Native Fallout 3 loading crash is an invalid memset destination (2026-10-27)

After installing 1003 and selecting New, Fallout 3 crashed at the loading
screen. The core watcher captured
`~/codex_scratch/fallout3-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793120815.sorbisdmp` (315,234
bytes; SHA-256
`598db656d53ca914f389a37f958c887fdeb4e17119ecbc89d0a4e7718b1f5193`).
Klog and Wine first-exception logs show a write fault at address `0x8100`,
EIP `0x7b9ee49b`, with `EBX=0x8100`, `EAX=0`, and `ECX=0x000aaab8`. The fault-map
line from 1003 did not fire because its lower bound was `0x00400000`.

The matching package's i386 `ucrtbase.dll` is loaded at `0x7b990000`.
Therefore the fault EIP is RVA `0x5e49b`. Its `memset` export begins at RVA
`0x5e460`; the faulting instruction at `+0x3b` is `movups XMMWORD PTR [ebx],xmm0`.
That matches the fault address exactly: the game/runtime called `memset` with
destination `0x8100` and a large fill count. This is a low invalid destination
pointer, not the later WineVulkan teardown fault.

Just 36 ms before the exception, Wine logged a failed writable section map:
view `0x20a00000`, size `0x100000`, offset `0x1200000`, followed by
`Mapping non-persisted file failed: 87` and `unaligned shared mapping`. The
failure and bad `memset` are temporally adjacent, but causality is not yet
established. In `ntdll/unix/virtual.c`, the writable shared-map path returns
`STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` after an `EINVAL` mmap result (or if host alignment
prevents the mmap attempt); the previous logs did not distinguish those
cases or record the required alignment/protection values.

Patch
`prospero-win/wine/patches/1004-ntdll-trace-null-fault-and-shared-map.patch`
adds logging only: it captures fault addresses below 4 MiB, queries both RBX
and RDI, and reports the writable mapping geometry, flags, and mmap errno.
The NTDLL PRX hash is
`b26c436beb32ca9494d7be7cd9a4d91c348495d69ea58075f3381c7fce567af0`.
The package
`pkgout/fallout3-native-10085-null-map-failure/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
has SHA-256
`9d74354e900d9447ef75ca349401b18f943f26a2db17bf369bf38c622f876b00` and was
uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`. Install that
exact filename for the next run. A fresh `GLDS10085` core watcher is armed on
the existing klog stream. The next useful evidence is the `PS4_MAP_FILE_SHARED_FAIL`
line and the queried mapping for `0x8100`/RBX.

`RBX+0xc` equals the fault address `0x1e070068`. This lies above the 1004

### Native Fallout 3 crash during the New-game load (package 1005, 2026-10-27)

Package 1005 got past the prior pre-loading crash, displayed the loading screen,
then crashed while entering the game. The watcher captured
`~/codex_scratch/fallout3-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793122005.sorbisdmp` (346,198
bytes; SHA-256
`d0096cbb8176ca36a1d33ddd924cb805fe11c32a77b88030cd355b3ba7ee379c`). The
first two 32-bit guest write faults during loading were resolved by the
existing fault hook (`ps4-retry-resolved-fault`), so those are not the fatal
fault.

The termination path is now visible in the runtime log. A newly created
native WoW64 thread (guest TEB `0x7ff6e000`) failed its signal-TCB allocation:
`mmap` returned `MAP_FAILED` with `errno=22`; `thread_init` rejected it, and
`pw_native_prepare_simulate` then terminated the process with
`STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` (`c000000d`) because `thread_get` had no initialized
state. The later `winevulkan.so+0x3aa23` SIGSEGV and `ntdll.prx+0x9d499`
privileged-opcode/SIGILL are teardown failures after that termination request.
The reason the late 16-KiB `mmap` fails is not established yet; the log shows
many successful per-thread TCB maps earlier in the same run.

### Native Fallout 3 signal-TCB pool package 1006

Patch `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0071-wow64native-reserve-signal-tcb-pool.patch`
reserves a 128-slot, 2-MiB low-address pool during native process initialization.
Threads allocate/release 16-KiB TCB pages from this pool, avoiding a new low
address mapping on every thread start. If pool reservation fails, the existing
per-thread mapping remains as a fallback; if an individual fallback map fails,
it retries with a null hint and logs both results. This is a targeted fix for
the observed late TCB allocation failure, not yet hardware-validated.

The new `wow64native.prx` SHA-256 is
`e486fc5d126baa4d87fb3921dadf70b437952279ca66d3c1e7d8449ebaf5a506`; both
packaged copies match. Package
`pkgout/fallout3-native-10085-signal-tcb-pool-1006/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
has SHA-256
`ee4d3561a01ffdf2c5d15d8d6d4ba761eb0c173b72479b63680ce0d4d1e4325b`, size
444,989,440 bytes, and was uploaded to
`/data/pkg/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`. A fresh core watcher is
armed on the persistent klog listener. Install this exact filename for the
next run. The first useful check is whether startup logs `signal-tcb-pool
ready` and whether the New-game load passes native thread initialization.

### Native Fallout 3 guest fault after the TCB-pool fix (1006, 2026-10-27)

The 1006 run initialized the 128-slot signal-TCB pool, allocated threads from
it, and reached the New-game transition without any pool exhaustion or
`prepare_simulate` termination. This confirms that 1006 passed the earlier
native thread-start failure. It then hit a different primary fault in
Fallout3.exe; the later WineVulkan surface-loss/teardown fault is secondary.

At 10:46:37, the first exception was a 32-bit guest read AV:

```
PW_EXCEPTION_SETUP code=c0000005 addr=2aebb4a4 ctxrip=a0bf4e ctxrsp=18d6f874
PW_EXCEPTION_REGS rax=04333938 rcx=1a1ecfc4 ... cs=0033 ss=003b
```

The faulting read is `mov esi,[ecx+4*eax]` at Fallout3.exe preferred VA
`0x0082bf4e` (RVA `0x42bf4e`, image loaded at `0x005e0000`). The effective
address matches exactly: `0x1a1ecfc4 + 4 * 0x04333938 = 0x2aebb4a4`.
This proves the first exception is Fallout 3 guest code using an implausibly
large table index; it does not yet establish why that index is invalid.

The watcher preserved
`~/codex_scratch/fallout3-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793123197.sorbisdmp` (344,859
bytes; SHA-256
`615175d26d45d6325578977b3ce3d1157bc49e2890525d2abe3032b5c8332d64`). The
host cannot decode this FACECS dump, so patch 0978 adds a bounded snapshot of
up to 16 DWORDs at guest ESP, guarded by the 32-bit CS and TEB32 stack bounds.
It changes exception logging only and preserves all 1006 runtime and game
payload behavior.

### Fallout NV package A/B: Oct 15 baseline vs refreshed runtime (2026-10-27)

The user clarified the A/B: GLDS10080 (`pkgout/oct15-stickkey-10080`) is the
package that launches Fallout NV; GLDS10085 is the regression package. Fallout
3 and Fallout NV share the Bethesda Gamebryo lineage, so a shared runtime
regression is relevant to both, but the Fallout 3 attempt below is not itself a
valid cross-title reproduction.

A direct staged-payload comparison shows this is a broad runtime change, not
just the Oblivion stick mapping. Of 1,789 files common to GLDS10080 and current
GLDS10085 package 1007, 1,634 differ: 1,615 under `win/` (including nearly all
Wine PE files), 15 PS4 PRXs, plus `eboot.bin`, `pkg.gp4`, and `param.sfo`. The
10085 stage also contains four extra build-log sidecars. Notable sizes:

| Payload | GLDS10080 | GLDS10085 package 1007 |
|---|---:|---:|
| `eboot.bin` | 247,584 | 732,496 |
| `wow64native.dll` | 40,960 | 134,583 |
| `wow64native.prx` | 264,448 | 281,872 |
| `ntdll.prx` | 1,011,152 | 1,044,080 |

The current Prospero-Win checkout is commit `73675f7` (the WoW64 native CPU
backend change, native by default unless a profile selects the translator or
OpenGL). GLDS10085's Fallout NV log confirms `cpu=native`; GLDS10080's older
`wow64native` payload lacks the current PS4 diagnostics/backend additions.
This makes the refreshed WoW64 CPU/signal path a leading candidate, but the
package diff is too broad to call it the proven cause. The 10085 NV trace still
needs its initiating fault decoded; downstream SIGSYS/`ud2` alone is not the
answer.

The follow-up Fallout 3 attempt on GLDS10080 selected `fallout3-gog` in
translator mode, then Wine logged `load_64bit_module failed (c0000135)` and
returned to the launcher. That is a profile/runtime load failure before
Fallout 3 gameplay, not evidence about the shared Gamebryo render failure. Its
later PS4 `CPU_FAULT_SUBMITDONE_TIMEOUT_IN_SUSPEND_ASYNC` happened during app
cleanup. The dump announced as
`/user/data/sce_coredumps/GLDS10080_1793124789/GLDS10080_1793124789.sorbisdmp`
was already unavailable when RETR was attempted.

Best isolating A/B: keep the GLDS10085 files, Fallout NV prefix, and all game
settings fixed, then run the same NV profile once with `cpu=translator`. The
separate profile `Fallout: New Vegas (Translator A-B)` is now uploaded at
`/data/prospero-win/profiles/fallout-new-vegas-translator-ab.profile`; it
duplicates the retrieved control profile byte-for-byte except for its ID/name
and `[runtime] cpu = translator`. FTP RETR readback matches (SHA-256

Package 1009 was installed/launched as GLDS10085 with the `Fallout: New Vegas

nearly flattened, while the wrapper heartbeat continued. Memory telemetry was
stable (`dmem_failures=0`, flexible free about 198 MiB), so this does not look
like the previous NTDLL fault or an allocation failure. The user closed it from

a normal process kill with kill reason `0`; no crash dump was announced. The
package's `gnm submit_done rc=0` was present, so this is not the known close
fault.

not yet make NV stable: replacing only those PRXs removed the observed
`SIGSYS`/`ud2` crash loop and exposed a gameplay hang. FPS was subjectively
lower. Treat the distinction as provisional because the second run ended by
user close and produced no dump. Keep the one-shot GLDS10085 dump watcher armed
for the next run. The next code investigation should compare the exact new and
10080 NTDLL builds at syscall #37 / the faulting offset and identify why the
new build reaches `ud2`; do not make another broad runtime/package change.

### Fallout NV native crash and repeated guest null-vtable fault (2026-10-27)

The next run used package 1008 and the regular Fallout NV profile. The log
confirms `cpu=native`, loads `wow64native.dll`, and reports successful native
initialization (`cs32=0033`, `cs64=0043`, `avx=1`). The process then crashed
after about 103 seconds of game runtime. This is a native-mode result; do not
repeat it with the translator as the next step.

The guest exception trace at `13:10:51.714` records an access violation on
read at runtime EIP `0x00c3ac0f`, address `0x8c`, with `EDX=0`, `ESI=0x3207d000`,
and `EAX=0x036d2f0c`. The exact console FalloutNV.exe was fetched to
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/FalloutNV-console-active-20261027.exe`
(SHA-256
`a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`). It was
loaded at runtime base `0x005e0000`; subtracting that base maps the fault to
RVA `0x65ac0f` / preferred address `0x00a5ac0f`. Disassembly shows the code
successfully reads a non-null object at `[ESI]` into EDX, then faults on
`mov eax,[edx+0x8c]`: the object's first word (its vtable pointer) is null.
The next instruction would call that vtable slot. This is a game-side null
object/vtable state at the observed instruction, not a failure to initialize
the native WoW64 backend.

This has the same fault shape as the earlier package-1008 translator run at
`12:28:49.587`: that run faulted at runtime EIP `0x00c60862`, address `0x8c`,
with `EAX=0`, on `mov edx,[eax+0x8c]`. Both addresses map into the same exact
FalloutNV.exe image at different helper sites. The earlier translator run's
dump is `GLDS10085_1793129334.sorbisdmp`; this native run's dump is
`GLDS10085_1793131851.sorbisdmp` (367,680 bytes, SHA-256
`c6420062de4f58d666af41ee22cb1bb2302570c4f050034c456a3b3785b0b08f`). The
active executable copy and both dumps are preserved under
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/`. The console log confirms the dump
completed successfully at 13:10:56.208. The exception/register trace and exact
PE instruction have been analyzed; the raw `.sorbisdmp` container itself still
needs a compatible parser if we want its full process memory and thread state.

Memory pressure is a correlation worth following, not yet a proven cause. In
the translator run, flexible free memory fell to about 12 MiB shortly before
the fault. In this native run, it fell from 64.5 MiB at `13:10:31` to 11 MiB at
`13:10:49`; the fault followed at `13:10:51`. Both runs had
`dmem_failures=0`, which only rules out failures counted by that specific
direct-memory allocator counter. At the low-memory snapshot, the largest
flexible holder was 125.7 MiB across 7,858 mappings named
`anon:0008009023db`, followed by 69.6 MiB named `orbis`; other large anonymous
holders were also present. Do not label this an OOM yet. There is no evidence
that the crash was caused by the native CPU mode, and the translator comparison
already reproduced the same guest fault pattern.

At the native-run fault, the wrapper counters read `mmap=4006`,
`munmap=2`, `mprotect=0`, `skipped=579`, `faults=0`; the latest direct-memory
counter still showed `dmem_failures=0`. These counters do not measure failed
32-bit guest heap allocations, so they are not proof that every game allocation
succeeded. The log also has a one-time `wgl:internal_context_create Failed to
create internal thread context` at 13:09:27 while flexible memory was still
about 259 MiB; there is not enough evidence to connect that separate message
to the later FalloutNV null-vtable crash.

Next investigation: keep native as the default gameplay path. Use the exact
1008 FalloutNV.exe and its captured fault to inspect the object's allocation
and initialization path, and add targeted logging for failed guest heap/object
allocations and the relevant list/vtable state near the fault. Also resolve
the large anonymous holder's allocator call site against the exact 1008 app
symbols before attributing it to file access or any other subsystem. Preserve
the baseline relocation, input, filesystem, and close-path fixes. The next
hardware run should be a native run only if the diagnostic makes a specific
prediction; do not spend another launch on an unchanged package or repeat the
translator A/B.

### Fallout NV package-1008 native Continue crash captured (2026-10-27)

The relaunch used the same `Fallout 3 Guest EIP Map 1008` package and regular

The watcher captured
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793135286.sorbisdmp` (373,134
bytes, SHA-256
`09c672d61921fb00ed863f4e1f694f83c94f8ce1fe2c4630966333a265ec4d58`). Its
matching klog window is
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793135286-klog-window.txt`
(SHA-256
`91b0970d6354b2bd11d4a45ca5328a4577686bcbdc2e90e6db72c998da7d2759`). The
exact package NTDLL was copied to
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/ntdll-GLDS10085-package1008.prx` (SHA-256
`39bf1b7e371e00a1630897f574116c158f0b402ca6dd6317812eb7afd5b55c34`). The
package SHA-256 is
`403c49585aee628732483709e8908d161537e3645ee88363a0ba160580718d8e`; the
selected CPU mode is native.

At 14:08:06.606, the process entered a repeated native Prospero SIGSYS followed
by Wine `0xc000001d` at `ntdll.prx+0x9d739`. The logged bytes at the fault are
`0f 0b` (`UD2`); the recorded Wine syscall index is `0x25` / #37, the return IP
is `0x6ffffff3f0f4`, and SIGSYS has `RAX=0xffffffffa0381699`, signal code
65542. Klog classifies the application crash as reason/exception code `0x4`.
This exactly reproduces package 1008's earlier Fallout NV translator failure
at `ntdll.prx+0x9d739` with the same syscall #37, RAX, and UD2 bytes (see the
package-1009 A/B section above). Since that earlier run used `cpu=translator`,
the native WoW64 backend is not required for this fault. It is distinct from
the earlier guest null-vtable access violation. The last memory sample before
the fault showed `flexible_free` around 11 MiB, with `dmem_failures=0`; low
headroom is correlated but not proven causal.

The actual first exception is the guest FalloutNV fault at `14:08:05.600`,
before the WineVulkan and NTDLL exceptions below. Patch 0978 logged an access
violation on read at runtime EIP `0x00c5fdde`, address `0x14`, with guest
`EDX=0x10`, `EAX=0x2f0c4068`, and return address `0x00c607d7`. The exact
console FalloutNV.exe used here is
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/FalloutNV-console-active-20261027.exe`,
SHA-256 `a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`.
The loader reported base `0x005e0000`; translating the runtime EIP through
that base gives RVA `0x67fdde` and preferred PE address `0x00a7fdde`.
Disassembly at that exact address is `fmul DWORD PTR [edx+0x4]`, which reads
`0x14` because EDX is `0x10`.

The caller at preferred address `0x00a807d2` computes the second vector
pointer as `[esi+0xb8] + 0x10` and passes it to the helper. Thus the first
fault is a guest-side invalid object/vector state: that field was zero when
the helper ran. This identifies the immediate bad pointer, but does not yet
establish why the field was zero (game state, memory corruption, or a runtime
interaction remain to be distinguished). The saved guest stack's prior return
address is runtime `0x00c39fbf` / preferred `0x00a59fbf`, the return from a
virtual method call. Preserve this trace when investigating object
construction/initialization; do not attribute the initiating fault to
WineVulkan based on later exceptions.

At `14:08:06.573`, after the guest fault and failed debugger startup, thread
`0090` then took a host-side access violation at `winevulkan.so+0x3aa23`,
address `0x280f1ff51f` (`0xc0000005`, error 6/write). The faulting bytes are
`00 6c bf 07 00 ee 89 00`; with `RDI=0x803066438`, the first instruction
computes `RDI + RDI*4 + 7`, exactly `0x280f1ff51f`. The loaded package
WineVulkan text range is `0x802fa0000..0x803088000`, so the PC is
module-relative `0x3aa23`. Treat this as a subsequent host exception, not the
initiating gameplay fault and not proof of OOM. The following `ntdll #44` and
SIGSYS/UD2 sequence occurs during failure/exit handling and is later still.

The exact package WineVulkan PRX is preserved in package 1008's stage and has
SHA-256 `5db25a6b6c7d5abb8d55e4411f7f9c68715d73d550d8556d219ef8c771409cb3`.
The local `winevulkan.shared.elf` is not a matching symbol file: its paired
PRX hash is `1d3ecaefe91cca602f3036e23994cfda0ca5ad9c5dddbf8f88859630a9d3bca0`,
and symbolizing the package offset directly against it lands in a GCC
exception table. Do not treat that as a source attribution. The retained
`winevulkan-prx-code.bin` confirms the fault bytes at code-image offset
`0x3aa23`, but has no symbol table. Locate/rebuild the exact package-1008
WineVulkan `.shared.elf` before making a source-level claim.

The secondary NTDLL fault *is* symbolized against the exact matching ELF:
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/prx/sce_module/ntdll.prx` has the same SHA-256 as
the package NTDLL (`39bf1b7e371e00a1630897f574116c158f0b402ca6dd6317812eb7afd5b55c34`),
and the paired unstripped `ntdll.shared.elf` is
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/prx/ntdll.shared.elf` (SHA-256
`12c3fc1eb8bc56ad4fd0103ff050d951c9985bc9fe7fb881ad0fec7b51b1349a`).
`llvm-addr2line -f -C -e <that ELF> 0x9d739` resolves to
`abort_process`, `dlls/ntdll/unix/thread.c:1645`; the exact disassembly is a
call to `_exit()` followed by `UD2`, because `_exit()` is declared noreturn.
This means the loop is reached while Wine is trying to terminate after the
earlier guest FalloutNV read fault and subsequent WineVulkan write fault. Keep
the NTDLL A/B history as secondary context: package 1009's October 15 NTDLL removed this particular

matching saved klog window is
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/GLDS10085_1793139695-klog-window.txt`
(SHA-256 `da46d7a2139b83ee8b1c291001a3594fd8dfb293be1e46a0c4ef52f7dff1a5f7`).
This is title ID `GLDS10085`, package 1011, native CPU mode. The exact active
FalloutNV.exe copy is retained at
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/FalloutNV-console-active-20261027.exe`
(SHA-256 `a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`).

The first useful fault record is a 32-bit game-side read fault at runtime EIP
`0x00c3dca8`, fault address `0x0f46e26c` (`CS=0x33`, SIGSEGV read, page not
present). With the executable loaded at `0x005e0000`, this maps to preferred
FalloutNV.exe address `0x00a5dca8`. Exact PE disassembly is `mov ecx,[ecx+4*esi]`
in a loop which indexes a pointer array at object offset `0xa0` and compares
against a count at offset `0xa6`. The guest general registers were not present
in this klog record, so the exact bad base/index and object identity are not
known. This is a distinct initiating guest fault from the earlier package-1008
null-vector read at `0x00a7fdde`; do not conflate them.

There is a useful memory-lifetime clue immediately before it: at 15:21:35.999,
the fault hook resolved a read at `0x0f46d42c`, mapping
`0x0f46c000-0x0f470000` with read/write protection. About 2 ms later the
unhandled fault address `0x0f46e26c` falls inside that same 16 KiB range. This
could mean the region changed lifetime/protection between the two accesses, or
that the signal record reflects a different context; registers needed to tell
which are missing. The `dmem-before=16384` values later in the trace are the
direct-memory byte count for each page, not 16 MiB of free memory. There is no
free-flexible-memory sample close enough to this fault to establish OOM.

The later host access violation repeats the known
`winevulkan.so+0x3aa23` write fault at `0x280f1ff51f` with `RDI=0x803066438`
and bytes `00 6c bf 07 00 ee 89 00`, after Wine begins terminating the guest
process. Treat that as downstream until the earlier FalloutNV.exe fault is
explained. The raw dump is retained for the local dump/debugger workflow; do
not infer decoded memory contents from its FACECS header or printable strings.

For a controlled A/B of the only game-side setting changed in this run, the
remote file
`/data/prospero-win/prefixes/fallout-new-vegas/drive_c/Games/FalloutNV/dxvk.conf`
was renamed to `dxvk.conf.budget-256-ab` by FTP after this crash. The installed
package is unchanged; the next run can use the same native profile and save
without reinstalling, with DXVK's default D3D9 memory report. Restore the

armed on the existing klog stream. If the fault repeats, the next diagnostic
should log the raw x86 registers at the first SIGSEGV frame (especially ECX and
ESI) and capture map/unmap/protection changes to the `0x0f46c000` allocation.
Do not infer that the 256 MiB DXVK hint caused the fault until the no-config
A/B is compared.

#### Package 1015 Continue fault and October 15 comparison update (2026-10-28)

The user installed package 1015 and selected Continue in the native
`fallout-new-vegas` profile. The runtime log records the first exception at
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261027-140110.log`, 06:32:23.765: `FalloutNV.exe`
read access violation at runtime EIP `0x00c39d5e`, address `0x8c`, with
`EAX=0`, `EDI=0x32074660`, and `ESI=0x03bc7280`. The active image base is
`0x005e0000`; the corresponding preferred PE address is `0x00a59d5e` (RVA
`0x659d5e`). Exact disassembly is `mov edx,[eax+0x8c]`, after `mov eax,[edi]`:
the object at EDI has a null first word/vtable when used. This is a guest
Gamebryo object-state fault. The subsequent WineVulkan write AV and NTDLL
`abort_process`/UD2 trace are failure cleanup, not the first fault. About one
second before the guest fault, flexible memory was 12 MiB and continued to
fall; `dmem_failures=0`. This is evidence of low memory headroom, not proof of
OOM or of the reason the object became invalid. No matching dump was captured
for this run, so preserve this as log/disassembly evidence only.

The exact package-level comparison was also refined. October 15 stage
`pkgout/oct15-stickkey-10080/pkg-stage` and package-1015 stage
`pkgout/falloutnv-native-fault-map-1015-cap16/pkg-stage` both contain Wine
11.17 from the same upstream Wine source commit `490f6d5dcb`; they were built
from different Prospero/Orbis patch overlays and runtimes, not different
upstream Wine release versions. Of 1,789 common paths, 1,633 hashes differ.
The 1,615 PE-tree hash differences are not all independent game-code changes:
binary sections differ in many files, while build provenance/path differs too.
The 1015 package also has the newer launcher/relocation wrapper and current
WoW64-native backend. Its relocation PRX is present; this is not the old
missing-relocation packaging mistake.

The prior controlled package tests already constrain the likely cause:
package 1009 replaced only both NTDLL payload copies with October 15 NTDLL and
reached gameplay, then stopped presenting after several minutes; package 1010
restored the October 15 NTDLL and wineserver pair and still hung. Thus the
known October 15 result is a partial baseline (reaches gameplay, then hangs),
and reverting just NTDLL or its matched server pair does not restore stable
gameplay. The 1015 fault is not explained by native WoW64 alone either: the
same null-vtable fault family was observed on the newer runtime with the
translator selected. Do not repeat those same NTDLL/server or CPU-mode A/Bs.

The user clarified that the unmodified October 15 package itself reaches the
game but freezes within about five minutes of entering gameplay. Treat that as
a partial baseline, not a stable control. There is no value in spending another
console cycle merely to reconfirm that behavior; compare the source/runtime
deltas offline and make the next hardware run only if a specific candidate
change predicts a measurable difference.

One tempting October 15-only patch, `0602-ntdll-map-unaligned-shared-sections-private.patch`,
does not currently explain the Fallout NV fault: the exact FalloutNV.exe used
for symbolization has no PE section with `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_SHARED`. This does not
rule out a shared section in another loaded module, but it rules out blaming
that patch for the main executable's null-vtable fault without further evidence.

An offline comparison of the effective NTDLL `virtual.c` sources narrows the
memory hypothesis further. `map_image_into_view_impl()`,
`NtAllocateVirtualMemory()`, and `NtFreeVirtualMemory()` are identical between
the October 15 Orbis source tree and the current PS5-derived tree. The current
tree's behavioral changes in this file are on executable mappings: apply
`host_mprotect()` after an executable `mmap()`, and route `mprotect_exec()` via
the Prospero protection wrapper (patches 0935/0946); the other nearby changes
are Oblivion-focused diagnostics. Therefore, do not assume a broad Win32
virtual-allocation regression from the source diff. The remaining focused
question is whether executable-page protection/direct-memory migration in the
actual 1015 app payload alters low-address guest mappings or consumes the
flexible-memory headroom seen before the fault. Verify that against the exact
1015 build inputs before proposing a package A/B.

The next code investigation should compare the effective October 15 and
current Wine patch overlays around executable-page protection and memory
pressure, then use one targeted runtime change if that identifies a specific
mechanism. No new hardware run is justified until that comparison produces a
concrete prediction.

#### RX migration reservation failure and package 1016 (2026-10-28)

The exact 1012 klog at
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/GLDS10085-1012-falloutnv-prefix-repaired-20261027-185037-klog.log`
contains 570 `exec-page flex-migrate mmap failed` events and 570 matching
`exec-protect-verify ... effective=0x3 migrate=-1` failures. These pages were
left read/write, without execute permission. This is direct runtime evidence
that the existing fallback is failing, not just a flexible-memory-pressure
correlation.

The failure mechanism is now pinned to the sequence in
`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`: `pw_wine_dmem_replace(..., 0)` unmaps and
releases the direct-memory page, then reserves that address again. The next
`mmap(MAP_FIXED)` therefore targets a PS4 virtual reservation. Existing
ORBW00032/33 probe results document `sceKernelMmap(MAP_FIXED)` returning
`EINVAL` over `sceKernelReserveVirtualRange()` reservations at both low and
high addresses. The new code explicitly removes the reservation after direct
memory release, before mapping flexible memory. If that map fails, it
re-reserves and backs the page with direct memory, restores the saved contents,
and reports the recovery result. Up to 64 successful migrations are logged
only after the retry confirms effective execute permission.

Built package 1016 from the full relocatable 1015 stage. Compared with 1015,
the only staged payload changes are `sce_module/ntdll.prx` and its matching
copy at `win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/ntdll.prx`; `pkg.gp4` and `param.sfo`
change for the package title. The relocation PRX, native WoW64 backend,
profiles, input/filesystem/close-path fixes, and every other payload file are
unchanged. The 1,785-file stage was verified against its sources.

The PS4 received the 445,644,800-byte package under the expected

the package-1016 NTDLL at
`/app0/win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/ntdll.prx`.

records show `migrate=0`, `retry=0`, and effective protection `0x5` (read and
execute). There were no `flex-migrate mmap failed` or `unreserve failed`
records. The failure seen in 1012 is therefore gone for the sampled pages.

off at about 2,923 blocks; flexible memory remained about 169 MiB free and
`dmem_failures=0`. No exception or new dump was captured before the user
closed the app. This is a later graphics/startup stall, not the former RX
mapping failure; the new mechanism is working, but the title has not reached
the menu. Do not roll back the RX change. The current listeners remain active.

#### Package 1017 Continue crash diagnosis (2026-10-28)

Package 1017 advanced beyond the previous frozen frame after Continue, then
crashed. The coredump watcher retrieved and preserved
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-1017-20261028/GLDS10085_1793198364.sorbisdmp`
(399,442 bytes; SHA-256
`13af7ccf391db99ddd6f5a97f67f56f0dc133fbe383da6541478bf3909d936e3`). Matching
runtime, klog, and run metadata are in the same directory. Title ID is
`GLDS10085`, package marker 1017, and CPU mode is native WoW64. Package 1017
keeps package-1016 NTDLL (SHA-256
`ee01d616aaa844f86bcef261efa754a13e13ec3305bafe04d8ef8c2f40bf412d`) and
changes bounded win32u Vulkan diagnostics only.

The first actionable exception is a guest access violation, not the later
SIGILL: Wine reports FalloutNV.exe read fault at runtime EIP `0x00c39d5e`,
address `0x8c`, with `EAX=0`, `EDI=0x3206e660`, and `ESI=0x32e0024`. The exact
console executable is
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/FalloutNV-console-active-20261027.exe`
(SHA-256 `a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`),
loaded at `0x005e0000`. Translating through its preferred base `0x00400000`
gives preferred address `0x00a59d5e` (RVA `0x659d5e`). Exact disassembly is
`mov edx,[eax+0x8c]`; the preceding instruction loads `[edi]` into EAX. The
object at `EDI=0x3206e660` therefore has a null first pointer/vtable. This is
the same Gamebryo object-state fault site seen in 1012/1013/1015 Continue
runs, including the earlier `0x00a59d5e` / `0x8c` signature. It is not a new
1017 regression or an RX migration failure.

Immediately before that exception, the guest-fault mapper recorded an access
at `0x3206d990` in the 16 KiB range `0x3206c000-0x32070000`, initially with
protection 0; its fault hook changed the range to read/write. The later
faulting object is in that same range and readable, but its first pointer was
zero. This makes the bad object/state concrete; it does not establish why the
object was null. Flexible memory was about 14 MiB at the preceding sample,
with `dmem_failures=0`, so there is no evidence this was an allocation
failure.

The later host fault at `winevulkan.so+0x3aa23` (`0xc0000005`, write to
`0x280f1ff51f`) and repeated NTDLL `0xc000001d` at `abort_process`/`ud2` are
downstream exception/teardown faults, matching prior runs. Do not target the
SIGSYS/UD2 or rebuild only to change the Vulkan diagnostic patch. Preserve
the 1016 RX migration correction and compare the guest object/map lifetime
against 1012/1013/1015 before choosing a targeted change.

#### FalloutNV null-vtable guard candidate (2026-10-28)

Prepared a title-local executable patch candidate for the recurring guest fault
at `FalloutNV.exe` preferred address `0x00a59d5e` (RVA `0x659d5e`). The helper
starts at RVA `0x659d30`. It walks a linked list, fetches each item, saves the
next node before invoking the item's virtual method, and reads the method slot
at vtable offset `0x8c`. The crash object is non-null but its first DWORD is
zero. The patched helper adds a null-vtable check and skips that entry while
preserving the original traversal order, threshold check, return behavior, and
callee-saved registers.

This remains an isolated candidate, not a shared Wine fix and not in any PKG.
The repeat fault is inside FalloutNV.exe; changing Vulkan diagnostics or the
shared package cannot guard this game-owned dereference. The exact input
FalloutNV.exe SHA-256 is
`a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`; the
patched candidate SHA-256 is
`f1950861167a2ae3a0c228bf88f0e58e15c6e065865b01c19e3b481d880bc731`. The
version-locked patch utility is `scripts/patch-falloutnv-null-vtable.py`,
with assembly and instructions in
`src/falloutnv-config/patches/null-vtable-guard/`. It changes exactly the
80-byte helper body, preserves executable size, and rejects any other input
hash or function bytes. The generated executable is kept outside the repo at
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-null-vtable-patch/FalloutNV.exe`.

The raw 1017 `.sorbisdmp` remains preserved with its metadata. This candidate
was derived from the first-exception Wine guest trace and exact static
FalloutNV.exe disassembly; locating/running the raw `.sorbisdmp` decoder remains
outstanding.

The candidate is now installed and verified in the active console prefix at
`/data/prospero-win/prefixes/fallout-new-vegas/drive_c/Games/FalloutNV/FalloutNV.exe`.
The original was retained on-console at
`/data/prospero-win/prefixes/fallout-new-vegas/drive_c/Games/FalloutNV/FalloutNV.exe.codex-backup-null-vtable-20261028-a50c0e91`.
FTP RETR confirmed the backup SHA-256 is the expected original
`a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`; FTP RETR
of the active executable confirmed candidate SHA-256
`f1950861167a2ae3a0c228bf88f0e58e15c6e065865b01c19e3b481d880bc731`. No PKG
or shared Wine payload was changed. The FTP transfer used GoldHEN on port 2121
without login; existing loggers/watchers were left running.

Next: run the same native WoW64 Fallout NV profile, package, prefix, and Continue
save that produced the repeat fault. The executable guard is a candidate, not a
confirmed fix. Watch whether it passes the prior Continue crash site and note

#### 2026-10-28: patched-EXE run, rollback, and Oct 15 → now regression analysis

**Patched-EXE run (08:16) and rollback.** The null-vtable guard candidate was
run once on package 1017, native, profile `fallout-new-vegas`. It did not reach
Continue. The dump was captured at
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-1017-guard-20261028/GLDS10085_1793200614.sorbisdmp`
(287,747 bytes) with full run metadata in `run-metadata.txt` beside it.

The first fault is a **new signature** not present in any of the 40 preserved
run logs: a *write* to `0x00000078` at runtime EIP `0x00BEB31D`, thread 0x24,
at the 320x240 startup stage (`frames_put=3 shown=1`).

    60a717:  8b 45 ec     mov  eax, [ebp-0x14]
    60a71a:  d9 45 0c     fld  dword [ebp+0xc]
    60a71d:  d9 58 78     fstp dword [eax+0x78]   <-- fault, EAX=0

RVA `0x0060B31D` -> preferred VA `0x00A0B31D` -> .text file offset `0x60A71D`.
**These bytes are byte-identical in the original and the patched executable.**
The patch changed exactly 79 bytes at file offset `0x659131-0x65917F`; the fault
is ~322 KiB away and Continue was never reached, so the guarded helper was not
the faulting code. The patch is not exonerated as a whole (it cannot be proven
never to have run), but it is not the faulting site.

Per the user's direction, **the EXE-patching approach is abandoned**: it does
not generalize to other titles. The console executable was rolled back and
re-verified by FTP RETR as the original
`a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`. The
candidate is parked on-console as
`FalloutNV.exe.null-vtable-candidate-f1950861`. Fix this class of failure in
Wine, not per title.

**Correction to the existing NTDLL isolation.** The handoff presents packages
1009 and 1010 as the NTDLL/wineserver A/B. Both ran with `cpu=translator`.
Since gameplay testing has moved to native, **there is no native A/B isolating
NTDLL**, and 1009's "reached gameplay" result must not be read as evidence
about the native path.

**Oct 15 → now behavioral delta (offline, from git).** Patches added to
`src/wine-patches-ps4` since Oct 15 number 66. Excluding pure trace/log/probe
patches, the behavioral ones cluster into three groups:

- *Executable-page protection and repair* (all new since Oct 15):
  `0933` guest-fault-page query, `0935` force Prospero exec protection after
  mmap, `0946` Prospero mprotect for executable pages, `0954` repair RX in the
  signal handler, `0955` retry adjacent exec pages, `0956` retry split image
  regions. Plus `prospero-win/wine/patches/1000` (retry a resolved guest fault)
  and the `pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` migration reworked by package 1016.
- *Native WoW64 signal/trap path*: `0910`, `0930`, `0934`, `0937`, `0938`,
  `0939`, `0940`, `0943`, `0944`, `0945`, `0960`.
- *Unrelated*: filesystem `0908`, input `0903`/`0904`/`0907`, win32u
  composition `0901`/`0902`.

None of this exists upstream. Upstream Wine's `ntdll/unix/virtual.c` has no
direct-memory backing, no page migration and no RX repair; the whole mechanism
is a port addition, so "compare to upstream" here means "compare to not doing
it at all".

**Leading mechanism, stated with its limits.** `migrate_exec_page_to_flex()`
in `src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` does this to a live 16 KiB granule:

    memcpy(saved, page, 0x4000);              // snapshot
    pw_wine_dmem_replace(..., 0);             // release direct memory -> unmapped
    sceKernelMunmap(page, 0x4000);            // drop the reservation -> unmapped
    mmap(page, ..., MAP_FIXED|MAP_ANON);      // fresh zero page
    memcpy(mapped, saved, 0x4000);            // restore the snapshot
    pw_wine_dmem_adopt(...);

There is **no locking anywhere in that file** (only atomics for log counters).
Between the release and the restoring memcpy the page is unmapped or
zero-filled, and any concurrent write by another thread is either faulted or
silently overwritten by the stale snapshot. In the 1012 klog this ran **570
times in a 9-second burst at 18:52:18-18:52:27, 102 seconds into the run** —
i.e. during active multithreaded gameplay, not during single-threaded image
load. Package 1016 did not remove this window; it made the previously failing
`mmap` succeed, so the migrations now complete instead of bailing out.

This is independently a real defect and it is title-agnostic. **It is not yet
tied to the observed crashes.** The gap is explicit: this function has one
caller, the executable-protection path, while the recurring faults are at guest
*heap* addresses (`0x06…`-`0x32…`). Corrupting a 16 KiB executable granule does
not directly explain a null vtable field on a heap object. Do not present this
as the cause until that link is established or ruled out.

Supporting but non-conclusive: patch `1000` exists precisely because a guest
fault was observed on a page that a fresh kernel query then reported as mapped
and writable — a transient-mapping signature consistent with a race of this
shape.

**Proposed next isolation (offline-justified, one variable).** Build NTDLL from
the current 1017 source with `migrate_exec_page_to_flex()` short-circuited to
return 0 (leave the granule as direct memory, non-executable), changing nothing
else. Package from the full 1017 stage replacing only the two `ntdll.prx`
copies. Prediction: if the migration window is implicated, the native Fallout NV
Continue path changes behavior; if it is not, the same guest fault recurs
unchanged and the exec-protection cluster is excluded, leaving the native
WoW64 signal/trap cluster as the next candidate. Oblivion may regress in this
build — that is expected and acceptable for an isolation run.

#### 2026-10-28: Fallout on the Oct 15 baseline — window B confirmed, plus a third, pre-existing bug

Fallout NV was run on `GLDS10080` (Oct 15), same `fallout-new-vegas` profile,
prefix and Continue save.

    09:05:52  PW_WINE64 cpu=native prefix_cpu=1
    09:05:52  PW_WINE64 profile id=fallout-new-vegas

**The Oct 15 package runs Fallout in native WoW64 as well.** Both sides of
window B are therefore `cpu=native`, which kills the "the default switched to
native in prospero-win `73675f7`" explanation. The difference between 10080 and
10091 is a real code change, not a CPU-mode configuration difference.

It reached gameplay. About 3.5 minutes later, while the user was entering
**VATS**, it faulted:

    09:09:22  wine: Unhandled page fault on read access to 00000024
              at address 7B2923FD (thread 0024)

`0x7B250000` is `C:\windows\system32\d3d9.dll`, so this is RVA `0x423FD` inside
d3d9 — a fault in a Wine module on a heavy D3D9 path, not a guest fault in the
game image. It is a different failure from the probe-1 Continue crash
(guest read of `0x090613E0` at runtime EIP `0x0066B0DA`).

**This is a third, separate problem, and it is pre-existing in the Oct 15
baseline.** It has been masquerading as the recorded Oct 15 behaviour "reaches
the game but freezes within about five minutes" — it is not a freeze and not a
timeout, it is a D3D9 fault triggered by a specific in-game action. None of the
memory or NTDLL work was ever going to address it, and it must not be counted
against any build in either bisection.

**The three problems, cleanly separated:**

| # | Problem | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fallout crashes at Continue, never reaches gameplay | window **Oct 21 16:46 -> Oct 22 12:09** (~10 builds) |
| 2 | GTA IV hangs at the menu | window **Oct 22 12:09 -> Oct 28 07:29** |
| 3 | Fallout faults in `d3d9.dll+0x423FD` entering VATS | **pre-existing in Oct 15**, not a regression |

Discriminator for bisection B is simply **does Fallout get into the game**.
10080 does (then hits problem 3); 10091 does not. Ignore VATS while bisecting.

Endpoint diff, for reference if a payload-split approach is chosen later:

    common files 1789, differing 1633
      1615  win/         Wine PE tree
        16  sce_module/  ntdll, wow64native, wineserver, win32u, winevulkan,

Both stages are Wine 11.17 from upstream commit `490f6d5dcb`, so the PE
differences are patch-overlay changes, not a Wine version bump — which makes a
PE-vs-PRX split plausible, but note that PE and unix PRX halves of the same DLL
share the unixlib interface, so a mismatched split may fail to start or
silently fall back (package 1009 fell back to the translator for exactly this
reason). Any such package must be checked for `cpu=` in its runtime log before
its result is believed.

  every other file is byte-identical

#### 2026-10-28: ROOT CAUSE of the GTA IV stall — RADV host-memory exhaustion

The GTA IV "hang at the menu" is **not** a graphics-API hang, not input, and not
the `radv.env` settings. It is a host-memory exhaustion that kills the render
loop. Evidence, from the 10:23:36 run of `GLDS10085` package 1017 with
`radv.env` already cleared:

`/data/prospero-win/radv.log` (fetched to
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-ab-20261028/radv-20261028-1017-stall.log`,
432,112 bytes) ends with:

    MESA: info:  radv/orbis: graphics pipeline enter #642 stages=0 mask=0x0 flags=0x800 library=1
    MESA: error: RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL stage=stage_array_alloc result=-1 bytes=95760
    MESA: info:  radv/orbis: graphics pipeline return #642 result=-1 elapsed_us=99

`result=-1` is `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY`. **641 graphics pipelines succeed;
pipeline #642 fails on a 95,760-byte host allocation, and RADV logs nothing
further.** That is the last RADV activity of the run.

Correlated runtime memory telemetry:

    10:24:32  flexible_free=168960K  dmem=337136K
    10:24:37  flexible_free= 62464K  dmem=545600K     dmem +208 MiB, flexible -106 MiB
    10:24:42  flexible_free= 62464K  dmem=607424K     dmem +62 MiB more
    10:24:43  <-- pipeline #642 fails (95,760 bytes)
    10:24:47  flexible_free= 62464K  dmem=604352K     frozen from here on

Direct memory ballooned 337 -> 607 MiB in about ten seconds while flexible free
collapsed 169 -> 62 MiB, then both froze. `dmem_failures=0` throughout — that
counter does not count this failure, which is the blind spot noted repeatedly
in this handoff.

The downstream picture now makes sense and should not be chased again:

- Vulkan presents ran at ~60/s to **#600 at 10:24:43** and then stopped. The
  earlier reading of "presents continuing while the game is stuck" was wrong:
  the trace only prints every 120th present, and there are none after #600.
- Audio continues because it is a separate thread; `frames_put` freezes at 7;
  `minflt` freezes at 149,253 and `inblock` at 3,999. All consequences.
- The user identified the stall point precisely as the **second of five GTA IV
  legal/EULA screens**, which auto-advance and do **not** wait for input. An
  input hypothesis was raised and retracted on that basis; `pad buttons=0`
  simply reflected no button being pressed.

**Hypotheses ruled out today, with evidence:**

- *RX / executable-page migration* — probe 1 runs that machinery successfully
  (`exec-range verify call=10641 ... effective=0x7`) while both Vulkan titles
  reach gameplay.
- *`win32u` / `force-copy-wsi`* — single-variable revert changed nothing.
- *`radv.env` (`ORBIS_WSI_NO_DEFER=1`, `ORBIS_WSI_FORCE_COPY=1`)* — cleared on
  the console; the stall is unchanged. It **was** real for performance: the user
  reports reaching the stall faster without the 8.1 MiB per-present copy, so
  leave it cleared. Original preserved at
  `~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-ab-20261028/radv.env.console-20261028`.
- *Native WoW64 backend* — both sides of window B log `cpu=native`.

**Connection to Titan Quest.** The Titan Quest checkpoint added patches aimed at
exactly this failure class: `0004-radv-command-buffer-host-oom-site`,
`0006-radv-command-buffer-allocation-diagnostics`,
`0007-radv-descriptor-pool-allocation-diagnostics` and
`0009-radv-graphics-pipeline-failure-stage` — the last of which is what printed
the `RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL stage=` line above. It also routed Wine's Unix
heap onto direct memory (`heap_map`/`heap_unmap`/`heap_log_fallback` in
`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`, 159 lines) specifically because "Wine's
Unix heap also consumes flexible memory". Titan Quest's freeze is plausibly the
same host-OOM, but that is **not yet established** — do not assert it without a
Titan Quest run showing the same `RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL`.

Note: `heap_log_fallback` never fired in this run, and `HEAP_DIRECT_HEADROOM`
is not present in the current working tree's `pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`, so the
direct-heap change may not be in the 1017 build at all. Confirm which build
actually carries it before attributing the dmem balloon to it.

**Next work is on memory, not on the bisection.** The questions are: what
allocates 270 MiB of direct memory in ten seconds around the first pipeline
batch; what ceiling it hits at ~600 MiB; and why a 95 KB host allocation then
fails. `radv.log` is the best instrument — it already carries the TQ allocation
diagnostics. Compare a working build (10091 or 10126) against 1017 on the same
GTA IV startup and diff their dmem growth curves and pipeline counts.

#### 2026-10-28: CAUSE IDENTIFIED — the Fallout line was built without the Titan Quest runtime work

**The Titan Quest branch was never merged.**
`git merge-base --is-ancestor checkpoint/titan-quest-2026-10-26 HEAD` is false
for both `fallout3-initial-support` (the current branch) and `main`. Every
package from 1006 to 1017 was therefore built from a tree that **lacks** the
Oct 22-26 runtime improvements. This is not a regression introduced by later
work; it is a different lineage that never received it.

Proved directly from the shipped binaries, by testing each `ntdll.prx` for the
direct-heap log string:

| Build | direct-heap | exec-range | GTA IV |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `oct15-stickkey-10080` | 0 | 0 | works |
| `orbis-native-rx-reloc-10091` | **1** | 8 | works |
| `orbis-native-rx-reloc-10126` | **1** | 8 | works |
| `orbis-native-netctl-10167` | **1** | 8 | untested |
| `falloutnv-native-rx-wsi-1017` | **0** | 1 | **stalls** |

**Mechanism.** `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0072-wine-heap-pluggable-backing.patch`
gives Wine's Unix heap a pluggable backing (`wine/ps5/pw_wine_heap.{c,h}`), and
`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` (+159 lines on the checkpoint) supplies the
direct-memory implementation (`heap_map`/`heap_unmap`/`heap_log_fallback`).
Together they move Wine's Unix heap off flexible memory. Its own comment states
the motivation: *"Wine's Unix heap also consumes flexible memory, including
allocations made for ntdll and other runtime users."*

Without them, Wine's heap falls back to anonymous `mmap` — flexible memory —
and starves RADV. Measured on the same GTA IV startup:

| | flexible_free | dmem | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| control (direct heap present) | holds **214 MB** | 322 MB, **flat** | reached gameplay |
| 1017 (direct heap absent) | collapses to **62 MB** | 607 MB, climbing | pipeline #642 fails at 95,760 bytes |

RADV's pipeline allocations are host allocations; with the Wine heap consuming
flexible memory there is not enough left, and `RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL
stage=stage_array_alloc result=-1` follows. Everything downstream — presents
stopping at #600, frozen `minflt`/`inblock`, audio continuing — is consequence.

Not fully explained: why 1017's `dmem` is *higher* (607 vs 322 MB) despite
lacking the direct heap. The flexible-memory collapse is the well-supported
part; do not assert the dmem difference without checking the `exec-range`
difference (8 strings vs 1) which indicates the executable-range work also
differs between the lineages.

**Behavioural (non-diagnostic) changes missing from the current branch:**

    src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c                              +159
    src/native/pw_vulkan_radv_ps4.c                                  +37
    src/native/pw_platform_bind_ps4.c                                +57
    src/native/pw_netctl_ps4.{c,h}        network adapter  (user: keep, valuable)
    src/native/pw_osk_ps4.{c,h}           on-screen keyboard
    src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0072-wine-heap-pluggable-backing.patch
    src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0073-wine64-allow-flexible-heap-ab-control.patch
    src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0083-skip-active-ps4-stack-force-exec.patch
    src/mesa-patches-ps4/0029-force-copy-wsi-ab.patch   (env-gated, inert unless radv.env sets it)

Note `wine64_main.c` in the current tree already references the absent
`src/native/pw_osk_ps4.h`, which is why the Oct 26 handoff records that the
standard app rebuild "cannot compile". That is the same missing-merge problem.

**Recommended fix:** merge or cherry-pick the Titan Quest runtime work into the
working branch, rebuild, and retest GTA IV. `0073` provides a built-in A/B
opt-out for the direct heap via Wine's module environment, so the change can be
validated both ways from one binary. Keep the netctl work — the user has
confirmed it is valuable and should be preserved, not reverted.

**Also fixed today, independently:** `/data/prospero-win/radv.env` still carried
the Oct 24 Titan Quest A/B settings (`ORBIS_WSI_NO_DEFER=1`,
`ORBIS_WSI_FORCE_COPY=1`), which forced an 8.1 MiB copy per present on any build
whose `libvulkan` is `2afce50c` or newer. It has been cleared (original at
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-ab-20261028/radv.env.console-20261028`).
The user confirms the flashy/stuttery transitions are **gone** and startup is
faster. Leave it cleared. This was not the stall, but it was a real defect that
affected Titan Quest throughout.

**Current best known state:** the `ORBW00019` card holding the 10126 stage with
10140's `libvulkan` reaches GTA IV gameplay with no flicker, `dmem` flat at
322 MB and `flexible_free` holding 214 MB. Its close-time crash is the known
`submitDone` suspend fault (`0026-wine64-report-gnm-submit-done.patch`), not a
result.

#### 2026-10-28 CORRECTION: the memory mechanism above is NOT established

The preceding section concluded that 1017 stalls because it lacks the
direct-heap backing, so Wine's Unix heap consumes flexible memory and starves
RADV. **That conclusion is withdrawn.** It was drawn from a 60-second early
window of the control run; the full run contradicts it.

Complete control-run curve (`ORBW00019`, 10126 stage with 10140 `libvulkan`):

    10:35:56  flexible_free=262144K  dmem=281344K
    10:36:11  flexible_free=219136K  dmem=322112K    <- the window quoted earlier
    10:38:28  flexible_free= 49152K  dmem=956864K
    10:39:09  flexible_free= 88064K  dmem=1063168K
    10:39:24  flexible_free= 79872K  dmem=1063168K

`dmem` did not plateau at 322 MiB; it climbed to **1.06 GiB**, and
`flexible_free` fell to **48 MiB**. Side by side:

| | flexible_free | dmem | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| control | **48 MiB** | **1.06 GiB** | reached gameplay |
| 1017 | 62 MiB | 607 MiB | pipeline #642 fails at 95,760 bytes |

The control ran with *less* free flexible memory and *more* direct memory than
1017 and worked. Absolute memory levels therefore do not explain the failure,
and the "Wine heap starves RADV" mechanism is unsupported.

The comparison was also never single-variable: the control's `libvulkan` is
`01ed7f33` and 1017's is `e53f7b31`, and their `ntdll` differs too. The
control's RADV predates `0009-radv-graphics-pipeline-failure-stage`, so its
`radv.log` contains **no pipeline lines at all** — we do not know how many
pipelines it created or whether it ever reached an equivalent of #642.

Also: the control run ended because the user closed it
(`AppFocusChanged [ORBW00019] -> [NPXS20001]` at 10:39:27, then
`kill_thread ... violent=1`), not because it stalled. A monitor reported
"STALLED" from stale counters of a dead process.

**What remains established:**

1. 1017 stalls at GTA IV's second legal screen, and RADV's last act is
   `RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL stage=stage_array_alloc result=-1 bytes=95760`
   after 641 successful pipelines. Presents stop at #600 in the same second.
2. The `ORBW00019` build (10126 stage + 10140 `libvulkan`) reaches GTA IV
   gameplay with no flicker.
3. `checkpoint/titan-quest-2026-10-26` is **not** merged into
   `fallout3-initial-support` or `main`, so packages 1006-1017 were built
   without the Oct 22-26 runtime work. This is factual and independently
   important — it also explains the Oct 26 note that the app rebuild "cannot
   compile" because `wine64_main.c` includes the absent `src/native/pw_osk_ps4.h`.
4. `ntdll.prx` binaries confirm the split: direct-heap strings present in
   10091/10126/10167, absent in 1017.
5. `radv.env` cleared: flicker gone, startup faster. Independent, real fix.

**What is NOT established:** that the missing direct-heap work causes the
stall. The correlation is real but confounded by `libvulkan` and `ntdll`
differing in the same comparison.

**To settle it properly, one single-variable test:** take the 1017 stage and
replace only the two `ntdll.prx` copies with 10167's (`72a265f7`), which carries
the direct-heap code and is the nearest build on the other lineage. Keep 1017's
`libvulkan` so the RADV pipeline diagnostics still fire. If GTA IV then passes
the second legal screen, the heap work is implicated; if it still fails at a
pipeline allocation, it is not. Check `cpu=` in the runtime log before trusting
the result — a mismatched `ntdll`/`wow64native` pair can silently fall back to
the translator, as package 1009 did.

#### 2026-10-28: ntdll single-variable swap — memory failure fixed, a second stall remains

Package `pkgout/ab-1017-with-10167-ntdll/` = the complete 1017 stage with

exactly those two files; `libvulkan`, `wow64native`, `wineserver`, `win32u` and
the whole PE tree are byte-identical, so the RADV pipeline diagnostics still
fire. Backend confirmed started, no translator fallback:
`cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, `wow64native initialized: cs32=0033 ss32=003b
cs64=0043 avx=1`, `profile id=gtaiv-test`.

Result: **the memory failure is fixed; a different stall appears.**

| | 1017 | 1017 + 10167 ntdll |
|---|---|---|
| pipelines | 641 ok, **#642 FAILS** (OOM, 95,760 B) | **629 ok, zero failures** |
| last RADV entry | `RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL` | pipeline #629 `result=0`, then silence |

| presents before stall | 600 | fewer than 120 |
| stall point | after the legal screens | **before the first frame** |

Two candidate explanations, not yet separated:

1. A second, independent bug that 1017 never reached because it died on memory
   first.
2. An artifact of the mix — 10167's `ntdll` with 1017's `wineserver` and
   `wow64native`. The coupling warning in this handoff applies, and package
   1009 hit exactly this class of problem.

#### 2026-10-28: 10167 in-game measurements — free memory is NOT the discriminator

With GTA IV in gameplay on 10167, `radv.log` (7,031,744 bytes, preserved as
`run-ab-20261028/radv-20261028-10167-working.log`):

| | 10167 (works) | 1017 (fails) |
|---|---|---|
| pipelines | **840 ok, 0 fail** | 641 ok, **#642 fails** (95,760 B) |
| `flexible_free` | **49,152K (48 MiB)** | 62,464K (62 MiB) |
| `dmem` | ~950,000K | 607,424K |
| `dmem_failures` | 0 | 0 |

**The working build runs at less free flexible memory than the failing one**
(48 MiB vs 62 MiB) while creating 200 more pipelines with zero failures.

This retires the "flexible collapse starves RADV" framing recorded earlier in
this session. Absolute free memory does not predict the failure. The mechanism
is allocation **routing**: with the direct-heap backing, Wine's Unix heap is
served from direct memory and the flexible pool stays genuinely available to
RADV's host allocations. Without it the Wine heap has already taken that pool,
and a 95 KB request fails despite 62 MiB appearing free.

The earlier "marginal headroom, expect it to return on a heavier title" caution
is therefore **also withdrawn** — it was based on comparing free-memory levels,
which these numbers show is not the relevant quantity. The correct statement is
that 1017 lacks the routing, not that 10167 is near a cliff.

#### 2026-10-28: Fallout on 10167 — the 320x240 startup stall is NOT graphics or memory

Fallout NV was launched on 10167 (`createApp ORBW00019` 11:04:47,
`profile id=fallout-new-vegas`, `cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`). It hit the known
intermittent startup stall rather than reaching the menu:

    11:05:38  frames_put=3  last=320x240  minflt=22695  inblock=1494
    11:06:39  frames_put=3  last=320x240  minflt=22695  inblock=1494   (frozen 60s+)

    pipelines ok=2  fail=0  last submit #1 result=0
    "syncobj POLL says not yet, label 0 - a zero timeout is a question, not a failure"

**RADV is idle and has failed at nothing** — 2 pipelines, 1 submission. The
stall is therefore **upstream of rendering**: the game never reaches meaningful
drawing. With 231 MiB flexible free, `inblock` frozen (no further file reads)
and no exception, it is not graphics, not memory, and not I/O starvation.

This is the stall previously recorded on packages 1011, 1012, 1014 and 1016
("one/three 320x240 frames, then no additional presents"). **It is a third distinct Fallout failure**,
separate from the Continue crash and from the pre-existing `d3d9.dll+0x423FD`
VATS fault. Previous handoff entries treated it as a property of whichever
package was installed at the time; the RADV evidence here shows it is not.

half the time". The user rejects that characterisation, and it was an
