    11:11:05  flexible_free= 17408K
    11:11:10  flexible_free=  8192K
    11:11:15  flexible_free=  6144K   dmem=130720K
    11:11:18  FAULT  c0000005 read 0x3009D196 at EIP 0x00A5DD25 thread 0x94

Flexible memory fell **104 MiB -> 6 MiB in 45 seconds**, then the guest faulted.
Prior Fallout crashes were recorded at 11-14 MiB; this run watched the whole
collapse.

**RADV was completely healthy at the crash:** 1,059 graphics pipelines, **zero
failures** (`run-ab-20261028/radv-20261028-fallout-continue-crash.log`,
3,515,974 bytes). Fallout's crash is therefore purely guest-side and is **not**
the GTA IV failure mode. The two bugs are confirmed separate by direct
measurement, not just by bisection windows.

Fault location: this run loaded `FalloutNV.exe` at its **preferred base
`0x00400000`** (not the usual relocated `0x005e0000`), so EIP `0x00A5DD25` is
already the preferred VA. That places it in the same Gamebryo helper cluster as
every previously recorded Fallout fault — `0x00a59d5e`, `0x00a5a152`,
`0x00a5ac0f`, `0x00a5dca8` — at a different offset.

**The key contrast with GTA IV:**

| | Fallout (crashes) | GTA IV on 10167 (works) |
|---|---|---|
| `dmem` | **130 MiB** | ~950 MiB |
| `flexible_free` at end | **6 MiB** | 48 MiB, stable |
| RADV | 1,059 pipelines, 0 fail | 840 pipelines, 0 fail |

**Fallout barely uses direct memory** — 130 MiB against GTA IV's 950 MiB — so
its allocations are served from flexible memory, which it drains to 6 MiB. The
recurring null/garbage pointer dereferences are consistent with an allocation
failing and returning NULL into Gamebryo code that does not check it.

**Next question for Fallout, and it is a new one:** why does Fallout's
allocation go to flexible memory while GTA IV's goes to direct memory on the
same runtime and the same backend? Both are PE32 + DXVK + native WoW64. The
answer determines whether the fix is routing (as it was for GTA IV) or headroom.
Compare the dmem region setup and `__wine_ps5_mmap` paths taken by the two
titles before proposing a change.

Dumps preserved: `ORBW00019_1793209168.sorbisdmp` and
`ORBW00019_1793211079.sorbisdmp` in `run-ab-20261028/`.

#### 2026-10-28: open item — startup latency regression vs Oct 15

The user reports these builds are consistently **slower to first frame** than
the Oct 15 (`GLDS10080`) package: they sit on the black screen noticeably
longer. This is a performance regression, tracked separately from the stalls,
and it is not new — `HANDOFF-2026-10-20.md` already has a section titled
"Startup latency is the gate".

Record it as its own item. It has been easy to confuse with the stalls all day,
because "slow to reach the first frame" and "never reaches the first frame"
look identical until the counters are checked. The distinguishing test is
whether `minflt`/`inblock` are climbing:

- loading : both climb steadily (e.g. `minflt` 22,687 -> 66,851 in 20 s)
- stalled : both flat (e.g. `minflt` 22,695 and `inblock` 1,494 for 60 s+)

Do not chase this until the Fallout allocation question is settled.

#### 2026-10-28: Oblivion works on 10167; Fallout's dxvk.conf ruled out

**Oblivion reaches gameplay on 10167** (`profile id=oblivion-gog`,
`cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, zero exceptions, `inputs=424`). Build 10167
therefore runs **two of three titles**; Fallout is the only failure.

Three-way allocation comparison on the same build:

| Title | `dmem` | `flexible_free` | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| GTA IV | ~950 MiB | 48 MiB, stable | works |
| Oblivion | ~317 MiB | dips to 95 MiB, **recovers to 120 MiB** | works |
| Fallout | **130-150 MiB** | drains to 6-9 MiB, then faults | crashes |

Oblivion's flexible memory is reclaimed rather than leaked, which Fallout's is
not. Fallout is alone in taking a small direct-memory allocation while draining
flexible to nothing.

**`dxvk.conf` ruled out.** The per-game
`/data/prospero-win/prefixes/fallout-new-vegas/drive_c/Games/FalloutNV/dxvk.conf`
contained exactly `d3d9.maxAvailableMemory = 256` (30 bytes; copy preserved at
`run-ab-20261028/dxvk.conf.fallout-before-removal`, renamed on console to
`dxvk.conf.disabled-20261028`, not deleted). It had been added to chase the
VATS crash — which this session established is a **pre-existing Oct 15 baseline
bug**, so it was treating a symptom that predates all of this.

Removing it changed nothing material:

    WITH    dxvk.conf : dmem 130 MiB,  flexible 104 -> 6 MiB, fault at EIP 0x00A5DD25
    WITHOUT dxvk.conf : dmem ~140 MiB, flexible 160 -> 9 MiB, fault at EIP 0x00A3368C

Same failure shape, same Gamebryo helper region, different offset. `dmem`
barely moved. **The 256 MiB budget hint is not what keeps Fallout off direct
memory.** Leave it disabled or restore it as preferred; it is not a factor.

**The open question is now sharp and offline-answerable:** on one runtime, one
backend, three PE32 + DXVK titles — why does Fallout's allocation go to
flexible memory while GTA IV's and Oblivion's go to direct memory? Compare the
`__wine_ps5_dmem_region` setup and the `__wine_ps5_mmap` paths the three titles
take at startup. The answer decides whether Fallout needs routing (as GTA IV
did) or genuinely needs more headroom.

RADV is healthy in Fallout's crashes (1,059 pipelines, zero failures), so this
is purely a guest-side allocation problem and not a graphics one.

#### 2026-10-28: exec-page migration is load-bearing — cannot be removed

**What changed between Fallout's last-good and first-bad build.** Comparing
`ntdll.prx` strings, 10080 (works) versus 10091 (crashes), the entire
executable-page protection and RX-repair machinery arrives in that window:

    ntdll ps4-fault-rx-retry build=0970 / ps4-image-fault / ps4-image-hook
    ntdll ps4-rx-fault-recovered / ps4-rx-fault-unrepaired
    ntdll ps4-sig-fault-frame / ps4-sig-fault-stack
    PS4_OBLIVION mprotect-range enter ...
    wine-ps4: exec-page flex-migrate ... / exec-range flex-migrate ...
    wine-ps4: exec-range build=0974 / exec-protect-verify build=0975
    wine-ps4: dmem-exec-selfcheck build=0971
    wine-ps4: direct heap fallback / pw_wine_heap_set_backing

Correlation across four builds is perfect:

| Build | flex-migrate | rx-retry | image-hook | Fallout |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 10080 | 0 | 0 | 0 | **works** |
| 10091 | 9 | 2 | 1 | crashes |
| 10167 | 9 | 2 | 1 | crashes |
| 1017 | 4 | 2 | 1 | crashes |

It spans both lineages, so it is not a branch artefact. Note this is **not**
the direct heap: 10080 and 1017 both have `direct-heap=0` yet differ in
outcome.

**Test attempted and its result.** Built `pkgout/ab-10167-no-exec-migration/`
from the complete 10167 stage with only the two `ntdll.prx` copies replaced,
compiled from the Titan Quest glue with `migrate_exec_page_to_flex()`
short-circuited to `return 0`. Verified before upload: `direct-heap=1` and
`rx-retry=2` matching 10167, `flex-migrate` strings down 9 -> 5 (dead code
eliminated), identical binary size, payload diff exactly the two PRX copies.

It **crashes at startup with SIGILL** about four seconds in, 69 log lines:

    11:52:59.192  wine-ps4: exec mprotect addr=10000 bytes=16384 prot=0x5 sce=0 libc=0
    11:52:59.192  wine-ps4: dmem-exec-selfcheck build=0971 addr=10000 ... prot=0x1
    11:52:59.193  wine-ps4: exec-range flex-migrate complete addr=400000 bytes=16384
    11:52:59.196  # A user thread receives a fatal signal
    11:52:59.196  # signal: 4 (SIGILL)

**The per-page migration is load-bearing.** It exists to make executable pages
actually executable; leaving a granule as non-executable direct memory makes
the first instruction fetch illegal. The `prot=0x1` in the self-check line
(read-only, no execute) is the immediate cause. **It cannot be removed — any
fix must make it safe.**

Also learned: there are **two** migration paths, `exec-page` (per-page) and
`exec-range` (range-based). This test disabled only the first; `exec-range
flex-migrate complete` still appears in the klog. Any future attempt must
account for both.

The race hypothesis is therefore **still unproven**. The correlation is strong
but the window contains the whole RX cluster, not just the migration, and the
isolation test could not run.

**Suggested next step — prove or refute the race directly rather than by
removal.** Add a bounded diagnostic that records the address range and
timestamp of each migration, then at each guest fault checks whether the fault
address falls inside a recently migrated granule. If guest faults land in
pages migrated moments earlier, the race is demonstrated; if they never do, it
is excluded and attention moves to the rest of the RX cluster. This is
diagnostic-only and carries no risk of the SIGILL above.

A design alternative worth considering if the race is confirmed: migrate
executable pages **eagerly at image map time**, while startup is still
effectively single-threaded, instead of lazily on fault. The dangerous
migrations are the 570 observed in a 9-second burst 102 seconds into a run,
during live multithreaded gameplay.

`ORBW00019` on the console has been restored to 10167 (445,382,656 bytes,
size-verified), which runs GTA IV and Oblivion.

Merge committed as `acebbeb`. `src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` was the only
conflict and was resolved to the checkpoint's version, because that is exactly
10167's code and is proven on hardware. This branch's
`restore_exec_page_to_dmem()` and `protect_exec_host_span()` came from the 1017
lineage, which does not work, and were intentionally dropped; re-add and test
deliberately if wanted. `src/native/pw_osk_ps4.h` is now present, so the app
rebuild is unblocked.

Two packages built from the merged tree, both repacked from the full 10167
stage replacing only the two `ntdll.prx` copies:

| Variant | Title | page-migrate | range-migrate | direct-heap |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **B** "Merged runtime B (both migrations)" | `ORBW00019` | 4 | **5** | 1 |
| **A** "Merged runtime A (no range migration)" | `GLDS10085` | 4 | **0** | 1 |

B matches 10167's feature profile exactly (same string counts, same 1,044,128

Both uploaded and size-verified at 445,382,656 bytes, on separate cards so they
can be compared without reinstalling. Watchers armed for both title IDs.

Test order: **B first** (expect GTA IV to work, confirming the merge is sound),
then **A** (if GTA IV still works, the range-migration removal was safe and can
stay out; if it breaks, the removal was the problem).

#### 2026-10-28 CORRECTION: variants A and B were invalid builds

Both merged variants failed on hardware:

    12:06:09  createApp ORBW00019   (variant B)
    12:06:18  # A user thread receives a fatal signal / signal: 4 (SIGILL)
    12:06:30  createApp GLDS10085   (variant A)
    12:06:34  stops at "wine startup: server_init_process"
    12:06:40  back to the launcher

**Neither result is meaningful — the builds were wrong.** `scripts/prx-ps4.sh`
only *links* PRXs; it reads an already-compiled
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/build/dlls/ntdll/ntdll.so`. That tree was built at
07:25 on 2026-10-28, i.e. **before the merge, from the 1017 patch series**. The
merge changed **105 patch files**, none of which were compiled. So variants A
and B are the merged `pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` grafted onto unmerged Wine C
code, and the SIGILL is the expected consequence of that mismatch.

This also invalidates the earlier `ab-10167-no-exec-migration` SIGILL for the
same reason. The conclusion drawn from it — "the per-page migration is
load-bearing" — is **not supported**; that build had the same glue/Wine
mismatch. It may still be true, but it has not been shown.

Feature-string and byte-size equality with 10167 was **not** sufficient
validation. Both matched exactly and the binary still failed. Do not use string
counts as a proxy for a correct build.

**Build-system facts worth recording:**

- `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` links PRXs from an existing Wine build; it does not
  rebuild Wine.
- `scripts/build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh` packages the title; it also reads
  prebuilt PRXs.
- **No script in `scripts/` or `prospero-win/tools/` applies
  `src/wine-patches-ps4`.** The Wine tree at
  `prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/source` is persistently patched, and rebuilds
  recompile it in place. Incorporating the merged series requires applying the
  new patches to that tree and a full Wine rebuild.
- `PW_WINE64_HEAP_FLEXIBLE` is a compile-time `-D` in
  `scripts/build-app-ps4.sh`, not a runtime switch, so patch 0073's direct-heap
  A/B also needs an app rebuild.

**Consequence for the merge:** commit `acebbeb` is still correct and worth
keeping — it unblocks the app rebuild (`pw_osk_ps4.h`) and puts the Titan Quest
runtime in the branch. But it changes nothing on hardware until Wine is rebuilt
from the merged series. **10167 remains the only validated runtime** and has
been restored to `ORBW00019` (445,382,656 bytes, size-verified).

Local artifacts kept for reference, both known-invalid:
`pkgout/merged-variantA/`, `pkgout/merged-variantB/`,
`pkgout/ab-10167-no-exec-migration/`.

#### 2026-10-28 BREAKTHROUGH: Fallout's crash is flexible-memory FRAGMENTATION

The `flexible_holder` telemetry separates Fallout from the titles that work,
and the discriminator is **mapping count, not bytes**:

    GTA IV  (works) : rank=1  bytes=183328K  count= 451  prot=0x7  anon:000800c3ee64
    Fallout (crash) : rank=1  bytes=154608K  count=4872  prot=0x7  anon:000800c3ee64
    Fallout also    : rank=5  bytes= 20960K  count= 128  prot=0x5  anon:000800c3ee64

Same caller (`0x800c3ee64`, inside `ntdll.prx` segment 0 at
`800b8c000-800c70000`), same protection, *less* total memory — but **10x the
mappings**. Fallout averages 31 KiB per mapping against GTA IV's 406 KiB.

Both titles map almost identical flexible totals (Fallout 476,192K, GTA IV
477,056K), so this is not a volume problem. The flexible pool is being
**shredded** for Fallout, and an allocation then fails despite ~60 MiB
nominally free because the free space is not usable in the shape requested.

**Mechanism.** There are two migration paths, and the checkpoint's own comment
states the split:

    /* Move a contiguous direct-memory span to flexible memory in one operation.
     * The per-page version above is retained only for mixed/sparse mappings where
     * a queried range is not wholly backed by direct memory. */
    static int migrate_exec_range_to_flex(uintptr_t address, size_t bytes)

GTA IV's executable regions are contiguous and wholly direct-backed, so they
take `migrate_exec_range_to_flex` — 451 large mappings. Fallout's are sparse or
mixed, so they fall back to `migrate_exec_page_to_flex` — 4,872 individual
16 KiB mappings.

This accounts for every prior observation:

- perfect correlation with the RX machinery arriving between 10080 and 10091
- Fallout's low `dmem` (131-143 MiB): executable pages are migrated *out* of
  direct memory into flexible
- `flexible_free` draining to 6-9 MiB and then a guest fault on a pointer that
  should be valid — an allocation returned NULL into Gamebryo code that does
  not check it
- why GTA IV and Oblivion tolerate the identical code

**Fix directions, in order of preference:**

1. **Coalesce in the per-page path** — batch adjacent pages that need the same
   protection into one mapping instead of one mapping per 16 KiB granule. This
   attacks fragmentation directly and leaves both paths intact.
2. **Teach `migrate_exec_range_to_flex` to handle sparse ranges** — migrate the
   direct-backed subspans as ranges rather than dropping the whole span to the
   per-page path.
3. Reduce how often migration is needed at all (eager migration at image map
   time, while startup is single-threaded).

**Acceptance criteria for any candidate:** Fallout NV reaches gameplay, and
**Oblivion and GTA IV must not regress** — both have fast reproducers and both
currently work on 10167. The measurable signal is the `flexible_holder rank=1
count` for `anon:000800c3ee64`: it should fall from ~4,872 toward GTA IV's
~451 without the byte total growing.

---

## 2026-10-28 (later): the flexible-fragmentation finding is WITHDRAWN

**Retracted claim.** An earlier section of this handoff reported that Fallout
fragments flexible memory roughly ten times worse than GTA IV — "4,872 mappings
/ 154 MB vs 451 / 183 MB, same RWX protection" — and treated that as the
difference between the crashing and the working title. **That is wrong and must
not be built on.** The numbers `4872` and `451` were never mapping counts. They
were `[PS4 SDL event enter] count=` values taken from
`~/codex_scratch/gta4-ps4/DL-klog-after-close.log` and `EK-run1-klog.log` —
SDL event counters from an unrelated capture, conflated with
`PW_WINE64 flexible_holder` telemetry.

**What the real holder telemetry says.** Comparing the crashing Fallout run with
the working GTA IV run at comparable points:

| source | rank=1 (prot=0x7) | rank=3 (prot=0x5) |
|---|---|---|
| `GLDS10085-1013-continue-runtime-20261027-1920.log` (Fallout, crashes) | 131,056K / **8,191** mappings / `anon:0008009025eb` | 49,632K / 3,102 |
| `run-ab-20261028/GTAIV-10085-pkg1017-run1-runtime.log` (GTA IV, works) | 137,760K / **8,610** mappings / `anon:0008009026fb` | 54,736K / 3,421 |

GTA IV holds **more** flexible bytes in **more** mappings than Fallout, at the
same protections, from the same code region. **Flexible fragmentation does not
distinguish the working title from the crashing one.** Do not re-open it.

### Two reasoning errors behind it, both worth avoiding

1. **`heap_log_fallback` latches.** It opens with
   `if (__atomic_exchange_n(&heap_direct_fallback_logged, 1, __ATOMIC_RELAXED)) return;`
   — so it prints **at most one line per process, ever**, no matter how many
   millions of fallbacks occur. "Zero fallback lines in klog" therefore never
   meant "zero fallbacks". (It does mean zero here: the line is absent from
   every log, and the same `sceKernelDebugOutText` channel carries
   "prefers direct memory" nine times, so the channel works and the latch never
   fired. But that conclusion needs the channel check, not the absence alone.)
2. **A symbolized address is not a provenance proof.** `anon:000800c3ee64`
   resolved to `pw_wine_heap_memalign`, which was read as "the Wine heap mapped
   this". But `heap_map()` maps with `map_protection`, which is
   `CPU_READ_WRITE` (0x3) and is only ever upgraded to `CPU_GPU_READ_WRITE` —
   never `0x7`. A holder at `prot=0x7` cannot have come from `heap_map`. The
   protection contradicted the symbol and the symbol was believed anyway.

### Facts established while chasing it (these do hold)

- `heap_map()` has exactly two unlogged `return NULL` paths: the
  `getenv("WINE_PS5_HEAP_FLEXIBLE")` opt-out and a size-overflow guard that
  cannot fire. The opt-out string is absent from both the 10167 and 10080
  eboots, and `PW_WINE64_HEAP_FLEXIBLE` is a compile-time `-D` defaulting to 0.
- `heap_use_direct_memory()` is called once from `__wine_ps5_dmem_region()`
  when `state > 0`, and its banner appears 9× in klog, so the backing **is**
  registered and `backing.map` is not NULL.
- Therefore every `heap_map` call in these runs succeeded, and heap spans and
  large blocks are **direct** memory at prot 0x3 — not the flexible RWX bulk.
- `PwWineHeapStats` already counted `failures`, but **nothing ever logged it**,
  which is why an allocation failure was never observed either way.
- `log_flexible_holders()` filters on `info.flags & 1`, and bit 0 is
  documented in `wine64_main.c` as *flexible*, so the holder lines really are
  flexible-only. That part of the telemetry is sound.
- In `pw_wine_heap.c`, every **large** block (anything whose
  `bytes + HEADER + sizeof(LargeHeader)` exceeds the 64 KiB top size class)
  gets its **own** page-rounded mapping from `map()`. `pw_wine_heap_memalign`
  reaches that path readily because it calls `allocate(bytes + alignment)`.
  This is a real property of the allocator; it is simply not a Fallout/GTA IV
  discriminator.

### What still stands, narrowly

Fallout's `flexible_free` fell 118,784K → 9,216K across the 45 s before the
11:20:09 fault, while GTA IV and Oblivion sit stably at 48,128K–77,824K. The
exhaustion **curve** is still a real difference. What was never shown — in any
run, at any time — is that **an allocation actually failed**. Only a guest read
fault was ever observed:
`WINE wine: Unhandled page fault on read access to 23326F30 at address 00A3368C (thread 0090)`.

### Instrumentation added to settle it (package 1018)

Ported by hand from `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0071-wine64-report-heap-allocation-failure.patch`,
which no longer applies in either direction (the sources drifted):

- `prospero-win/wine/ps5/pw_wine_heap.h` — `PwWineHeapStats` gains
  `last_failed_bytes`, `last_failure_reason`, `last_map_bytes`,
  `last_map_errno`, `backing_refusals`.
- `prospero-win/wine/ps5/pw_wine_heap.c` — each failure site records a reason
  (1 size-overflow, 2 small_alloc, 3 large map failed, 4 large table full,
  5 calloc overflow, 6 memalign guard, 7 realloc); `map()` counts
  `backing_refusals` every time the direct backing declines, and records
  `errno` when the anonymous fallback itself fails. **`backing_refusals` is the
  counter the latched log could never provide.**
- `src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` — `__wine_ps5_memory_stats` widened from
  8 to 14 counters. (Note: `prospero-win/wine/ps5/pw_wine_dmem_ps5.c` has a
  near-identical function but the PS4 build does **not** compile it —
  `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` compiles the glue. Editing the wrong one changes nothing
  on hardware and breaks `tests/test_pw_wine_dmem_ps5.c`, which asserts `== 8`.)
- `prospero-win/native/wine64_main.c` — new line every 300 ticks:
  `PW_WINE64 heapfail count=N last_bytes=N reason=N heap_anon=N map_bytes=N map_errno=N`

Host unit tests `prospero-win/tests/test_pw_wine_heap.c` pass with the change.

**How to read the result.** If `count=0` holds right up to the guest fault,
memory exhaustion is not the mechanism and the whole memory line of inquiry
should be dropped for Fallout. If `count` climbs, `reason` and `last_bytes` say
which allocator path gave up and how large the request was. `heap_anon` says
separately whether direct memory is being declined at all.

### Build-system corrections

- The 12:11–12:31 Wine rebuild **did** compile the merged series: patches landed
  in the tree at 12:11 (`dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c`, `signal_x86_64.c`,
  `server/`, `ws2_32`, `winevulkan`, `win32u` all re-dated), and only four
  *diagnostic* patches conflicted and were skipped (0975, 0976, 0991, 0994).
- That rebuild reports `make exit=2`. Three targets fail —
  `dlls/nsiproxy.sys/ip.o` (incomplete `struct sockaddr_dl`/`sockaddr_inarp`),
  `dlls/wow64/x86_64-windows/wow64.dll` (`undefined reference to
  __imp_IsBadStringPtrW`), and `server/wineserver` (`undefined symbol: thr_kill2`,
  `pw_wine_thread_set_priority`). **All three are harmless for PRX production
  and pre-existing.** `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` never reads those ELFs: `link_objects()`
  greps the *object list* out of `$work/make.log` and relinks from `.o` files
  with `-Wl,--warn-unresolved-symbols`, supplying the missing symbols from
  `src/native/pw_wine_threads_ps4.c` as a shim. Do not try to "fix" these.
- **`make.log` is an input, not just a record.** It was still the 07:29
  pre-merge copy; `prx-ps4.sh` would have relinked using pre-merge object lists.
  It is now the 12:31 build's log, with the pre-merge copy kept beside it as
  `make.log.premerge-0729`. The object lists for `ntdll.so`, `win32u.so` and
  `wineserver` turned out identical between the two, so nothing earlier was
  invalidated by this — but check it after every rebuild.
- Relink validation now uses **PRX hashes before and after**, since feature
  strings and byte sizes were already shown to match across genuinely different
  builds. `ntdll.prx`: `112da41e…` (pre-merge) → `d7bf7541…` (merged) →
  `537459ab…` (merged + heapfail). Each step changed, as it must.
- One loss to note: the relink overwrote `prx/ntdll.shared.elf`, so the exact
  unstripped symbols for the 10167 runtime are **gone**. Any address from a
  pre-12:35 log can no longer be symbolized against its own binary. Per
  `AGENTS.md`, copy `prx/*.shared.elf` aside with the package next time.

### Package 1018: a self-inflicted stack smash (fixed in 1019)

`GLDS10085` package 1018 crashed with SIGSEGV ~46 s after launch
(12:47:08 launch → 12:47:54 signal 11), before the first 300-tick telemetry
sample, so it produced no `PW_WINE64 memory` or `heapfail` line at all. The user
placed it "right after the spinning dots". Dump retained as
`run-ab-20261028/GLDS10085_1793216874.sorbisdmp` (the watcher beat GoldHEN's
`CleanUpCore:DELETE` at 12:48:02).

**Cause — do not repeat this.** `dlls/ntdll/unix/loader.c:1090`, inside
`pw_report_heap()`, declares its **own** copy of the heap stats struct on the
stack rather than including `pw_wine_heap.h`:

```c
struct pw_heap_stats {
    unsigned long long live, peak_live, mapped, peak_mapped;
    unsigned long long allocs, frees, failures, foreign, large;   /* 9 x u64 = 72 B */
} heap = { 0 };
extern void pw_wine_heap_stats( struct pw_heap_stats *stats );
```

Adding five counters to `PwWineHeapStats` made `pw_wine_heap_stats` write
112 bytes into that 72-byte buffer — a 40-byte stack smash on the loader's
refusal path, which runs during startup. The Wine tree had been compiled at
12:28, before the 12:45 header edit, so the caller and callee disagreed about
the struct's size.

**Rule this establishes:** `PwWineHeapStats` has an **out-of-tree duplicate
declaration** and its layout is effectively frozen. Growing it requires a full
Wine rebuild *and* editing `loader.c`'s private copy in the same change.
Adding a counter is not a safe log-only change. `sizeof(PwWineHeapStats)` must
stay **72**; assert it if in doubt.

**Fix (1019).** The new counters moved into a separate `PwWineHeapFailures`
struct (40 B) with its own `pw_wine_heap_failures()` getter, leaving
`PwWineHeapStats` at 72 B. Both the glue and the heap link into `ntdll.prx`
under `-Bsymbolic`, so the new symbol needs no PRXDESC export. Verified by
compiling a `sizeof` probe, not by inspection. Host
`tests/test_pw_wine_heap.c` passes. `ntdll.prx` `537459ab…` (1018, broken) →
`410fe618…` (1019). Unstripped symbols for this runtime are preserved at
`run-ab-20261028/symbols-1019/` (11 `.shared.elf` files).

**Process note.** 1018 changed two things at once: the merged (never
hardware-validated) runtime *and* new instrumentation, against the A/B rule in
`AGENTS.md`. The stack smash fully explains 1018, so 1019 still carries both.
If 1019 also fails early, isolate the merge next rather than adding anything
further — do not stack a third change on top.

### Packages 1018 and 1019 were BOTH void: `libvulkan.prx` was missing

Caught by the user from the package size alone ("almost 50 MB smaller than the
others"), not by any check of mine. Both packages shipped **without the Vulkan
driver**:

```
                       1018/1019      1017 (known good)   deficit
sce_module             10,599,936     36,504,800          25,904,864
win/                  303,931,329    329,943,622          26,012,293
pkg total             393,609,216    445,644,800          51,773,440
```

Three PRXs were absent — `libvulkan.prx` (24,807,408 B — **all of RADV/Mesa**),
`libfreetype.prx` (913,904 B) and `wineps5.prx` (183,728 B). Each is staged
**twice**, into `sce_module/` and into `win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/`, which
is why one missing set shows up as two ~26 MB deficits.

**Why.** `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` builds only its own 11 modules and cleans just
`prx/stubs`, so it neither creates nor removes these three. They were already
gone from `prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/prx/sce_module/` before this session's
relinks — most plausibly swept by the disk cleanup that reclaimed 142 GB. The
decisive part: **`scripts/build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh` copies whatever PRXs it
finds and does not fail, or warn, when one is absent.** A Vulkan-less package
builds and signs cleanly.

They are produced by separate scripts, none of which `prx-ps4.sh` or the
packager invokes: `scripts/radv-prx-ps4.sh` (libvulkan),
`scripts/freetype-prx-ps4.sh`, `scripts/wineps5-prx-ps4.sh`.

**Restored for 1020** from `pkgout/falloutnv-native-rx-wsi-1017/pkg-stage/`
after confirming they are byte-identical across every retained stage and
untouched by this session's work (RADV/Mesa, FreeType, the printer driver):
`libvulkan` `464eb951682aa5dd899db8c59e155860`,
`libfreetype` `5d9aaaca8b2ded2244423bb9ab918b58`,
`wineps5` `978c8514f500cd762ab23657e510ef7b`.

**Consequence for the 1018 diagnosis.** The `PwWineHeapStats` stack smash
(previous section) is real and confirmed from source, and would crash 1018 on
its own. But 1018 *also* had no Vulkan driver, so it had two independent
fatal defects. Had the smash been fixed alone, the next failure would almost
certainly have been misattributed to the merged runtime. **Do not read 1018's or
1019's behaviour as evidence about anything.**

### Required pre-upload gate (this session's main process lesson)

Verifying payload hashes and feature strings only confirms what was *added*. It
cannot see what should already have been there, and it did not. Before any
upload, check the candidate against the latest known-good package:

1. **Package size vs the known-good baseline.** More than a few hundred KB apart
   needs an explanation before the upload, not after. (1020: 445,513,728 vs
   1017's 445,644,800 = 131,072 B, and the staged trees differ by 76,245 B
   because 1020's eboot is 30,832 B *larger* — accounted for.)
2. **`diff` the staged file lists** both ways; nothing in the known-good stage
   may be absent from the candidate.
3. **Assert the separately-built PRXs exist** in `sce_module/` *and* in
   `win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/`: `libvulkan.prx`, `libfreetype.prx`,
   `wineps5.prx`. The packager will not tell you.
4. Only then the payload hashes and feature strings.

This belongs in `build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh` as a hard failure; until then it
is manual. **Also: `pgrep -f <pattern>` matches the watching shell's own command
line.** A `while pgrep -f build-relocatable; do sleep 5; done` wait loop spun
forever against itself, the queued upload never ran, and a follow-up
`pgrep -f ftp-put.py` check then falsely reported it in progress for ~8 minutes
while `SIZE` correctly returned 0. Confirm a transfer with `ps -eo args` or the
`226` reply, never with a self-matching `pgrep`.

## 2026-10-28 (later still): memory exhaustion RULED OUT for Fallout, by measurement

Package 1020 (merged runtime + heap failure instrumentation, all PRXs present)
reached the menu, the user pressed Continue, and it faulted — with the counters
live. The reading at the fault:

```
13:05:22  memory   flexible_free=10240K dmem=143280K heap=49824K dmem_failures=0
13:05:22  heapfail count=0 last_bytes=0 reason=0 heap_anon=1 map_bytes=0 map_errno=0
13:05:25  WINE wine: Unhandled page fault on read access to 27308454 at address 00E8EEE3 (thread 0080)
```

**No allocation failed — not one — at 10 MB flexible free.** `dmem_failures=0`
throughout. `heap_anon=1` for the whole process: the direct-memory backing
declined exactly once, at the first `map()` before `heap_use_direct_memory()`
registers it, and never again. **The memory-exhaustion hypothesis for Fallout is
dead.** It was never supported by anything but a descending `flexible_free`
curve. Do not revive it without a counter showing a refusal.

`PW_WINE64 mmap=4245 munmap=2 mprotect=0 skipped=579 faults=1 images=65` also
rules out the follow-on idea: the port unmaps essentially nothing, so it is not
pulling guest memory out from under the game.

### What the faults actually are: a bad object pointer, at a different site each run

Both Fallout faults dereference a non-NULL, plausible-looking guest heap pointer.
`FalloutNV.exe` is based at `0x00400000`; `.text` spans VMA `0x401000`–`0xFDE58B`,
so both EIPs are in the game's own code. Disassembled with
`x86_64-w64-mingw32-objdump -d -m i386` against `build-ps4-logs/FalloutNV.exe`
(md5 `49f3078187cb1f868581f7b3ac8c9164`, 16,079,360 B; the second local copy
`8f24a29a…` carries the abandoned null-vtable patch, whose only delta is near VMA
`0x659D31` — far from both fault sites, so the disassembly holds for either):

```
11:20 run — EIP 00A3368C, read 23326F30
  a3368a:  8b 08     mov (%eax),%ecx       ; load object pointer
  a3368c:  8b 11     mov (%ecx),%edx       ; <-- FAULT: read its vtable
  a33692:  8b 52 48  mov 0x48(%edx),%edx
  a33696:  ff d2     call *%edx            ; virtual call

13:05 run — EIP 00E8EEE3, read 27308454
  e8eee0:  56        push %esi
  e8eee1:  8b f1     mov %ecx,%esi         ; esi = this (thiscall)
  e8eee3:  8b 46 34  mov 0x34(%esi),%eax   ; <-- FAULT: this->+0x34, this=0x27308420
```

Allocations all succeed, almost nothing is unmapped, and the faulting site moves
between runs. That is the signature of **memory corruption**, not starvation.

### Live suspect: the exec-range migration (and this names it properly)

The rank-1 flexible holder in the 1020 run is
`anon:000800caf354  152,352K  count=4843  prot=0x7`, and `800caf354` matches the
`exec-range` callers in klog for the same run (`caller=800caf3b7` ×15,
`caller=800caf354` ×2). So the RWX flexible bulk comes from
**`migrate_exec_range_to_flex`**, not the Unix heap — `heap_anon=1` proves the
heap independently. 3,046 `exec-range` calls in this run alone.

(This supersedes the earlier guess that the bulk was `pw_wine_heap_memalign`;
that symbol never fit, because `heap_map` maps at `CPU_READ_WRITE` 0x3 and can
never produce `prot=0x7`.)

**Mechanism.** `migrate_exec_range_to_flex()` snapshots a span, unmaps it,
re-mmaps it, then copies the snapshot back — and there is **no locking anywhere
in `pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`** (only `__atomic` on log counters). Any concurrent
write landing inside that window is silently reverted to stale bytes. A reverted
pointer write corrupts an object reference, and the crash surfaces later wherever
that reference is used — which matches the varying site and the non-NULL garbage.
It is also consistent with Fallout working on the one build that lacks this
machinery.

The earlier "RX/exec-page migration ruled out" note does **not** contradict this:
that probe only showed the path runs without breaking GTA IV and Oblivion, which
migrate far less. It never tested a title doing thousands of migrations.

### A/B package 1021: exec migration disabled, nothing else changed

The gate already existed: `exec_migration_allowed = bytes <= PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES`
(`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c:878`, bound defined at :595, was 16 MiB).
Setting the bound to **0** makes it false for every nonzero request, so
`protect_exec_range()` — and the per-page fallback inside it — never runs, and
`PROT_EXEC` on dmem-backed memory falls back to plain `pw_wine_dmem_protect()`.

Rather than hand-edit the constant (easy to forget to restore, against the
additive rule in `AGENTS.md`), it is now `#ifndef`-guarded and
`scripts/prx-ps4.sh` passes `${PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS:-}` when compiling the glue.
1021 was linked with `PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS="-DPW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES=0"`;
**the default build is unchanged**, so this reverts by simply not setting it.

Verified the define actually reached the compiler, rather than assumed: the glue
compiled with the define is `ac458e7df905058c7a00218827c6ed6e` and without it
`50220e75b81dd8efc88408bac730b358`, and the object linked into 1021's ntdll is
`ac458e7d…` — the OFF build.

**What to expect.** If migration is the corruptor, 1021 reaches gameplay and the
`prot=0x7` rank-1 holder collapses. Runtime confirmation that the gate is off:
every `exec-range build=…` line should read `fallback=skip-large` instead of
`fallback=bounded`. Oblivion and GTA IV must be re-checked on 1021 before this
becomes a fix, since they depend on exec protection working.

## 2026-10-28: why four packages were spent guessing — the guest-fault diagnostics were missing

**The afternoon's real mistake.** The 12:11 patch-apply skipped four patches as
conflicts and I recorded them as "only diagnostics, harmless". Two of them —
and, it turned out, five `kernelbase` patches whose absence I never checked —
are the *only* way this port reports a guest fault's registers. Packages 1020,
1021 and 1022 therefore produced no `PW_EXCEPTION32` records at all, and four
builds were spent inferring from klog what those records state directly.

**`PW_EXCEPTION32` is what "decoded a dump" has always meant here**, not the
container. `dlls/kernelbase/debug.c:531` `log_exception_context32()` (called at
:692) emits, on a guest fault:

```
PW_EXCEPTION32 tid=0090 eip=00a3368c esp=17e5f7bc ebp=082c42a0 eax=08e77c50 ebx=00000028 ecx=23326f30 ...
PW_EXCEPTION32 stack bytes=32 words=...
PW_EXCEPTION32 pc-page eip=00a3368c base=00A33000 alloc=00400000 size=5947392 state=00001000
PW_EXCEPTION32 pc-bytes eip=00a3368c words=448b118b,528b2424
```

In the 11:20 run `ecx` held the faulting pointer outright. Sources:
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0962`-`0965`, `0973`.

**Why it was absent.** The code is `#ifdef __i386__`, so it compiles only into
the **32-bit** `kernelbase.dll`. The tree *did* build an instrumented one at
12:28 — `build/dlls/kernelbase/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll`, 4,830,783 B, **21**
`PW_EXCEPTION32` strings — but the packager ships a pinned host Wine PE set
instead, and that copy is 1,273,870 B with **zero**. It was byte-identical
(`502c01b3e4f3a51946cfd150b980ed9c`) in 1017, 1020 and 1022, so *no* package
today had guest-fault diagnostics. Substituting it is an existing, supported
input: `PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL` (`scripts/build-app-ps4.sh:322`, whose own comment
reads "Optional i386 Wine build containing bounded fault-time exception
context").

**Mandatory pre-flight, now also in `AGENTS.md`:** before investigating any
guest fault, check
`strings <stage>/win/wine/lib/wine/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll | grep -c PW_EXCEPTION32`.
Zero means the package cannot report a guest fault; rebuild and pass
`PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL` rather than inferring from klog.

### The `.sorbisdmp` container is encrypted — stop planning around decrypting it

Measured on `GLDS10085_1793220239.sorbisdmp` (306,514 B, retained with full
provenance at `~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/dump-capture-1020/`):
64-byte plaintext header (`00 00` + `FACECS` at +2, u64 version 2, two
(offset,size) u64 pairs `0x130`/`0x4AB12` and `0x130`/`0x4AA32`), then a body
from `0x58` with **Shannon entropy 7.9994 bits/byte**, all 256 values present,
0.42% zeros, and only 74 ASCII runs >=8 in 306 KB. Nothing decompresses as
raw/zlib/gzip anywhere in the first 32 KiB. That is ciphertext, matching
fail0verflow's note that dumps are encrypted from ~FW 4.50. SocraticBliss's
public decryptor targets `orbiscore-systemcrash.orbisstate` (body at `0x4000`,
AES-CBC) and needs per-console sealed keys that are not published and not on
this workstation. Keep dumps for provenance; do not build an investigation on
decrypting one. Full detail is in `AGENTS.md`.

### Fallout fault inventory (all from klog alone, hence the thin conclusions)

| run | build | fault | EIP | reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11:20 | 1017 pre-merge | read `23326F30` | `00A3368C` | `mov (%ecx),%edx` then `call *0x48(%edx)` — vtable load, **`ecx` confirmed by PW_EXCEPTION32** |
| 13:05 | 1020 range | read `27308454` | `00E8EEE3` | `mov 0x34(%esi),%eax` — `this` field, `this=0x27308420` |
| 13:15 | 1021 no migration | read `004A4A40` | `004A4A40` | **equal ⇒ instruction fetch**; `.text` never became executable |
| 13:32 | 1022 per-page | read `00000098` | `00A86579` | `mov (%edx,%edi,4),%ecx` then `fcomps 0x98(%ecx)` — **NULL** in a pointer array |
| 13:43 | 1020 rerun | read `1E125690` | `00C7E3F1` | `call 0xc802d0` then `mov 0x8(%eax),%esi` — **callee returned** `0x1E125688`; the game *does* null-check at `c7e3e5`, so this is a bad non-NULL return, not a missing check |

Game PE `build-ps4-logs/FalloutNV.exe` md5 `49f3078187cb1f868581f7b3ac8c9164`,
base `0x00400000`, `.text` VMA `0x401000`-`0xFDE58B`.

**The 1020 rerun also showed the crash is not deterministic**: same binary, same
save, it passed `minflt=120,900` having faulted at `minflt≈113,839` the run
before. Timing-sensitive, not fixed bad data in the save.

### Settled, and not settled

Settled by measurement:
- **Memory exhaustion is not the mechanism.** `heapfail count=0`,
  `dmem_failures=0`, `heap_anon=1` across three builds down to 10 MB free.
- **Exec migration is mandatory.** Disabling it kills the game at a prologue.
- `_exit()` does not terminate on PS4, so `abort_process` falls into the
  compiler's `ud2`. Real defect, but **teardown only** — the 8 SIGILLs/run land
  0.6-0.8 s *after* the fault, so they are a consequence, not a cause. (I
  initially read them as the cause; the timestamps disproved it.)

Not supported, dropped:
- Heap fragmentation (withdrawn earlier; the numbers were SDL event counts).
- The unlock-window race: per-page shrank the window ~1000x (16 MiB -> 16 KiB,
  4,177 distinct pages, RX verified `effective=0x5`) and the crash persisted
  with a *changed* signature. Cost +70 s to first frame (43.5 s -> 113.9 s) and
  ~300 migrations/s sustained, bursting on carousel transitions.

### A/B gates added (both default-off and provably zero-impact)

`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` now has `#ifndef`-guarded
`PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES` (0 disables migration) and
`PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_PER_PAGE_ONLY` (1 forces the per-page loop), and
`scripts/prx-ps4.sh` passes `${PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS:-}` when compiling the glue.
Proof they are additive: relinking with no flags reproduced `ntdll.prx`
`410fe618d48b9fdf4e1f1e3af7a92cff` — byte-identical to 1020 — and the glue
object matched the migration-ON reference `50220e75b81dd8efc88408bac730b358`.

### Package 1023

1020's runtime bit-for-bit (`ntdll.prx` `410fe618`) plus the instrumented i386
`kernelbase.dll` via `PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL`. Expect the package ~3.5 MB larger
than 1020's 445,513,728 B, because the instrumented DLL is 4,830,783 B against
the pinned 1,273,870 B — that growth is **expected**, not a missing-file
regression. Symbols at `run-ab-20261028/symbols-1023/` including the DLL itself.

## 2026-10-28 final: mprotect-cache hypothesis REJECTED; crash depth is constant

Package 1026 distrusted Wine's host protection cache
(`PW_PS4_MPROTECT_TRUST_CACHE=0` via marker file). The switch was confirmed
active at runtime — `PW_WINE64 mprotect_trust_cache=0 (marker ...)` — and the
counters prove the behaviour changed completely:

| build | `mprotect` issued | `skipped` |
|---|---|---|
| 1024 (cache trusted) | **0** | **579** |
| 1026 (cache distrusted) | **586** | **0** |

**It did not fix the crash, and did not change how far the game gets.**

```
run       depth at fault
1020-r1   minflt=134024  inblock=6939
1020-r2   minflt=134549  inblock=7208
1023      minflt=133317  inblock=6765
1024      minflt=133504  inblock=6707
1026      minflt=133387  inblock=6662
```

**Correction to earlier notes in this handoff:** remarks that a run had "passed
1020's fault point of minflt≈113,839" were wrong — that figure was an
intermediate sample, not the value at the fault. Every run faults at
**minflt ≈ 133.3k–134.5k, within 1%**. Nothing tried today got further than
anything else.

**This changes the character of the problem.** The crash depth is near-constant
while the faulting instruction varies. That is a specific stage of the save load
failing, not timing-random corruption — and it undercuts the reasoning behind
the window-race and cache hypotheses, both of which predicted variable progress.

### 1026 fault, for the record

```
fault=08058598 base=08058000 alloc=023B0000 size=32768 state=COMMIT protect=RW type=PRIVATE
eax=0805834c edx=00000093(147)   pc: 8b 0c 90  mov (%eax,%edx,4),%ecx   (eax+147*4 = fault)
eax-data field10=00000500 field40=00030410 field44=00130800   <-- REAL data, not zeros
object addr=08056e0c vtable=010a464c refs=00000000             <-- refcount ZERO
frames  00b452a0 -> 00afc1f9 -> 00afc673 -> 00aa1f4c
```

Differs from the 14:38 fault in three ways: the bad address is inside the same
allocation as the object (`alloc=023B0000`) rather than in the far `0x2F`/`0x32`
million region; `eax-data` read real contents instead of zeros; and the object's
refcount is 0. There may be two distinct failures here.

### Hypotheses tested and rejected today (do not re-run these)

1. **Heap fragmentation** — withdrawn; the 4,872/451 figures were SDL event
   counts from an unrelated log.
2. **Unix-heap / dmem exhaustion** — `heapfail count=0`, `dmem_failures=0`,
   `heap_anon=1` down to 10 MB free, every build. (Note the scope: this covers
   `pw_wine_heap` only, **not** the guest's Win32 allocator.)
3. **Exec-migration unlock window** — per-page shrank it ~1000x (16 MiB -> 16 KiB,
   4,177 distinct pages, RX verified `effective=0x5`); crash persisted, +70 s
   startup. Disabling migration entirely kills the game at a prologue, so it is
   mandatory.
4. **`abort_process` SIGILL** — teardown artifact; the 8 per run land 0.6-0.8 s
   *after* the fault.
5. **Stale `host_prot` cache** — this section.

### Unexplained and still worth pursuing

- **The 14:38 fault remains a genuine anomaly**: `fault=2F14827C` with
  `state=MEM_COMMIT`, `protect=PAGE_READWRITE`, `ReadProcessMemory` returning
  **72 bytes of zeros**, faulting on a bare `mov (%eax),%eax`. Committed,
  readable via the slow path, unreadable by the CPU. Not explained by anything
  ruled out above.
- **`mprotect=0 skipped=579`** — in every build before 1026, Wine issued *zero*
  host protection changes all run. A real defect independent of this crash.
- **The guest Win32 allocator has no failure instrumentation at all.** The
  faulting addresses live in guest private memory (`alloc=023B0000`,
  `0x2F000000`). `heapfail` does not cover it.

### Permanent gains from today (keep these regardless)

- **Guest-fault diagnostics restored** — `PW_EXCEPTION32` + the new `fault-page`
  record, via `PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL`. Pre-flight check now mandated in
  `AGENTS.md`.
- **`.sorbisdmp` format documented** as encrypted, with measurements, in
  `AGENTS.md`; stop planning work around decrypting it.
- **Two additive A/B gates** in the glue (`PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES`,
  `PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_PER_PAGE_ONLY`) plus `PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS` in
  `prx-ps4.sh`, proven zero-impact at default (relink reproduced `ntdll.prx`
  `410fe618…` byte-identically).
- **Marker-file toggle pattern** for ntdll-side switches, since ntdll has a
  private environment and `radv.env` never reaches it. It logs its own state,
  which is how 1025's unreachable gate would have been caught immediately.
- **Pre-upload gate** (size vs baseline, file-list diff, separately-built PRXs,
  payload hashes, diagnostics present) — caught nothing further after it was
  adopted, having missed a Vulkan-less package twice before it existed.

### Recommended next step

Stop hypothesis-driven A/Bs. Two systematic options, in order of expected value:

1. **Bisect commit `022241d` (2026-10-20)**, which introduced the exec migration
   into an otherwise-working Oct 15 baseline. That commit is the known boundary
   between "gets into the game" and "does not"; it contains more than the
   migration, and the migration itself is required, so the breaking sub-change
   may be elsewhere in it.
2. **Instrument the guest Win32 allocator** (`NtAllocateVirtualMemory` /
   commit path) the way `pw_wine_heap` now is. Every faulting address today was
   guest private memory, and that allocator is the one subsystem never measured.

## 2026-10-28 BREAKTHROUGH: the faulting page is NOT PRESENT, and the migration's mmap is failing EINVAL

Package 1030 added `PW_SEGV_ENTRY`, a bounded trace of every signal reaching
`segv_handler` with the raw trap and error code. The Fallout fault:

```
ntdll PW_SEGV_ENTRY n=10 sig=11 trap=12 err=0x4 si_addr=272f9af4 rip=e7230e
WINE  Unhandled page fault on read access to 272F9AF4 at address 00E7230E
PW_EXCEPTION32 fault-page fault=272F9AF4 base=272F9000 alloc=27000000 size=12288
               state=00001000 protect=00000004 type=00020000
```

**`err=0x4` decodes as bit0(P)=0, bit1(W/R)=0, bit2(U/S)=1 — the page is NOT
PRESENT.** Not a protection violation. The decoding self-validates in the same
run: exec faults read `err=0x15` (P=1 protection + I/D=1 fetch) and a teardown
write read `err=0x6` (P=0 + W=1).

**This kills every protection-based hypothesis at once**, and explains why they
all failed on hardware: distrusting the host_prot cache (1026, all 586 mprotects
forced, 0 skipped) could not help because protection was never wrong — the
mapping is *absent*, while Wine's bookkeeping reports `MEM_COMMIT` /
`PAGE_READWRITE`.

### Why the page is absent

```
flex-migrate complete        2023
flex-migrate map failed       215   <-- all errno=22 (EINVAL), addr=ffffffffffffffff
flex-migrate scratch failed    46   <-- also EINVAL
release / adopt failed          0
```

All 215 `map failed` fall in the single minute of the crash, at
`available=64512K` free flexible and `flexible_holder rank=1 count=4767`
mappings. EINVAL with 63 MB free points at a kernel VM-entry limit, not memory
pressure — and the *scratch* mmap failing too (`mmap(NULL, 131072, RW,
PRIVATE|ANON)`) shows it is not specific to `MAP_FIXED`.

`migrate_exec_range_to_flex()` releases before it maps:

```c
release_rc = pw_wine_dmem_replace(&dmem, address, bytes, 0);   /* range UNMAPPED */
mapped = mmap((void *)address, bytes, PROT_READ|PROT_WRITE,
              MAP_FIXED|MAP_PRIVATE|MAP_ANON, -1, 0);          /* <-- EINVAL, 215x */
```

so each failure leaves a range that is unmapped in hardware and committed in
Wine. The faulting page is exactly such a hole — the migration walked the
allocation in 16 KiB steps and stopped one page short of it:

```
15:35:19.172  flex-migrate complete addr=272f0000 bytes=16384
15:35:19.172  flex-migrate complete addr=272f4000 bytes=16384
              (272f8000, containing 0x272f9af4, never migrated)
15:35:24.217  Unhandled page fault on read access to 272F9AF4
15:35:25.964  exec-range verify addr=272f8000 migrate=-1 protect=-1 effective=0x3
```

`migrate=-1` on that page confirms its migration failed.

### What this retroactively explains

- **Per-page migration (1022) made it worse**: it creates ~1000x more mappings,
  so it hits the VM-entry limit harder. It crashed earlier and differently.
- **Disabling migration (1021)** removed the holes but lost RX, dying at a
  prologue.
- **The constant ~133.5k minflt fault depth**: the failures begin when the
  mapping count crosses the limit, which happens at a reproducible point in the
  save load.
- The early "fragmentation" instinct was right in *substance* — mapping count,
  not byte count — even though the numbers first cited for it were bogus SDL
  event counters from an unrelated log.

### Fix, in two parts

1. **Never leave a hole (correctness).** The failure path attempts
   `pw_wine_dmem_replace(address, bytes, READ|WRITE)` and a `memcpy` restore,
   but **nothing logs whether that restore succeeded**, and a hole only persists
   if it did not. Instrument the restore result first, then make the path safe:
   either confirm a replacement mapping is obtainable before releasing, or treat
   a failed restore as fatal and loud rather than silently leaving committed
   memory unmapped.
2. **Stop hitting EINVAL (root cause).** 2,023 migrations produce thousands of
   individual flexible mappings; once the kernel's VM-entry table fills, mmap
   returns EINVAL regardless of free memory. Coalesce migrations, skip ranges
   already flexible, or reuse a pool so the mapping count stays bounded.

Part 1 stops the crash; part 2 stops the condition that triggers it. Do part 1
first — it is small, and it converts a silent memory-corruption bug into a
diagnosable failure.

## 2026-10-28: upstream sync lands, but package 1033 breaks every title

### The regression, precisely

Every title dies a few seconds after the profile loads, before `__wine_main`:

```
PW_WINE64 ntdll=/app0/win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/ntdll.prx prefix=... exe=C:\Games\GTAIV\GTAIV.exe
PW_WINE64 environment status=0
PW_WINE64 run status=0
PW_WINE64 thread entered, calling __wine_main
WINE wine: cannot get path to ntdll.so (set WINE_PS5_NTDLL_DIR)
```

GTA IV, Fallout and Pinball all fail identically, so it is one systemic bug,
not three. **Package 1032 is unaffected and remains installable.**

`dlls/ntdll/unix/loader.c:381 init_paths()` tries `dladdr()` first and falls
back to `getenv("WINE_PS5_NTDLL_DIR")`; on PS4 both now fail.

### Ruled out

- The title does set it: `ops->set_env` at `src/pw_wine_start.c:80`, and
  `PW_WINE64 environment status=0` is logged.
- `ntdll_dir` is populated identically to the pre-sync build
  (`wine64_main.c` lines ~1020/1261 vs ~1050/1247 before) -- same code.
- `set_env()` is byte-identical in both (`setenv(name, value, 1)`).
- `pw_prx_env_ps4.o` is still linked into ntdll (`prx-ps4.sh:207`), and both
  the working 1032 and broken 1033 `ntdll.prx` contain `setenv`, `environ`
  and `pw_ps4_setenv` strings in equal numbers.

So the mechanism is present on both sides; what broke is the **binding** --
the title's `setenv` reaching the publishing copy inside `ntdll.prx`. That
copy exists because this console's `setenv` reports success and never
publishes `environ` (see the header comment in `src/native/pw_prx_env_ps4.c`).

### Dead end, for the record

Calling `pw_ps4_setenv` directly from `wine64_main.c`'s `set_env()` does not
link: `orbis-ld: error: undefined symbol: pw_ps4_setenv`. It is in ntdll's
PRXDESC export list (`prx-ps4.sh:80`) but the title resolves ntdll symbols at
runtime, not link time, so a direct call needs `sceKernelDlsym` against the
already-loaded module handle (the title logs `load status=0 ... module=0x7f`).
That is the obvious next thing to try.

### Where to look next

Why the title's `setenv` no longer binds to ntdll's. Candidates, in order:
1. `prx-ps4.sh` links `pw_wine_heap_libc.o` and `pw_wine_dl_libc.o` into every
   module except ntdll and wineserver, to keep one heap and one dl registry.
   If upstream's ntdll now pulls its own libc copy, the title's `setenv` and
   ntdll's `getenv` are looking at different `environ`.
2. Resolve `pw_ps4_setenv` through `sceKernelDlsym` in the title and call it
   for every Wine variable, which removes the dependence on symbol binding
   altogether.

### What the sync did deliver

- **Upstream's OSK works on PS4**, which ours never did:
  `PW_WINE64 osk preload libSceImeDialog id=0x96 status=0 handle=126` and
  `libSceImeDialog.sprx` loads. It compiled unmodified -- the PS4 SDK has
  `sceImeDialog*` and `sceCommonDialogInitialize`. Trigger is a two-finger
  touchpad click (upstream d5a1601). `native/pw_osk_ps5.c` had to be added to
  the source list in `scripts/build-app-ps4.sh`.
- `cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, zero errors to the launcher, OSK preload at no
  memory cost (`flexible_free=448512K->448512K`).

### Six PS4 adaptations the sync needed (all committed)

1. `pw_name_size` needs `PW_PS4_DNS` beside `__GLIBC__` -- PS4 declares
   `getnameinfo` with `socklen_t` ("conflicting types").
2. `pw_ps4_gethostbyname` delegating to upstream's `gethostbyname`; our ws2_32
   unixlib patch calls it by name ("missing library for symbol"). Upstream's
   `getaddrinfo` now drives `sceNetResolverCreate`/`StartNtoa`/`StartNtoa6`
   with IPv6 ours never had, so our PS4 DNS implementation is superseded.
3. `<sys/mman.h>` and `<errno.h>` in `wine/wow64native/unix.c` -- not reached
   transitively through the PS4 SDK headers as they are on PS5.
4. `getdents` redeclaration guarded by `__PS4__` -- the PS4 `<dirent.h>`
   already declares it with a different signature. The toolchain defines
   `__PS4__`, `__ORBIS__` **and** `__PROSPERO__`, which is why PS5 code paths
   are selected here at all.
5. `pw_qpc_clock.h`, `pw_title_menu.h`, `pw_key_shared.h` staged into the Wine
   tree (`build_wine_ps5.sh:260-264`); incremental patching skips this and
   upstream patch 0900 fails with "'../pw_qpc_clock.h' file not found".
6. `native/pw_osk_ps5.c` added to `scripts/build-app-ps4.sh`.

### Build rules learned (do not rediscover)

- **Wine must be built with `make -k`.** `dlls/nsiproxy.sys/ip.o` fails
  permanently (incomplete `struct sockaddr_dl`); without `-k` it aborts the
  run and silently leaves `win32u.so`/`ws2_32.so` at their previous build
  while `ntdll.so` looks updated.
- `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` reads object lists from `.deps/wine-ps5/make.log`. A
  target built in a separate `make` invocation has no link line there, so the
  logs must be concatenated or the relink reports "no ELF link in make.log".
- `tools/build_wine_ps5.sh` is **not** our build path: it wants the PS5
  boilerplate and payload SDK, which this PS4 port does not have. The tree at
  `.deps/wine-ps5/build` is already configured and is rebuilt with `make`.

### State

- `prospero-win`: `ac2334c` merge, `1fa53b8` PS4 fixes, 0 behind origin/main.
- Local work preserved at `8471db8`, tagged `known-good-2026-10-28-1032`.
- Wine tree backed up: 94 MB tarball + complete 16,656-line diff + base
  `490f6d5dcb`, at `~/codex_scratch/wine-tree-backup-20261028/`.
- 14 upstream patches still unapplied, including `0974` (host-page protection)
  and `0604` (commit semantics) -- the two most relevant to the Fallout fault.

## 2026-10-28 evening: the sync, and what "additive" actually means here

Upstream merged (265 commits). Local work preserved at `8471db8`, tagged
`known-good-2026-10-28-1032`; Wine tree backed up (94 MB tar + 16,656-line
diff + base `490f6d5dcb`) at `~/codex_scratch/wine-tree-backup-20261028/`.

### The central lesson

Resolving every conflict in upstream's favour and re-adding our changes
afterwards was **wrong for the PS4 companion layer**. `wine/ps5/*`,
`wine/wow64native/*`, `wine/wowprospero/*` and `src/pw_x86_*` are not shared
code with a PS4 flavour -- they are PS4 companions. Measured against the merge
base, upstream changed **12 of the 16** of them **not at all**:

```
unchanged upstream: pw_wine_compat_libc.c pw_wine_cwd.c pw_wine_cwd_libc.c
                    pw_wine_dl_libc.c pw_wine_threads.c wineps5/unix.c
                    wowprospero/{cpu,unix}.c thread_budget.h
                    pw_x86_engine.{c,h} pw_x86_block.c
changed upstream:   wow64native/unix.c +1138, cpu.c +141, wow64native.h +113,
                    src/pw_x86_hostexec.c +192
```

**Default for that layer is ours**, with upstream merged in deliberately. The
three `wow64native` files cannot take upstream's work at all (see ABI below).
`pw_x86_hostexec.c` (+192) is a genuine loss worth revisiting; it affects the
translator path only.

### Three regressions the sync caused, in the order they surfaced

**1. Every title died at `init_paths`** -- `cannot get path to ntdll.so (set
WINE_PS5_NTDLL_DIR)`. The title sets it with plain `setenv` and relied on that
symbol *binding* to ntdll.prx's publishing copy (this console's `setenv`
reports success but never publishes `environ`; hence
`src/native/pw_prx_env_ps4.c`). The sync moved that binding.
**Fix:** `src/pw_wine_start.c` resolves `pw_ps4_setenv` from the PRX
descriptor via `pw_prx_lookup` -- the mechanism already used for
`__wine_main` etc. -- and calls it for every variable. A direct call does not
link (`undefined symbol: pw_ps4_setenv`): the title resolves ntdll symbols at
runtime, not link time.

**2. `wow64native` ABI mismatch** -- `native diag: unix init status=c000000d
ready=0 fs_set=0`, then `prepare_simulate terminating`. `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER`
from the first check in `process_init`: `params->version != PW_NATIVE_ABI_VERSION`.
Upstream bumped the header to **4**; the PS4 PE side is pinned at **3**.
`scripts/build-app-ps4.sh:263` says it outright -- *"The PS4 Unix companion uses
ABI v3. The host install tree also contains an upstream ABI-v4 wow64native.dll,
which must never enter a PS4 package"* -- and SHA-pins it
(`d7029105cae5d9ba...`). Taking upstream's `wow64native.h` broke the halves
apart. **Fix:** restore `wine/wow64native/*` from `8471db8` (ABI v3). This also
explains GTA's `SIGSYS code=65542`: downstream of the same failure.

**3. Profiles refused** -- `status=unsupported`, `apps=6`. Our profiles use
`keyboard_stick`, `stick_deadzone`, `native_profile`, `dxvk_config`,
`nglide_resolution`; upstream's parser rejects unknown keys, and upstream
independently added `shared_input`/`fast_clock`. The two parsers diverged.
**Interim fix:** upstream's parser kept, those five keys stripped from the
profile data; originals in `~/codex_scratch/profiles-backup-20261028/`.
**⚠ Profiles live on the console at `/data/prospero-win/profiles/`
(`PW_WINE64_ROOT_DATA`), not in the package** -- editing `src/profiles/` and
rebuilding changes nothing until they are uploaded. After uploading:
`apps=17`, one refusal left (`fallout-3-gog.profile`, out of scope today).

### Where it stands (package 1036)

Backend now initialises fully:

```
thread_init enter version=3 expected=3
thread_init host-fs status=00000000 base=880dacc60
thread_init ready status=00000000 ready=1
```

Next failure, further along:

```
wine: failed to create main module for L"C:\Games\OblivionGOG\Oblivion.exe", status c0000018
wine: could not load kernel32.dll, status c000001d
```

`c0000018` = **STATUS_CONFLICTING_ADDRESSES** -- the 32-bit image cannot be
mapped at its base. Likely an address-space layout collision: upstream's
`0982-ntdll-ps5-high-area-as-large-as-the-kernel-grants.patch` is one of the 14
that **failed to apply**, while our `1003-ntdll-map-low-guest-fault` and
`1005-ntdll-map-full-32bit-guest-fault` still map guest ranges. Start there.

### Won from the sync

- **Upstream's OSK works on PS4**, which ours never did:
  `osk preload libSceImeDialog id=0x96 status=0 handle=126`, `.sprx` loads, no
  memory cost. Unmodified upstream code; the PS4 SDK has `sceImeDialog*` and
  `sceCommonDialogInitialize`. Needed `native/pw_osk_ps5.c` added to
  `scripts/build-app-ps4.sh`. Trigger: two-finger touchpad click (d5a1601).
- Upstream's `getaddrinfo` now drives `sceNetResolverCreate`/`StartNtoa`/
  `StartNtoa6` with **IPv6** ours lacked, so our PS4 DNS is superseded;
  `pw_ps4_gethostbyname` just delegates.

### Build rules (do not rediscover)

- **`make -k` is mandatory.** `dlls/nsiproxy.sys/ip.o` fails permanently
  (incomplete `struct sockaddr_dl`); without `-k` it aborts the run and leaves
  `win32u.so`/`ws2_32.so` stale while `ntdll.so` looks updated.
- `prx-ps4.sh` reads object lists from `.deps/wine-ps5/make.log`; a target built
  in a separate `make` has no link line there, so logs must be concatenated.
- `tools/build_wine_ps5.sh` is **not** our build path (wants PS5 boilerplate and
  payload SDK). The tree is already configured; rebuild with `make`.
- It stages `pw_qpc_clock.h`, `pw_title_menu.h`, `pw_key_shared.h` into the Wine
  tree (lines 260-264); incremental patching skips this and upstream 0900 fails
  with "'../pw_qpc_clock.h' file not found".
- The toolchain defines `__PS4__`, `__ORBIS__` **and** `__PROSPERO__`, which is
  why PS5 code paths are selected on PS4 at all.

### 2026-10-28 RESULT: GTA IV reaches gameplay on the synced upstream base

Package 1036 (`falloutnv-sync-1036.pkg`), user-confirmed **into the game with
working controls**:

```
tick=13800  last=1920x1080  cpu_ms=687198  audio=36557  audible=19343
Unhandled page fault / RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL / SIGSYS : 0
```

`audible=19343` is real sound output, not a ticking callback. This validates the
265-commit sync for the Vulkan path and satisfies the "must not break GTA IV"
constraint. Note it also passed the point where package 1017 died
(`RADV_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_FAIL stage=stage_array_alloc` at pipeline #642) with
zero pipeline failures.

**Counter caveat, again:** `frames_put` read 6-7 the whole time, including
in-game. It is unreliable and must not be read as a stall -- the handoff
already recorded it reading `5/0` during actual gameplay on GLDS10080, and it
was misread as a stall once more today. Upstream's `alive` line has also
**dropped `minflt`/`majflt`/`inblock`**, which were the only reliable
loading-vs-stalled discriminator. Restoring them as a PS4 addition is worth
doing before the next stall investigation.

### Oblivion: a relocation conflict, not a general sync regression

```
GTAIV.exe     at 00D90000   relocated, works
FalloutNV.exe at 00400000   preferred base
Oblivion.exe  -> c0000018   STATUS_CONFLICTING_ADDRESSES
```

GTA IV was **relocated away from its preferred base and still loaded**, so the
loader works; Oblivion cannot be relocated and must have `0x400000`. In
`dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c:1129`, when `preload_info` is absent Wine reserves
`0x00010000-0x40000000` wholesale -- a range that contains Oblivion's base:

```c
else reserve_area( (void *)0x00010000, (void *)0x40000000 );
```

So the question is why `preload_info` is now absent (or why the range is not
released for the main image) on the synced build. Related and still unapplied:
upstream's `0982-ntdll-ps5-high-area-as-large-as-the-kernel-grants.patch` is one
of the 14 that failed to apply, while our `1003-ntdll-map-low-guest-fault` and
`1005-ntdll-map-full-32bit-guest-fault` still map guest ranges. Start there, and
check whether Oblivion.exe carries a `.reloc` section at all (no local copy on
this workstation; it is console-side).

### Open, in priority order

1. Oblivion's `c0000018` (above).
2. The 14 unapplied upstream patches, notably `0974` (fault on protected pages
   sharing a host page) and `0604` (commit stacks as Windows does) -- both
   directly relevant to the unresolved Fallout `kprot=0` fault.
3. Restore the `keyboard_stick`/`stick_deadzone` profile feature by merging our
   parser keys with upstream's `shared_input`/`fast_clock`, rather than the
   interim strip. Originals: `~/codex_scratch/profiles-backup-20261028/`.
4. Restore `minflt`/`inblock` to the `alive` line.
5. `src/pw_x86_hostexec.c` -- upstream's +192 lines were discarded with our
   restore; translator path only.

### 2026-10-28 late: package 1040, and where Oblivion actually fails

1040 = 1036 plus six changes. Five upstream patches that had failed during the
sync went in once the earlier ones had landed and moved their context:

```
0602  map unaligned shared sections private   (16 KiB host page vs 4 KiB section)
0604  commit stacks as Windows does, demand-commit on fault
0903  keep the FS base of a resumed 64-bit thread
0971  no debugger start in a title
0982  high area as large as the kernel grants
0026  sceGnmSubmitDone()  -- the close fix, from prospero-win-patches-ps4
```

`0603-ntdll-report-the-ps5-title-s-memory` was **reverted**: it fails to link
with `undefined symbol: __wine_ps5_memory_budget`, which needs PS4 glue we do
not have. Reporting only; nothing lost.

**GTA IV is unaffected by all six** -- `tick=7080 last=1920x1080 audible=1366`,
zero faults, SIGSYS, RADV failures or image conflicts. `PW_WINE64 gnm
submit_done rc=0` confirms the close fix is live.

### Oblivion: the failure is PE-side, not in the unix mapper

Still `c0000018` on 1040, so `0602`/`0982` did not fix it. The
`PW_IMG conflict why=...` trace added to `dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c`
(`map_fixed_area` alignment / `find_view_range` / `limit_low` / mmap EEXIST)
**never fired**, which is itself the finding: the failure does not come from
the unix mapper at all.

`wine: failed to create main module` is emitted by **PE-side**
`dlls/ntdll/loader.c:2927`, from `build_module()` at :2919 (or
`convert_to_pe64` / `RtlDosPathNameToNtPathName_U_WithStatus` just above it).
That is `i386-windows/ntdll.dll`, a different binary from the `ntdll.so` the
trace went into. Instrument there next, or trace
`NtMapViewOfSection`'s status on the main image.

Still true and worth keeping in view: GTA IV **relocated** to `0x00D90000` and
runs, so the loader's relocation machinery works; Oblivion needs its preferred
`0x400000`.

### Instrumentation gap to close first

Upstream's `alive` line **no longer carries `minflt`/`majflt`/`inblock`**. Those
were the only reliable loading-vs-stalled discriminator, and without them a
slow load and a hang are indistinguishable -- which came up directly today when
GTA IV's loading screens felt slow and nothing could confirm either way. The
audio counter is useless for this: it ticks at the same rate (11,036 vs
11,041/min) whether or not the game is progressing.

Also note `cpu_ms` is **not** a progress metric. Less CPU at the same tick can
mean more blocking, not more efficiency; it was misread that way today.

Restore `minflt`/`inblock` to the `alive` line as a PS4 addition before the
next Oblivion or Fallout run.

### 2026-10-28 final run: 1040 results on Fallout

**0604 works, but does not fix the Fallout crash.** Demand-commit handled 34
faults during startup -- every one a thread-stack growth (`rip=7bf2e493`,
different `ptid` each time, ~1 MiB ranges) -- against the flat `faults=1` seen
all day. Then fault 35 killed it at the same point as always:

```
Unhandled page fault on write access to 3004E874 at address 7BE8FC5D (thread 0024)
PW_SEGV_ENTRY n=35 err=0x6 si_addr=3004e874 rip=7be8fc5d
              kq=0 kstart=3004c000 kend=30050000 kprot=0 ktype=0 kflags=0
```

Same core signature (write, page-not-present, kernel says unbacked), but
`rip=7be8fc5d` is **Wine's own code**, not the guest -- Wine writing to guest
memory it believes is committed. The region is 16 KiB and is not a stack, which
is why 0604's demand-commit did not cover it.

**0982 is a regression.** `FalloutNV.exe` loaded at `00400000` on **19
consecutive runs** from 09:00 to 16:01, and at `00530000` for the first time on
1040. The `PW_IMG conflict why=mmap-eexist base=400000 size=1009000` trace shows
why: `mmap` returns EEXIST while Wine's own `find_view_range()` believes the
range is free, so something maps `0x400000` outside Wine's view tracking.
Fallout survives by relocating; **Oblivion cannot relocate and dies**, which is
the user's original diagnosis and is now evidenced.

**0604 also costs startup time**, which the user observed directly and the
mechanism explains: stacks are no longer committed whole, so every stack growth
is a SIGSEGV round-trip through `virtual_handle_fault`. Fallout spawns many
threads and pays it repeatedly. Worth the memory on PS5; a poor trade for a PS4
title that already worked.

### Next experiment, narrow

Revert **0982** alone, keep 0604, and check:
1. does `FalloutNV.exe` return to `00400000`?
2. does Oblivion load at all?

Then, separately, find what maps `0x400000` outside Wine's view tracking --
`mmap-eexist` with `find_view_range()` clean is the specific contradiction to
chase, and the PS4 dmem glue is the obvious suspect since it maps without going
through Wine's wrappers.

Also still open: the Fallout fault itself (unbacked non-stack region, now with
Wine as the faulting party), and restoring `minflt`/`inblock` to the `alive`
line so "slow or hung?" is answerable at all.
