## 2026-10-28, 18:00-18:30 -- the `0x400000` mystery was never a collision

Three claims in the section above are **wrong**. Corrections first, because
they each cost an afternoon.

### Retraction 1: `0982` is not a regression

Package **1041** = 1040 with `0982` reverted. Result unchanged:

```
18:02:06  PW_IMG conflict why=mmap-eexist base=400000 size=1009000 host_page_mask=3fff
          Loaded L"C:\Games\FalloutNV\FalloutNV.exe" at 00530000: native
```

The inference ("19 loads at `0x400000`, then `0x530000` on 1040, so 1040's
patches did it") was unsound for a simpler reason than a wrong patch: **Fallout
was never run on 1033-1038.** Its last `0x400000` load was 16:01 on the
*pre-sync* 1032 runtime, so the regression window was 1032 -> 1040, which
contains the entire 265-commit sync, not six patches.

### Retraction 2: nothing maps `0x400000` outside Wine's view tracking

`find_view_range()` was right; the range *is* free. `anon_mmap_tryfixed()`
(`virtual.c:974`) **synthesizes** the errno on the PS4 path:

```c
if (sceKernelReserveVirtualRange( &ptr, size, PS5_MAP_FIXED | PS5_MAP_NO_OVERWRITE, host_page_size ))
{
    errno = EEXIST;          /* any failure reason, reported as a collision */
    return MAP_FAILED;
}
__wine_ps5_dmem_region( start, size );   /* success path only */
```

So `mmap-eexist` means "the reservation was refused", never "occupied". Do not
go looking for a squatter again.

### Retraction 3: memory exhaustion was not ruled out

`dmem_failures=0` and `heapfail count=0` instrument **direct memory** and the
**Unix heap**. The *flexible* pool (~440 MB/title), where Wine's anonymous
`mmap`s land, has **no failure counter at all**. It is the pool that runs dry.

### What actually happens

Every genuine conflict has a size that is not a multiple of the 16 KiB host
page; the fourteen that are aligned are just the relocation scan doing its job:

| conflict | size | size % 16K | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mmap-eexist base=400000` | `1009000` | **0x1000** | real |
| `view-range base=10000000` | `133000` | **0x3000** | real |
| `view-range base=10000000` | `9000` | **0x1000** | real |
| `view-range base=1..e000000` x14 | `600000` | 0x0 | normal scan |

`anon_mmap_tryfixed()` is the **only** PS4 reservation site that neither rounds
nor asserts host-page alignment (contrast `anon_mmap_fixed()` at 633-634,
`mmap_add_reserved_area()` at 808-809, and `ROUND_SIZE` at 677/697/737/2166/
2186/2457). On a 4 KiB host it cannot misbehave -- PE sections are always 4 KiB
multiples -- so this is PS4-specific and invisible upstream. The asserts never
fired because the reserve fails *first* and returns EEXIST.

One refusal causes all three symptoms:

1. **Relocation.** Fallout cannot take `0x400000`, so it moves to `0x530000`.
   Oblivion cannot relocate and dies at `c0000018` in `build_module()`.
2. **Exhaustion.** `__wine_ps5_dmem_region()` is on the success path, so a
   refused range is never registered; `__wine_ps5_mmap` then requires
   `MAP_FIXED` **and** a registered region (`pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c:841`) to
   use direct memory, and everything there falls to flexible instead.
3. **Slowness.** Unregistered ranges commit one SIGSEGV per 16 KiB host page
   (`PW_SEGV_ENTRY` stepping down by exactly `0x4000` at a fixed `rip`) rather
   than in bulk via `pw_wine_dmem_replace`.

Measured, same log, same day:

```
GTA IV  (reaches gameplay)  dmem=858,720K   flexible_free plateaus 49,152K   survives
Fallout 1041                dmem peak 144,304K  flexible_free -> 10,240K     dies 3m35s
Fallout 1040                                    flexible_free -> 18,432K     dies 3m24s
Fallout 16:01 PRE-SYNC      at 0x400000         flexible_free ->  8,192K     dies 3m40s
```

The pre-sync run at `0x400000` dies the same way at the same time: **relocation
is not what kills Fallout, exhaustion is**, and it predates the sync.

### Package 1042

`host_size = ROUND_SIZE( start, size, host_page_mask )` before the reserve, used
for the reserve, `__wine_ps5_dmem_region()`, `anon_mmap_fixed()` and the
failure-path `munmap()`. Adds `PW_RESERVE refused base=.. size=.. host_size=..`,
which fires only when rounding mattered.

**`PW_RESERVE` is the package marker.** A Fallout run showing `mmap-eexist`
with no `PW_RESERVE` beside it is the *old* build -- confirmed at 18:18, when a
launch 1 minute after upload was still 1041 because the pkg had not been
installed.

Predictions, any failure of which kills the theory: Fallout loads at
`0x400000`; `dmem` share rises and `flexible_free` stops reaching zero; fault
count drops sharply. Oblivion is the cleanest test -- it dies before executing
an instruction, so it either loads or it does not.

Risk to watch: rounding reserves up to 12 KiB past an image tail. Wine rounds
its own view sizes identically (2166/2186), but a **new** conflict where there
was none is the signature of this going wrong. GTA IV works today and is the
regression check before Fallout is the win.

### Still open

- `heap_dmem=0K` and `heap_spill=0K` in **every** run today: the Unix heap's
  direct-memory backing (`pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c:336`, `heap_map`) never
  engages, leaving ~50 MB in flexible that was designed not to be.
- The flexible pool still has no failure counter. Add one.
- `minflt`/`inblock` still missing from the `alive` line.
- `make` without a target fails in `nsiproxy.sys/ip.c` (missing BSD routing
  structs in the PS4 SDK). Build `dlls/ntdll/ntdll.so` by name; `prx-ps4.sh`
  takes the **last** matching link line from `make.log`, so appending an
  incremental build is safe.

### CORRECTION to the section above: alignment was NOT the cause

The 16 KiB-alignment theory is **wrong** and package 1042 (which shipped it) is
a **no-op**. `map_fixed_area()` already rounds before calling:

```c
char *start = base, *end = (char *)base + ROUND_SIZE( 0, size, host_page_mask );
```

so the size reaching `sceKernelReserveVirtualRange` was always host-page
aligned. The `size=1009000` in a `pw_img_conflict` line is the **original image
size printed at the failure site**, not the value passed to the syscall. Do not
reason about syscall arguments from that number.

Method note, because this was the third wrong theory in a day: each one was
built by inferring a cause from a logged value. The one that worked was adding
a log line that prints what the kernel was actually asked and what it answered.
**Instrument the failing call before theorising about it.**

### Actual root cause (measured, package 1043, FW 12.02)

`sceKernelReserveVirtualRange` **never succeeds on PS4** as invoked here:

```
PW_RESERVE refused rc=0x8002000c base=400000 want=0x130000 host=0x130000 align=0x4000 got=400000 prot=0x3
```

- `rc=0x8002000c` -> low byte `0x0c` = **errno 12, ENOMEM**, not EEXIST.
- **54 of 54 refusals in one launch, all the same rc. Zero grants.**
- Smallest refused: `want=0x10000` (64 KiB) at `PROT_NONE`. A 64 KiB refusal
  with ~440 MiB free is the *call* being rejected, not a shortage.
- `want=0x130000` is already 16 KiB-aligned -- alignment dead a second way.

**`errno = EEXIST` in `anon_mmap_tryfixed()` manufactures the collision.**
Nothing ever occupied `0x400000`; `find_view_range()` was right all along. Every
hour spent hunting a squatter was spent chasing an errno we wrote ourselves.

Consequence chain, now evidenced rather than inferred:

```
reserve always fails
  -> reserved_areas stays empty
  -> mmap_add_reserved_area() and __wine_ps5_dmem_region() never run
  -> map_fixed_area() always fails      -> Fallout relocates to 0x530000
                                        -> Oblivion dies c0000018 (cannot relocate)
  -> no range is dmem-backed            -> all of it lands in the ~440 MiB
                                           flexible pool -> dry at ~3.5 min
```

The decisive asymmetry: **plain `mmap` succeeds at these same addresses** --
that is how `FalloutNV.exe` loads at `0x530000` seconds after the reservation
of `0x400000` is refused.

### Package 1044

On refusal, fall back to `mmap` + verify-the-address-returned, which is what
the generic branch of `anon_mmap_tryfixed()` already does on every other
platform, then call `__wine_ps5_dmem_region()`. The PS4 kernel ignoring
MAP_EXCL was the reason the reservation was preferred; verify-after-map covers
that case.

**Scoped to `prot != PROT_NONE` on purpose.** `reserve_area()` requests
PROT_NONE blocks up to 1.7 GiB to claim address space. If PS4 charges flexible
memory on mmap regardless of prot, honouring those would consume the whole pool
at startup -- worse than the refusal. Bulk reservation stays refused; only real
mappings take the fallback. **Abort signal: `flexible_free` well below 448512K
in the first startup lines means mmap charges flexible and the guard was
wrong.** Measure that before widening the fallback to PROT_NONE.

### Still unknown

Why the PS4 kernel returns ENOMEM for a 64 KiB fixed reservation at all. Flag
values (`PS5_MAP_FIXED` 0x10, `PS5_MAP_NO_OVERWRITE` 0x80) and the
`host_page_size` alignment argument are the untested variables; a standalone
probe that sweeps flags and alignment and prints rc would answer it directly,
and would be cheaper than another full package.

## 2026-10-28, 19:15 -- THE regression: the eboot was never released

`0x400000` *was* occupied, and by us. Package 1045's query at the failure site:

```
query rc=0 start=400000 end=49c000 qprot=0x5 qtype=0 qflags=0x11 name=executable hole=0
```

**`name=executable` is our own eboot**, mapped 0x400000-0x49c000. The kernel's
ENOMEM was honest: you cannot reserve over the running executable. Retract the
claim two sections above that nothing was ever there -- `find_view_range()`
tracks Wine's views, which never included the host executable.

`src/reloc-wine-app/app_entry.c` already solves this:
`pw_relocated_wine_release_eboot()` unmaps the eboot's low segments right
before the guest starts. That is the whole reason the eboot is tiny and the
app lives in a high PRX.

**The upstream sync dropped the call site.**
`src/reloc-wine-app/0001-delay-eboot-release-until-game-start.patch` was never
reapplied to `prospero-win/native/wine64_main.c`: the function sat exported and
uncalled, and there are **zero `relocwine:` lines in the entire day's log**.

```
pre-sync   release runs  -> 0x400000 free -> 19 consecutive loads at 0x400000 (09:00-16:01)
post-sync  release gone  -> eboot squats  -> Fallout -> 0x530000; Oblivion -> c0000018
```

Restored in **1046**. Expect `relocwine: unmapping eboot seg0 0x400000..0x49c000`
and `PW_WINE64 relocatable eboot release status=0` within the first seconds.

This is NOT expected to fix the flexible-memory exhaustion: the pre-sync 16:01
run loaded at 0x400000 and still died at 3m40s with 8 MiB free. That is an
older, separate bug.

### Why the sync census missed it

The census covered `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/` and `src/wine-patches-ps4/`.
**`src/reloc-wine-app/` sits outside both.** Any future sync must check every
`*.patch` in the repo, not just the numbered series.

### Other drops found in the same audit

Confirmed, with evidence:

- **`PW_WINE64_HEAP_FLEXIBLE` and `PW_WINE64_SERVER_DIRECT_OFF` are dead.**
  `build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh` validates each as 0/1 and passes `-D`, but
  **no source file reads either any more** -- two A/B controls that look wired
  and do nothing. Check with: every `-D` the build passes, grepped against the
  sources that compile.
- **`0005-no-fd-capture-on-ps4` dropped**: the log still shows
  `fd2_capture=failed status=-3`, which that patch existed to stop.
- **`0083-skip-active-ps4-stack-force-exec` dropped** (functional, zero matches).
- Diagnostics lost: ntdll loader traces (0076-0080), force-exec traces
  (0081/0082), startup handoff (0074), `pw_diagnostics.c` (0004/0008/0009),
  dsound mixer (0006). These cost visibility, not function.

**Method caveat:** exact-line matching cannot separate "dropped" from
"superseded". `0071-wine64-report-heap-allocation-failure` scores 0/32 yet
`PwWineHeapFailures` is present -- that patch was deliberately redesigned after
the package-1018 stack smash. Treat the audit as a candidate list, then confirm
each by behaviour or by grepping for the feature rather than the patch text.

# 2026-10-29: the left stick, and two upstream PRs

## The stick worked all along -- a second profile was shadowing it

`keyboard_stick` was correct in `oblivion-gog.profile`, the parser was correct,
the overlay was correct, and `seen->left_stick` carried live values. The runtime
was reading **a different file**. Two profiles on the console both declared
`id = oblivion-gog`:

| file | `keyboard_stick` |
|---|---|
| `oblivion-gog-native.profile` | absent |
| `oblivion-gog.profile` | `left_stick` |

`pw_wine_library_load()` sorts entries by filename with `strcmp` and
`pw_wine_library_find()` returns the **first** id match. `'-'` is 0x2D and `'.'`
is 0x2E, so `oblivion-gog-native.profile` sorted first and won.

Every symptom follows from that file's `[input]`, which has only `mode`,
`mouse = right_stick` and `mouse_speed`:

- `set=0x7` (MODE|MOUSE|SPEED) against `0x67` from a host parse of the *other* file
- `mouse=2`, which is **why the mouse worked** and made the stick look like an
  isolated failure rather than a wrong-file problem
- `keyboard_stick=0`, so `key_stick` was NULL and no keys were ever emitted

Fixed by giving the variant `id = oblivion-gog-native`, then (at the user's
request) deleting the six redundant Oblivion profiles, leaving only
`oblivion-gog.profile`. Backups: `~/codex_scratch/stick-1048-20261029/consoleprofiles/`
and `oblivion-gog-native.profile.bak`.

Confirmed working on 1049:

```
PW_WINE64 input status=ok keyboard_stick=1 stick_deadzone=25 ... mouse=2 mode=0 set=0x67
PW_WINE64 stick n=58 setting=1 x=0x49 y=0x82 dead=25 held=0x4 events=1
```

`x=0x49` is 55 counts left of centre, past the 31 threshold; `held=0x4` is
STICK_LEFT; `events=1` is the `A` key-down.

## ⚠ Traps this cost a morning

1. **`PW_WINE64` output does NOT reach klog.** It goes to ps5log, on the console
   at `/data/prospero-win/logs/session-N.log`. `next.txt` holds the next index,
   so the live session is `(next-1)`. The live file returns **FTP 550** while the
   title holds it; `session-N.previous.log` is the rotated copy. A klog `tail`
   filtered for `PW_WINE64` is silent no matter what happens. Puller:
   `/tmp/claude-1000/.../scratchpad/pull_ps5log.py` (tolerates the 550).
2. **`PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL` must be passed to
   `scripts/build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh`.** Omitting it silently ships the
   stock `kernelbase.dll` (1,273,870 B, **zero** `PW_EXCEPTION32`) instead of the
   instrumented one (4,834,908 B, 23 records, md5 `b383ad83ef61`, built at
   `prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/build/dlls/kernelbase/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll`).
   The first 1049 build did exactly that; the whole 3.5 MB package-size difference
   was that one file. **Always diff package size against the last known-good and
   explain any delta.**
3. **A gated diagnostic makes silence ambiguous.** The 1048 stick log fired only on
   `deflected || count != before_stick`, so "setting is off" and "axes frozen"
   both reported as nothing at all. 1049 logs unconditionally at startup and
   samples every 120 frames, and named the cause in seconds. **Log a decision's
   inputs, not its effects.**
4. **Check which file the runtime loaded, not just the file on disk.**
   `PW_WINE64 profile id=...` prints the id, never the path. Four correct
   components were verified before anyone asked which file was being read.

## Retractions (do not re-run these)

- **`&seen->left_stick` -> `&pad.left_stick` was NOT the fix.** The handoff
  predicted it. The working mouse path reads `&seen->right_stick`, the same
  struct, so `seen` was proven live by the thing that worked.
- **The shipped `pw_game_profile.o` was NOT stale.** `stick_deadzone` is absent
  from the PRX at byte level, but the PS4 clang compiling the current source
  produces an object with the identical string profile. Clang inlines that
  comparison; gcc emits it. **String presence in a PS4 binary proves nothing.**
- **Upstream's `errno = EEXIST` is NOT simply a bug.**
  `sceKernelReserveVirtualRange` returns ENOMEM (`0x8002000c`) for an occupied
  range *and* for one it cannot grant -- measured: `base=400000 want=0x130000`
  refused with `0x8002000c` while the eboot genuinely held `0x400000-0x49c000`.
  The callers need EEXIST for a collision, so translating the errno faithfully
  would make `map_view` fail hard where it should relocate.

## Upstream: two PRs open against `mpereiraesaa/prospero-win`

Fork is `plastikman/prospero-win`. Worktree: `~/codex_scratch/pw-upstream`
(separate from the PS4 build tree, which stays on `sync-trial-2`).

| PR | branch | files |
|---|---|---|
| [#736](https://github.com/mpereiraesaa/prospero-win/pull/736) `input: a stick can drive the four direction keys` | `feature/keyboard-stick` | 8 |
| [#737](https://github.com/mpereiraesaa/prospero-win/pull/737) `ntdll: name the occupant of a refused reservation on PS5` | `fix/reserve-refusal-diagnostic` | 3 |

Both bodies state that no PS5 firmware/artifact identity can be offered and that
the host checks are the reproducible part. Upstream CONTRIBUTING is firm that a
host pass does not substitute for console evidence.

### How to produce a verifiable wine patch

`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/source` is a git repo sitting exactly on the
documented base `490f6d5dcbb2a5047345b8af88d114bbcaad69a8`, with our patches as
uncommitted changes. So:

```sh
git -C prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/source worktree add --detach ~/codex_scratch/wine-upstream-state 490f6d5dcb
cd ~/codex_scratch/wine-upstream-state
for p in ~/codex_scratch/pw-upstream/wine/patches/*.patch; do git apply --whitespace=nowarn "$p"; done   # 100/100 clean
git add -A && git commit -m "upstream state"      # now diffs are isolated
# ... edit ... then: git diff > new.patch
```

Upstream checks: `python3 tests/test_build_wine_ps5.py` (reports the patch
count), `make audit check-whitespace`, `python3 tests/test_docs_links.py`.
A new patch needs a `PUBLICATION_ALLOWLIST.txt` line **inserted in place** (that
file is deliberately NOT sorted -- `sort -o` reorders 527 lines) and a row in the
`docs/WINE_PS5_BUILD.md` table.

## The relocation argument, with evidence

Upstream's `0130` says the PS5 title's own image may occupy a program's preferred
base (`0x400000`) and relocates the program when it does. That cannot work for an
image with no relocations. `Oblivion.exe`, measured:

```
PE32  ImageBase=0x400000  Characteristics=0x0123  RELOCS_STRIPPED=True
sections: .text .rdata .data .tls .rsrc   (no .reloc)   BASERELOC rva=0x0 size=0x0
```

It must have `0x400000` or fail, on PS4 **and** PS5. Fallout NV relocating to
`0x530000` is upstream's path working as designed; Oblivion is the case it cannot
reach. So the relocation work is a PS5-relevant feature, not a PS4 port.

Portability, measured: our `eboot_segments()` duplicates upstream's
`pw_prx_parse_module_info()` byte for byte -- info size `0x160`, count at
`+0x148`, segments at `+0x108 + i*16`, size `+8`, protection `+12`. PS4-specific
is only the hardcoded `RANGE_END 0x55c000` (should be derived), the
`<orbis/libkernel.h>` header name, and the two-module build/packaging.

# 2026-10-29 (afternoon): correcting the Fallout framing

## ⚠ There is no "3.5 minute death". It was VATS all along.

This section replaces an earlier draft of it that was wrong. The sequence of
errors, recorded so it is not repeated:

1. The handoff got it **right** at line ~2705: Fallout "reached gameplay. About
   3.5 minutes later, while the user was entering **VATS**, it faulted" --
   `wine: Unhandled page fault on read access to 00000024 at address 7B2923FD
   (thread 0024)`, which is `d3d9.dll` RVA `0x423FD`. That section states
   plainly that it is **problem 3, pre-existing in the Oct 15 baseline, not a
   regression**, that "none of the memory or NTDLL work was ever going to
   address it", and that it "must not be counted against any build".
2. Later summaries compressed "faulted at 3.5 min entering VATS" into "dies at
   ~3.5 min of flexible exhaustion" -- inventing both a cause and an event.
3. Today that compressed version was believed, and an exec-migration memory
   corruption hypothesis was built on top of it.
4. The `access=0000008c` / `00000024` fault records cited as evidence of guest
   structure corruption **are the d3d9 VATS fault itself** (`7B2923FD`). An
   already-explained fault was used as new evidence.

**So: Fallout on the current build reaches gameplay and hits the pre-existing
VATS fault, the same as oct15.** The difference between oct15 and now is
**speed**, not survival.

## What is actually open for Fallout

| # | problem | status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | crash at Continue, never reaches gameplay | windowed earlier; **not** the current behaviour |
| 2 | GTA IV hangs at the menu | separate |
| 3 | `d3d9.dll+0x423FD` entering VATS | **pre-existing Oct 15, both builds**, a real bug, nobody's regression |
| 4 | **current build is much slower than oct15** | the live question |

Do not treat 3 as a measurement of anything else. Avoid VATS when testing.

## The speed question, with the one hard measurement

| | oct15 | 1049 (current) |
|---|---|---|
| `exec-range flex-migrate` | **0** | 57,052 in one run |
| `exec-protect-verify` | **0** | thousands |
| klog `build=` markers | none | 0968, 0970, 0971, 0974, 0975 |
| user's description | "so much faster" | slow |

Migrations ran on **at least 6 threads** (18134 / 3042 / 563 / 130 / 93 / 89 by
tid), largest single window **16,777,216 B = 16 MiB**.

### Exec migration is LOAD-BEARING (measured, package 1050)

1050 = 1049 with `PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES=0`. **It dies ~32 s in:**

```
wine-ps4: exec-range call=1378 addr=23b0000 bytes=89128960 prot=0x7 fallback=skip-large
wine-ps4: fault-page-rx-repair attempt=5 before=0x1 sce=0 libc=0 verify=0 after=0x1
wine-ps4: image-rx-watch page=4a6000 effective=0x1 type=0 flags=0x12
```

Both `sce` and `libc` mprotect return **0 (success)** while the page stays
`effective=0x1` (read-only); the guest needs `0x5`. Without migration,
dmem-backed image pages never become executable. The migrations are not wasted
work -- they are how executable memory is produced.

### ⭐ The highest-value unknown

**oct15 performs zero migrations and still runs.** It therefore obtains
executable pages by some other route. Establish what that is before refactoring
anything: it likely explains the entire speed difference, and may make the
migration path unnecessary rather than merely safer. Start by diffing the
Oct 15 package's `ntdll.prx` / glue against the current one, and by finding when
`exec-range` / `fault-page-rx-repair` (build markers 0968, 0971, 0974) entered
the tree.

## Exact build / test commands (2026-10-29, all verified this session)

Everything below was run from `/home/zpeirce/ps4-gladius`. Console is
`10.168.2.52`, FTP `2121`, klog `3232`.

### 1. Rebuild the Wine PRXs, optionally with an exec-migration A/B define

`scripts/prx-ps4.sh` takes no arguments; it is driven by environment and reuses
`$PW_ROOT/.deps/wine-ps5/make.log` from the previous Wine build. It compiles
`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` at line 62, which is where the two knobs
land.

```sh
# default (migration on, 16 MiB cap) -- what 1049 shipped
sh scripts/prx-ps4.sh

# A: migration disabled entirely  -> produced 1050 (KNOWN BAD, dies ~32 s)
PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS="-DPW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES=0" sh scripts/prx-ps4.sh

# B: per-page migration, window 16 MiB -> 16 KiB  -> produced 1051
PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS="-DPW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_PER_PAGE_ONLY=1" sh scripts/prx-ps4.sh
```

**⚠ `prx-ps4.sh` does not echo its cflags**, so a typo'd define silently builds
the default. Verify by compiling the object twice and comparing:

```sh
CC=build/ps4-wine-sdk/bin/prospero-clang
$CC -std=c11 -O2 -fPIC -I prospero-win/wine/ps5 \
    -c src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c -o /tmp/glue-default.o
$CC -std=c11 -O2 -fPIC -I prospero-win/wine/ps5 -DPW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES=0 \
    -c src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c -o /tmp/glue-ab.o
md5sum /tmp/glue-default.o /tmp/glue-ab.o
```

Known md5 prefixes: default `dcf1770819d7`, `MAX_BYTES=0` `cfbca32ca810`,
`PER_PAGE_ONLY=1` `384d2a289d1f`. Resulting `ntdll.prx`: default
`b9b7030fd3b2`, `MAX_BYTES=0` `84ba9764d0db`.

### 2. Build the package

**⚠ `PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL` is effectively mandatory.** Omit it and the package
silently ships the stock `kernelbase.dll` (1,273,870 B, **zero**
`PW_EXCEPTION32`) instead of the instrumented one (4,834,908 B, 23 records,
md5 `b383ad83ef61`) -- i.e. no guest-fault diagnostics at all. The first 1049
build did exactly that; the whole 3.5 MB package-size difference was that one
file.

```sh
KB="$PWD/prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/build/dlls/kernelbase/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll"

TITLE_ID=GLDS10085 \
TITLE="FNV per-page migrate 1051" \
PKG_OUT="$PWD/pkgout/falloutnv-perpage-1051" \
PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL="$KB" \
  scripts/build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh
```

Other env the script honours: `PW_WINE64_WAIT_WATCHDOG`,
`PW_WINE64_SERVER_DIRECT_OFF`, `PW_WINE64_HEAP_FLEXIBLE` (each 0/1),
`PS4_IPHLPAPI32_DLL`, `--neo`, `PW_BUILD_ROOT`, `PW_APP_OBJ`, `PKG_BASE_OUT`.

Takes ~12-15 min. `pkgout/wine-app-relocatable-base/` filling up is the normal
intermediate stage, not a stray nonrelocatable package -- the shipped package is
built from it with `relocated_wine_app.prx` added.

**⚠ Two traps when waiting for it to finish:**

- Do **not** wait with `while pgrep -f "build-relocatable-wine-ps4"` -- the wait
  command's own command line contains that string, so `pgrep` matches itself and
  the loop never exits.
- Do **not** use `grep -q '^pkg: '` as the completion sentinel -- the nested base
  build prints its own `pkg:` line first. Match the final package path, e.g.
  `grep -q 'pkgout/<name>/.*\.pkg'`, or just poll for the `.pkg` file.

### 3. Gate the package before uploading

```sh
P=pkgout/falloutnv-perpage-1051
PKG=$P/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg
R=$P/pkg-stage/sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx
KBP=$P/pkg-stage/win/wine/lib/wine/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll

stat -Lc%s $PKG                                   # expect 449052672
strings $KBP | grep -c PW_EXCEPTION32             # MUST be nonzero (23)
md5sum $KBP                                       # expect b383ad83ef61...
md5sum $P/pkg-stage/sce_module/ntdll.prx          # the only intended difference
strings $R | grep -c 'relocatable eboot release'  # 1
strings $R | grep -c 'PW_WINE64 stick n='         # 1
strings $R | grep -c 'PW_WINE64 input status'     # 1

# every file that differs in size from the last known-good package
join -j2 -o 0,1.1,2.1 \
  <(cd $P/pkg-stage && find . -type f -printf '%s %p\n' | sort -k2) \
  <(cd pkgout/oblivion-stickdiag-1049/pkg-stage && find . -type f -printf '%s %p\n' | sort -k2) \
  | awk '$2!=$3 {printf "%+9d  %s\n", $2-$3, $1}'
```

A package differing only by a define has an **identical size** and an identical
file list; only `ntdll.prx`'s md5 moves (the PRX size is unchanged because
alignment padding absorbs it). An unchanged md5 means the define did not reach
the build.

### 4. Upload

```sh
python3 scripts/ftp-put.py 10.168.2.52 2121 \
  pkgout/falloutnv-perpage-1051/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg \
  /data/pkg/falloutnv-perpage-1051.pkg
```

`scripts/deploy.sh <target>` wraps this for its own named targets and refuses to
upload a package older than its inputs; for ad-hoc A/B packages call `ftp-put.py`
directly. All GLDS10085 packages share one title ID, so installing one replaces
the previous.

### 5. Read the logs

```sh
# ps5log -- where every PW_WINE64 line goes. NOT klog.
python3 -I scripts/pull-ps5log.py ~/codex_scratch/<run-dir>

# klog -- where wine-ps4:, relocwine: and the kernel's own lines go
python3 -u sdk/orbis-sdk-v1/orbis-compat/scripts/ps4/log-receiver.py klog \
  --host 10.168.2.52 --port 3232 --out ~/codex_scratch/<run-dir>/klog.log
```

Only one klog client can attach at a time; a stale receiver from an earlier
session will hold port 3232 and yours will exit with `klog closed by console`.
Check with `pgrep -af log-receiver.py` before blaming the console.

Confirm which package is actually running, rather than trusting install order:

```sh
# the measurement itself is the best check -- migration count in the run
grep -c "exec-range flex-migrate" <klog>     # 1049: thousands, 1050: 0, oct15: 0
```

`[user]` vs `[system]` on the `EXEC /app0/eboot.bin` line matters: `[system]` is
the crash reporter's own relaunch, **not** the game. Analyse the `[user]` pid.

### 6. Inspect a PE (used to prove the DXVK/builtin question)

```sh
python3 -I scripts/pe-headers.py <ftp-path-on-console>   # headers over FTP
x86_64-w64-mingw32-objdump -d --start-address=<va> --stop-address=<va+N> <dll>
```

## Problem 3 in detail: the VATS d3d9 fault (now the main blocker)

This is the only thing actually stopping Fallout play, and it has never been
worked on because it kept being classified as "pre-existing, not our
regression". It is nobody's regression; it is just an unfixed bug.

**The observation** (line ~2705, original and correct):

```
09:09:22  wine: Unhandled page fault on read access to 00000024
          at address 7B2923FD (thread 0024)
```

A read of **`0x24`** -- a null pointer plus field offset `0x24`, i.e. a null
`this` or a null member being dereferenced, not an allocation failure.

**Which module it is -- established today.** Two `d3d9.dll` candidates exist:

| | size | sha256 (16) | ImageBase | covers RVA 0x423FD? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DXVK app-local | 6,470,070 | `96373c2d585a4cf3` | `0x62440000` | **yes, `.text`** |
| Wine builtin (shipped in the package) | 201,742 | `10016c6c84da4c94` | `0x10000000` | **no -- no section reaches it** |

So under the recorded base the faulting module is **DXVK's app-local
`d3d9.dll`, v2.6.2+**. A local copy is at `~/codex_scratch/fallout3/d3d9.dll`
(sha matches the one retrieved from the console prefix).

**⚠ What is NOT established.** The RVA depends entirely on the assumed load
base: `0x7B2923FD - 0x7B250000 = 0x423FD`, but e.g. a base of `0x7B290000`
would make it `0x23FD`. `0x7B250000` is an assumption in the original record,
not a measured module base, and `0x7B......` is also where Wine places its own
builtins. **Confirm the base from the run's module list before trusting any
disassembly.**

Disassembly attempts at RVA `0x423FD` (both `-b binary -m i386` and via the PE
headers, which agree) put that address *inside* the 6-byte instruction at
`0x423f9`, so it is not an instruction start under that assumption -- which is
itself a reason to doubt the base. The surrounding window is a 64-bit atomic
read-modify-write (`lock cmpxchg8b 0x748(%esi)`, a counter at object offset
`0x748`), but that is context, not the faulting instruction.

**How to take it further:**

1. Get the real base for `d3d9.dll` in the failing run (module list / load log),
   then recompute the RVA.
2. Disassemble from a known function start, not a guessed offset:
   `x86_64-w64-mingw32-objdump -d --start-address=<base+rva-0x200> ...` and walk
   forward, or find the enclosing function via the export/pdata tables.
3. A read of `0x24` off null in DXVK v2.6.2 on a D3D9 path entered by VATS
   suggests a device/context object that is null on that path -- VATS uses
   render-to-texture for the overlay, so a null swapchain/backbuffer or
   render-target view is the shape to look for.
4. Cheap experiments that need no rebuild: try a newer or older DXVK
   `d3d9.dll` in the prefix, and try `d3d9.maxAvailableMemory` / other
   `dxvk.conf` knobs already discussed at line ~1314.

## The unlocked migration window -- now directly implicated by the 1049 fault

Earlier notes marked this hazard unmotivated because the then-known crash was
the separate VATS `d3d9.dll` fault. The 2026-10-29 1049 Continue/load traces now
tie a guest read fault to the exact page while another thread is migrating it.
The old VATS finding remains separate; this is new direct evidence for the
migration race.

`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c:461`, `migrate_exec_range_to_flex()`:

```c
saved = mmap(NULL, bytes, PROT_READ|PROT_WRITE, MAP_PRIVATE|MAP_ANON, -1, 0);
memcpy(saved, (const void *)address, bytes);              /* (1) snapshot      */
release_rc = pw_wine_dmem_replace(&dmem, address, bytes, 0);  /* (2) make gone */
mapped = mmap((void *)address, bytes, PROT_READ|PROT_WRITE,
              MAP_FIXED|MAP_PRIVATE|MAP_ANON, -1, 0);     /* (3) remap flex    */
memcpy(mapped, saved, bytes);                             /* (4) copy back     */
adopt_rc = pw_wine_dmem_adopt(&dmem, address, bytes);
munmap(saved, bytes);
```

No lock is held across (1)..(4), so a concurrent write into
`[address, address+bytes)` is lost: after (1) before (2) it is overwritten by
(4); between (2) and (3) it faults or is lost; between (3) and (4) it is
overwritten by (4). The file's own comment says as much. With windows up to
16 MiB across 6+ threads the exposure is large. The 1049 fault-time query found
the exact 16 KiB range absent while another thread migrated it; the flexible
remap had failed and direct-memory restore completed just after the guest
fault. This directly demonstrates the transient hole race. It does not
establish that every bad guest pointer or every Fallout crash has this cause.

Secondary cost, relevant to speed: the scratch `mmap(NULL, bytes, ...)`
allocates up to 16 MiB of **flexible** memory per migration, 57,052 times a run.

### The two knobs (both `PW_PRX_EXTRA_CFLAGS`, compiled into the PRX)

Read in `pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` (~line 610), reaching the compiler via
`scripts/prx-ps4.sh:62`. **`prx-ps4.sh` does not echo its cflags** -- verify a
define took effect by compiling the object twice and comparing md5: default
`dcf1770819d7`, `MAX_BYTES=0` `cfbca32ca810`, `PER_PAGE_ONLY=1` `384d2a289d1f`.

| define | default | effect |
|---|---|---|
| `PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_MAX_BYTES` | `16*1024*1024` | `0` disables migration -> **crashes, see 1050** |
| `PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_PER_PAGE_ONLY` | `0` | `1` per-page loop: window 16 MiB -> 16 KiB, same end state |

If the window is ever worth fixing properly, options in preference order:
make the range **unwritable rather than absent** for the duration (verify with
`sceKernelVirtualQuery`, not mprotect's return code, since 1050 shows mprotect
returns success without changing `effective`); serialise against guest writes
per mapping; migrate at **image load** before the guest can reach the pages (the
`image-rx-*` diagnostics suggest the load path already knows the ranges); or
adopt whatever oct15 does.

### A/B packages (one variable each, all byte-identical but `ntdll.prx`)

| pkg | ntdll.prx md5 | migration | window | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1049 `oblivion-stickdiag-1049` | `b9b7030fd3b2` | on | <=16 MiB | Oblivion fine; later Continue/load read fault matches active migration |
| 1050 `falloutnv-nomigrate-1050` | `84ba9764d0db` | **off** | -- | **dies ~32 s, pages stay 0x1** |
| 1051 `falloutnv-perpage-1051` | `64a1d882fc16` | on | **16 KiB** | Very slow; separate Continue/load fault at RVA `0x00757BD5` |

1051's performance result is not representative of 1049's default range
migration. User's latest estimate for the slowdown against Oct 15 is about
30%.

## 2026-10-29: Fallout NV native loading A/B and gameplay-entry faults

User ran the Oct 15 baseline (`GLDS10080`) and the per-page migration build
(`GLDS10085`, package `falloutnv-perpage-1051`) with the same Fallout NV profile
and native WoW64 CPU mode.

| Build | Timings / result |
|---|---|
| Oct 15 `GLDS10080` | 13.85 s on dots; 7.26 s dots-to-carousel; 1:49 to Continue; 37.06 s Continue-to-first gameplay frame; corrected total **2:47** |
| 1051 `GLDS10085` | First 1080p carousel frame at about **4:16** after launch; severe frame-transition pauses; crashed while loading into gameplay after Continue |
| 1049 `GLDS10085` | First 1080p carousel frame about **1:22** after app start; Start usable around **1:30**; crashed after about **55 s** on the carousel following Continue. User estimates roughly **30% slower** overall than Oct 15. |

1051 is specifically the `PW_PS4_EXEC_MIGRATE_PER_PAGE_ONLY=1` A/B (16 KiB
per-page executable migration). Its slow result is not representative of the
1049 default migration build. During the run, the Wine heartbeat stayed alive,
but reported only four frames. The last pre-fault memory sample was
`flexible_free=8192K`, `dmem=83104K` at 09:30:00.837. The klog window recorded
8,145 per-page migration completions. The current heartbeat lacks
`minflt`/`inblock`, so this run cannot separate storage latency from migration
and memory pressure.

At 09:30:02.065, while loading after Continue, Wine recorded an access violation
on thread `0x90`: read `0x2f132d00` at guest PC `0x00b57bd5`. The main game image
was loaded at `0x00400000`; this is RVA `0x00757bd5`. Matching
`build-ps4-logs/FalloutNV.exe` (MD5 `49f3078187cb1f868581f7b3ac8c9164`) decodes
the instruction as `mov (%esi),%eax`; the guest exception state/stack identifies
`0x2f132d00` as the object pointer. The caller at `0x00b57c45` calls this
routine after loading a non-null element from an object array. This is a guest
game-image object dereference during gameplay loading, not the VATS D3D9 fault.
Flexible memory was nearly exhausted at the time, but that correlation does
not prove it created the bad pointer.

The exact run logs, package metadata, and matching game PE are retained at
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-1051-continue-20261029/`. No
`.sorbisdmp` was retained per the user's instruction; the Wine exception context
is in the ps5log excerpt. The packaged 32-bit `kernelbase.dll` had 23
`PW_EXCEPTION32` markers, although this failure emitted `PW_EXCEPTION_SETUP`,
register, and guest-stack records instead of a `PW_EXCEPTION32` record.

The controlled 1049 comparison has now run with the same native profile,
prefix, save, and Continue/load path. It uses the default exec-range migration
path (not 1051's 16 KiB per-page mode), so it is faster than 1051 but still
slower than Oct 15 and still crashes during gameplay loading. Its package SHA256
is `95c5564b8900734a32376ae09a1d8395b7141e648ae4573b689af2a6e78f8052`;
`ntdll.prx` MD5 is `b9b7030fd3b2d198eaf2468b1f20867c`. It retains relocation and
instrumented 32-bit kernelbase (`PW_EXCEPTION32` marker count 23).

At 09:48:17.380, 1049 hit a guest read AV on thread `0x94` at
`FalloutNV.exe+0x0015945A` (absolute EIP `0x0055945A`), reading `0x3213044C`.
The matching PE is MD5 `49f3078187cb1f868581f7b3ac8c9164`; the instruction is
`mov (%eax),%eax`. The captured return chain includes `0x0043B242` and
`0x004EFFAB`; disassembly shows the code walks a game object/container chain
and dereferences the object at `0x32130440`. The PS4 fault record reports the
page `0x32130000-0x32134000` as unmapped at fault time. In the same klog
sequence, the migration worker is processing that exact 16 KiB page:
`exec-range verify call=68526 addr=32130000 bytes=16384`, immediately followed
by `exec-range flex-migrate map failed ... bytes=16384 errno=22 restore=0`.
This sequence means the direct-memory mapping had been released, the flexible
replacement failed, and direct-memory backing was restored. The guest read
fault landed in that temporary hole. The migration worker's thread ID differs
from the faulting thread, so this is concurrent access during the unmapped
window, not the migration thread faulting on its own copy.

The 15:51:52 earlier occurrence has the same fault EIP and caller chain. Its
Wine-side `VirtualQuery` record says the page was committed private RW, while
the simultaneous PS4 fault-time query says that page was absent. Matching
`exec-range verify` and failed-migration records again name the faulting page.
This confirms a transient VM-view mismatch during migration; the fault is not
evidence by itself of a stale game object. The earlier 14:38:33 occurrence at
the same accessor also coincides with migration of its faulting page. See
`prior-matching-signature.log` for the preserved contexts and klog.

Immediately before the 1049 fault, Wine reported 9 MiB free flexible memory
and zero dmem allocation failures. That low headroom may contribute to the
`EINVAL` mapping failures, but the log does not prove why `mmap` returned it.

This exact EIP and read-dereference had already occurred twice in earlier
native logs: at 14:38:33 reading `0x2F14827C`, and at 15:51:52 reading
`0x3213066C`. The latter has the same caller return chain (`0x0043B242`,
`0x004EFFAB`) as the new 1049 fault. Those earlier run excerpts and the current
run are retained under
`~/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-1049-continue-20261029/`. All three
faults coincide with migration of the page they access, so the repeated crash
signature now directly implicates the migration hole race. The earlier excerpts
do not preserve enough user action context to assert that all three occurred at
the same point in the game.

No `.sorbisdmp` was retained per the user's instruction. Use the in-process
exception context and klog mapping record. The targeted retry is implemented
in package 1052 below. Keep VATS `d3d9.dll+0x423FD` separate. Do not use the
rejected patched FalloutNV.exe route.

### 2026-10-29: package 1052, retry faults inside active RX migration

Added a lock-free active-range table to
`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`. It publishes the span before the snapshot
and unmap, then clears it after migration or restoration. In ntdll's page-fault
path, a fault waits up to 250 ms only when its address overlaps an active
migration. It retries the guest instruction only after `sceKernelVirtualQuery`
confirms the requested read/write/execute permission is present. Unrelated or
unresolved faults continue through Wine's normal exception path. Read recovery
emits a bounded `exec-migrate read-fault-recovered` klog record. The Wine-side
change is saved as
`src/wine-patches-ps4/1011-ntdll-retry-fault-during-exec-migration.patch`; its
PRX export is listed in `scripts/prx-ps4.sh`.

Built and uploaded a strict A/B package:

- Console installer filename: `IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
- FTP path: `/data/pkg/falloutnv-migration-retry-1052.pkg` (port 2121)
- Local package:
  `pkgout/falloutnv-migration-retry-1052-strict/IV0000-GLDS10085_00-GLDS100850000000.pkg`
- SHA256: `3d4406cf98cfc3cc7f73f32d8b089bdd4aa0f7b2e3053542364f5caf69a8b3b3`
- `ntdll.prx` MD5: `99c31b0ac2e129ad8d6c9c78dce8d191`; the helper export and
  recovery log string are present. Packaged 32-bit kernelbase retains 23
  `PW_EXCEPTION32` markers.

The strict package stage is byte-identical to 1049 except for both copies of
`ntdll.prx`; the eboot is byte-identical too. This preserves 1049's relocation,
filesystem, input, native WoW64, and close-path behavior while changing the
fault retry. A full rebuild also changed seven unrelated PRXs, so that package
was not uploaded; this strict variant avoids those unrelated variables.

The 1052 Continue/load run reached the loading carousel and then showed a black
screen before **the application crashed**. The PS4 stayed running and recovered
to its normal UI; this was not a console/system UI crash. The user reports this
run took roughly 4:30 to reach the same point, versus about 2:40 on the Oct 15
baseline, and the screen had no FPS counter.

Klog confirms an app crash for `GLDS10085` PID `0x2cb`, reason `0x4`, at
10:22:32.367, followed by an app coredump. The last Wine heartbeat was

### 2026-10-29: package 1053, aggregate RX-path timing

The user suspects executable-memory remapping explains most of the slowdown.
The evidence makes it the leading hypothesis: Oct 15 was native without the
current migration path; default migration 1049 was slower; and 1051's per-page
migration was much slower. The 1052 klog showed 1,358 completed migrations,
455 failed fixed remaps, and 61 failed scratch allocations in about 228 s.
Those counts do not quantify elapsed cost, so package 1053 adds low-volume
timing rather than changing migration behavior.

The timing instrumentation is in `src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`. It
emits one `wine-ps4: exec-rx-timing` record about every 30 seconds while
mprotect calls continue. It measures total mprotect and executable mprotect
call counts and summed elapsed nanoseconds; timed `sceKernelVirtualQuery`
count/time in the RX checks; migration count/time/bytes/success/failure; and
scratch mmap, the two copies, direct-memory release, fixed mmap, and adoption
phase times. It adds no per-operation timing lines. Migration total includes
existing migration diagnostics; phase times exclude those diagnostics. These
are summed per-call elapsed times, so concurrent calls can make totals exceed
the 30-second wall window. Use the counters and phase breakdown to identify
where time accumulates; do not interpret the sum as exclusive process CPU time.

refcount zero. These are similar invalid-object symptoms across different
addresses and runs—not proof yet that they share one root cause, or that these
objects are COM objects in the strict interface/`IUnknown` sense.

The new measurement materially sharpens the diagnosis. Wine's fault-page query
reports committed private PAGE_READWRITE for `0x3009D000`, and the diagnostic
handler can read the object's vtable (`0x010A8F90`) and refcount (`5`). But the
same-time kernel `PW_SEGV_ENTRY` reports `err=0x4` (user read, not-present),
`kprot=0` for the kernel range `0x3009C000-0x300A0000`. The exec-migration
probe reports `match=0 active=0`. The evidence therefore points to a mismatch
between Wine's VM bookkeeping and the effective kernel mapping/protection; it
does not support an RX-migration cause. Flexible memory was down to 14 MiB, but
`dmem_failures=0`, so this is not proof of OOM. The later winevulkan/syscall
SIGILL cascade follows the guest AV and is secondary.

#### First source-level delta to investigate

The first concrete post-Oct-15 behavior change is executable-page protection.
The preserved Wine 11.17 source snapshot at
`codex_scratch/prospero-win-v0.1.1/.deps/wine-ps5/source/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c`
has `mprotect_exec()` call libc `mprotect()` directly. The current source routes
both forced-exec and normal protection through `host_mprotect()`; PS4 binds
that to `__wine_ps5_mprotect()`, which can migrate executable direct-memory
pages by snapshotting, unmapping, replacing, copying, and adopting them in
flexible memory. The relevant patches were added after Oct 15:

- `0935-ntdll-force-prospero-exec-protection-after-mmap.patch` (Oct 17)
- `0946-ntdll-use-prospero-mprotect-for-executable-pages.patch` (Oct 18)

The matching klog `PW_SEGV_ENTRY` at 06:28:34.465 reports x86 page-fault error

at `0x0808C000-0x08090000` while Wine still considers the guest page

mapping discrepancy seen at different addresses in 1057, 1059, and 1064, not a
new NTDLL initialization failure. Memory telemetry showed 6 MiB flexible free
and zero dmem allocation failures; do not label this OOM without evidence.

implementation and its strings were present in the PRX, but the Wine signal path

present in 1055, 1058, 1059, 1060, 1061, 1064, and 1067, and absent in 1069.

#### Package 1071 result: migration retry works, Continue reaches a repeat heap-object failure

The user installed 1071 and pressed Continue in the native `fallout-new-vegas`
profile. Native WoW64 initialized successfully. The 1071 relink correction is
confirmed in live evidence: the fault hook ran, and at 08:22:24 it accepted a
read fault at `0x080A96C0` after `sceKernelVirtualQuery` confirmed the page was
readable (`map-fault-probe ... result=kernel-allows`), then emitted
`exec-migrate-late-retry ... addr=80a96c0`. This was a recoverable fault on a
different thread/address; it is not the fatal Continue fault.

The fatal first guest exception is a read AV at FalloutNV.exe `0x00A59FB5`
(RVA `0x00659FB5`), address `0xBC`, with `EAX=0` and `ESI=0x32132880`. The
exact PE instruction is `mov edx,[eax+0xbc]`. Its caller path shows a virtual
method at vtable offset `0xB8` returning, then the game rereads `[ESI]` and
dispatches the next method at offset `0xBC`. At the exception, that reread
yielded null; roughly 100 ms later the existing diagnostic read of the same
object at `0x32132880` showed first DWORD `0x010BD44C` and refcount 1. Treat
that late sample as racy; it does not disprove that the fault-time value was
null.

This is not an isolated random address. The exact object address
`0x32132880` is the same address that faulted in package 1058. There, Wine
reported it committed/read-write while the kernel reported the containing
16-KiB page `0x32130000-0x32134000` absent; the retained map history for that
page includes interleaved `host-mprotect`, `dmem-unmap`, `reserve`, and
`map-dmem` operations. In 1071, the fatal instruction itself is a null-pointer
read rather than a missing host-page read, but the object address ties it to
the same allocation/page family. This makes the direct-memory remap / object
state interaction the leading investigation, not proof yet of causation.

server/bootstrap failure, before the Fallout executable or WoW64 backend can

I reviewed the relevant findings across all four handoffs (2026-10-09,
2026-10-14, 2026-10-20, and 2026-10-26) plus `docs/FINDINGS.md`. The older
handoffs preserve the known-good Oct 15 runtime behavior, relocation,
filesystem/input/close-path work, ABI 4 packaging requirements, and previous
server startup investigations. The findings spell out that the PS4 startup
fixes are a dependent sequence, not interchangeable one-off experiments:

- `0112` skips the unusable PS4 prefix ownership check.
- `0734` reopens server files instead of relying on unsupported descriptor
  duplication; `0735` shares the NLS mapping fd and clears the stale error.
- `0736` hands the in-process client fd to ntdll; `0737` and `0738` transfer
  protocol descriptors by number because PS4 descriptor passing is unavailable.
- `0742` uses the PS4 nonblocking ioctl path; `0743` handles a null environment;
  `0744` maps the shared-data host page; `0745` respects server fd ownership;
  `0746` uses the already-booted prefix; and `0747` avoids the unavailable auxv.
- `0771` drains the wake pipe with `poll()`; `0871` installs `mapping_fd_ops`
  when the shared mapping fd has no owner. `0735` without `0871` can leave that
  fd ownerless and trigger the previously observed null `fd_ops` failure.

The earlier test carried only the partial mapping fix and consequently failed
before `server connected`. I rebuilt with the complete recorded chain. All 107
queued patches apply in order to the pinned Wine commit
`490f6d5dcbb2a5047345b8af88d114bbcaad69a8`; affected wineserver/ntdll targets

#### Correction after complete-chain launch: 0866 request-pipe ownership fix (2026-10-30)

The user installed the complete-chain package and reported spinning dots followed
by a crash. The app log now reaches `server connected` at 11:28:37.089 and emits
an alive tick at 11:28:37.636, so the previous in-process server startup failure
is resolved. It then crashes before the game produces a frame. This is not the
Fallout load/Continue fault.

The retained klog reports a fatal SIGUSR2/SIGTHR on the `orbis` thread. I
symbolized the NTDLL frames against this exact package's `ntdll.shared.elf`:
`wine_server_call` (`server.c:723`) was called by `NtOpenKeyEx`
(`registry.c:155`), then the native WoW64 syscall dispatcher. This is the
recorded in-process server thread/request-pipe failure path. The exact crash
dump path announced by GoldHEN was
`/user/data/sce_coredumps/GLDS10085_1793384917/GLDS10085_1793384917.sorbisdmp`;
GoldHEN returned FTP 550 by the time it was retrieved, so the dump was not
available. The live Wine and klog records provide the actionable context.

The audit had carried the descriptor transfer patches `0736`-`0738`, but missed
the required follow-up `0866-ntdll-ps5-do-not-close-the-servers-request-pipe`.
In v0.2 source, `create_server_thread()` still unconditionally closed
`request_pipe[0]` immediately after passing that descriptor number to the
in-process server. On PS4 the server and ntdll share the descriptor table, so
the close invalidates the very pipe the server must read. Patch 0866 keeps it
open after successful handoff and closes it only when the server refused the
thread. This is the known functional fix for the observed first-request abort,
not another speculative change.

I replaced the colliding, diagnostic-only `0866-win32u-trace-ps4-rawinput-payload`
entry in the isolated v0.2 queue with the functional NTDLL ownership patch.
The full queue now contains 108 patches and applies cleanly in order to pinned
Wine commit `490f6d5dcbb2a5047345b8af88d114bbcaad69a8`. Rebuilt NTDLL and the
PS4 PRX set. The replacement stage was repackaged from the complete-chain
candidate; its only runtime payload difference is the two matching NTDLL PRX
copies (plus generated SFO/package metadata). The 0112, 0734-0738, 0742-0747,
0771, 0871 fixes and prior relocation, filesystem, input, close-path, ABI 4,
NLS, Vulkan/FreeType, and `cpu.o` payloads remain present.

#### 2026-10-30: server diagnostics isolate native signal-frame startup probe

The user installed and launched `Fallout NV v0.2.0 server diagnostics` at
11:47:05 local. It used the same native profile (`cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`).
Wineserver reached `server connected`; its new diagnostic at 11:47:07.415 was
`init_thread 0024 ok (reply_fd=34 wait_fd=36 errno=13)`. No `kill_thread`,
`kill_process`, or `protocol error` record preceded the crash, so the earlier
hypothesis of a malformed request or wineserver kill is not supported by this
run. GoldHEN identified `Fallout NV v0.2.0 server diagnostics`, crashed at
11:47:08.021 with reason `0x1f`, and announced
`/user/data/sce_coredumps/GLDS10085_1793386026/GLDS10085_1793386026.sorbisdmp`.
Klog at `/home/zpeirce/codex_scratch/gta4-ps4/V-run-20261029-klog.log` records
SIGUSR2/SIGTHR signal 31 at 11:47:07.419, while the Wine stack is in
`server_select` called from `NtOpenKeyEx` during initial startup. The matching
NTDLL ELF symbolizes the frames as `server_select` → `NtOpenKeyEx` →
`__wine_syscall_dispatcher`.

The source-level cause is now strongly localized to the native signal-frame
self-test, not the NLS/server pipe chain. `signal_init_process()` calls
`pw_native_frame_verified()`, which installs a temporary SIGUSR2 handler and
calls `pthread_kill(pthread_self(), SIGUSR2)` to verify the kernel signal frame.
The actual PS4 compiler headers for this build map `SIGUSR2` to 31; the klog
labels that signal `SIGUSR2/SIGTHR`. The probe therefore invokes the same
signal number the kernel reserves for its thread-signal path, which matches the
fatal signal immediately after `server_init_process_done`. This is based on the
code path, macro values, and matching crash timing; confirm with the next run.

selection remain unchanged. The standalone source patch is also at

**Fallout NV memory dialog traced to its Gamebryo preflight (2026-10-30):**
Disassembly of the exact FalloutNV.exe retained from run 1072 finds the dialog
at `.rdata` VA `0x010a2590`, with title `Memory Error` at `0x010a2580`. The
caller at `0x00aa3954` invokes `GlobalMemoryStatusEx` with a 0x40-byte
`MEMORYSTATUSEX`, compares `ullAvailPageFile` against `0x20000000` (512 MiB),
and calls `MessageBoxA` with this exact text if the value is lower. Therefore
this is Fallout's own conservative startup gate, not the PS4's application
manager reporting an out-of-memory kill.

The existing run log reports `flexible_free=258048K`, `dmem=55680K`
(`dmem_peak=64816K`), and zero tracked heap/direct-memory allocation failures
at the memory dialog. These establish that the runtime was not reporting a
failed Wine heap allocation. The exact `GlobalMemoryStatusEx` return values
were not logged, but the Wine implementation in patch 0850 makes the relevant
calculation explicit: `ullAvailPageFile` is the flexible free bytes plus the
largest single direct-memory block. Counting only one block is deliberately
conservative and can under-report allocatable memory when direct memory is
fragmented. The game's 512 MiB threshold and the existing log telemetry are
enough to target that generic reporting flaw; no more launch is needed just to
capture the API trace.

The proposed runtime fix is patch
`src/wine-patches-ps4/1014-ntdll-sum-free-ps4-direct-memory-ranges.patch`:
query disjoint 64 MiB ranges and sum their largest available blocks, without
overlap, before reporting commit headroom. This is a generic PS4 memory-report
correction, not a Fallout-only bypass. The temporary `[debug] winedebug`
profile edit was restored after the user declined another launch. Its backup,
diagnostic version, and verified restored readback are retained under
`/home/zpeirce/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-1075-memory-status-followup-20261030/`;
the restored console profile matches the original 436-byte file SHA-256
`48c4e55ad7b0e9e5b6960fd57eb019f3824dee4bffb22200674c28db5f96e3ad`.

### 2026-10-30: 1076 bounded history for the native not-present write

The 1072 native Continue fault at `FalloutNV.exe` EIP `0x00b49604` was a
not-present write to `0x0aaec000` (`err=0x6`). Both Wine and
`sceKernelVirtualQuery` reported that 16 KiB page as read/write. The existing
`capture_map_history_for_fault()` logged `result=kernel-allows` and returned
before dumping its map-operation ring. The 1072 fault therefore lacks the
mapping history needed to distinguish an allocation/remap race from a
misreported mapping. Flexible memory was about 2 MiB at this point and many
RX migrations were failing, but neither observation alone identifies the
fault's cause.

#### Package 1076 result: Continue crashes in a different guest path; migration pressure precedes it

1076 was installed and launched with native WoW64 confirmed (`wow64native initialized`). Fallout NV reached the 60 FPS menu; Continue then crashed. The run's filtered logs, exact executable, PRX/ELF, package manifest, and provenance-only encrypted dump are under `/home/zpeirce/codex_scratch/falloutnv-coredumps/run-1076-settled-map-history/`. The dump is retained but, per `AGENTS.md`, is not being treated as a decoder input.

The first guest exception is `FalloutNV.exe` EIP `0x00A6DF48` (RVA `0x0066DF48`), a read AV at `0x34`: `esi=0x34`. Exact instruction is `flds (%esi)`. Its stack return chain begins `0x00C796F7`, `0x00C74DF6`, `0x00931443`, `0x0056F8D4`. The caller at `0x00C796E0` loads an array entry from `[esi+0xa4]`, adds `0x34`, and passes that pointer to `0x00C74DD0`; the resulting argument is null-plus-0x34. This is a new first-fault site relative to 1071's `0x00A59FB5` null-vtable fault, although both are guest null-object failures during Continue.

Immediately before the first guest exception, the same-thread klog recorded reads at `0x32130660` and `0x32130550` from page `0x32130000-0x32134000` while that page was being repeatedly unmapped/remapped for executable-memory migration. The migration fault handler waited/retried those reads after the kernel query reported RW. The trace includes multiple `dmem-unmap`, `reserve`, `map-dmem`, and `mprotect` calls on that 16 KiB page. At the final telemetry sample before the crash, flexible free memory was 17 MiB; RX migration totals showed 2,175 attempts, 607 failures (529 map failures, 78 scratch failures), and 1,568 successes. These observations make migration churn and possible concurrent-data exposure a strong lead, but do not yet prove that it produced the null array element.

Thus 1076 did not answer the original settled-write question; it did provide a new first-exception signature and contemporaneous remap history. Do not repeat unchanged Continue. Before a behavior change, address the known handoff finding: `migrate_exec_range_to_flex()` snapshots a range, releases it, maps/copies it back without stopping concurrent guest writers; the source comment already documents that concurrent writes in this window can be silently reverted. Also examine whether the repeated failed migrations correspond to kernel VM-entry `EINVAL` (prior handoff measured this failure mode) and whether reducing mapping churn can preserve enough free flexible memory.

#### Package 1077: freeze guest writes during direct-memory RX migration

Implemented the guarded-snapshot correction in `src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` for both range and page migration. After publishing the migration range, the code now applies `PROT_READ` to the live direct-memory mapping and verifies via `sceKernelVirtualQuery` that the first and last addresses are readable and not writable. If protection or verification fails, it restores read/write and aborts migration before snapshotting. The existing signal-side migration wait makes concurrent guest writes wait until the page is migrated and the caller applies final RX protection.

This closes the known lost-write window before and during the snapshot. It does not eliminate the brief interval after a fresh RW mapping is installed but before its saved bytes are copied back; another thread's read could still observe transient contents there. A fully atomic replacement needs a prepopulated alias or thread-stop mechanism and remains future work. This is a targeted correction to a documented race; it is not yet proven to explain Fallout NV's observed null guest object.

write fault (`pferr & 7 == 7`), it queries the containing mapping, and only if

accesses. This tests whether the PS4 kernel's page translation is stale even

destination stride (`ECX=0x58`), with `EDX=0x29f` iterations. This is a game

faults, but is a new Fallout 3 instruction and allocation. It does not prove

#### 2026-10-31: Fallout 3 package 1080 advances past mapping fault, then hits null dispatch

The user launched Fallout 3 from the existing `fallout3-gog` profile on package 1080 and clicked Start. It crashed while loading. Exact GOG PE SHA-256 is `df9f0e1ec2c5413a9cef2db2dde6d926b99707d528049af4eb15538587d55f99`. The instructions first load an array entry from `[ESI+0x1f0]` using index `[ESI+0x298]`, test the entry itself for null, then read its first DWORD and use it as a dispatch pointer. At the fault, `ECX=0x1d041690` is the non-null entry and `EDX=0`, so `mov eax,[edx+0x50]` dereferences null. This is distinct from package 1079's `0x0087CE10` non-present read at `0x0550018C`; it is not a missing-page retry target.

Klog confirms a separate successful `nonpresent-read-retry build=1080` on readable page `0x05E1A000` earlier in the same run. The process later advanced to the distinct null-pointer fault. No dmem allocation failures were logged (about 41 MiB flexible memory remained shortly before the crash); a Vulkan present succeeded less than two seconds before the first fault. The later `winevulkan.so` access violation is teardown fallout after the guest exception, not the first fault. Do not make a callback-skip or game-binary patch from this evidence alone.

relocatable eboot release status was 0. No frame was presented. The prior
`wow64cpu!syscall_32to64_return` IRET failure at `0x7bf01211` with #GP selector
error `0x20` did not recur.

The first new Wine exception at 12:43:51.097 was `c0000005`, EIP
`0x7bf7dab7`, ESP `0x21fc28`, with read-access parameter `0xffffffffffffffff`.
Klog reports #GP/trap 9 with error 0 and selectors CS `0x33`, SS `0x3b`. It
then repeatedly faults at EIP `0x7bf66f40`, reaches a stack overflow at that
address, and emits a SIGSYS during teardown. The repeated faults and teardown
are secondary until shown otherwise.

The exact packaged 32-bit `ntdll.dll` was symbolized, not inferred from an
unmatched runtime. Its CodeView GUID matches the unstripped host-Wine build;
the rebuilt PE's `.text` is byte-identical to the staged package's `.text`.
Klog's executable mapping `0x7bf24000..0x7bf9c000` matches the PE `.text` at
RVA `0x1000`, giving mapped image base `0x7bf23000`:

- `0x7bf7dab7` maps to ntdll RVA `0x5aab7`, in `ioqueue_thread_proc`
  (`dlls/ntdll/threadpool.c`). It lands in the final two displacement bytes of
a `je` instruction beginning at RVA `0x5aab3`, rather than at an instruction
boundary. This makes the first context EIP suspect; it does not yet identify
what corrupted or supplied it.
- `0x7bf66f40` maps to RVA `0x43f40` in `TpSetPoolMinThreads`
  (`dlls/ntdll/threadpool.c:3052`). The captured stack also contains return
  addresses mapping to `perform_relocations` and `wine_dbg_vprintf`, but it
  does not provide a complete call chain.

#### MDK corrected WOW64 context logger and second follow-up package (2026-10-31)

The 13:02 run selected `mdk-windows` and logged `cpu=translator`, prefix
`mdk-windows`, and executable `C:\SHINY\MDK\MDKD3D.exe`. The first
`PW_WOW64_PRE_IRET` line had nonsensical `RIP=0` and `RSP=0x3b` because patch
1019 declared the saved WOW64 context as the host `CONTEXT` (AMD64), while
`BTCpuSimulate` passes `I386_CONTEXT`. This is a logger type/format bug; those
printed RIP/RSP values are not evidence that the saved context is zero.

The bad format nevertheless exposed the overlapping words at the I386

`0x7bf2e34c`, CS `0x33`, EFLAGS `0x10246`, with descending stack pointers.
These values are reconstructed from the overlapping `R8`/`R9` print fields and
need confirmation from the corrected typed logger.

The first guest exception was immediately afterward: read access to
`0xffffffffffffffff`, context EIP `0x7bf7dab7`, ESP `0x21fc28`. Using the exact
package-matched ntdll from run 12:43, runtime base `0x7bf23000` maps this to
RVA `0x5aab7`, file VA `0x7bc5aab7`, inside the immediate operand of
`_impl_from_TP_IO.part.0` (instruction begins at RVA `0x5aab6`), not an
instruction boundary. Subsequent recursive faults at `0x7bf66f40` and stack
overflow are secondary. The run later reported `frames_put=3`, `shown=1`, but
never established a gameplay frame; klog recorded the GLDS10044 application
crash/exit. Full bounded logs and this mapping are in
`/home/zpeirce/codex_scratch/mdk-diagnostic-20261031/translator-iret/run-20261031-1302/`.

#### Results from 1022 + `+seh` run (2026-10-31 15:30)

The 15:30:19 launch used profile `mdk-windows`, `cpu=translator`, and
`winedebug=+fps,+loaddll,+seh`. It crashed before presenting a frame. The full
session log and matching klog slice are retained at
`/home/zpeirce/codex_scratch/mdk-diagnostic-20261031/translator-iret/run-20261031-1530/`.

The first `PW_WOW64_PRE_IRET` record is:

```text
cpuflags=014c0001 ctxflags=0001003f eip=7bf33310 esp=0021fd10 cs=0023 ss=003b
targetcs=0023 targetss=003b eflags=00000202 eax=00474f82 ebx=7ffdd000 ecx=0
edx=0 esi=0 edi=0 ebp=0 fs=0 gs=003b
```

`cpuflags` decodes as `Machine=0x014c` (I386) and `Flags=1`
(`WOW64_CPURESERVED_FLAG_RESET_STATE`). Thus the hardcoded `CS=0x33, SS=0x3b`
RESET_STATE IRET branch in patch 1018 is taken; `targetcs=0x23` is only the
32-bit selector global and does not describe that branch's final IRET frame.
The first `+seh` record is an access violation reading
`0xffffffffffffffff` at guest EIP `0x7bf7dab7`, around 40 microseconds after
the first pre-IRET record. It maps to the `_impl_from_TP_IO` assertion-stub
bytes in the packaged 32-bit ntdll. Later AVs at `0x7bf66f40` are recursive
secondary faults. This run confirms the selector/reset-state hypothesis is
not sufficient to explain the crash; the key unresolved question is whether
the initial context was already bad when Wine stored it or was corrupted
afterward.
